# Complexity audit

DESIGN.md Part G, "Six standing rules", requires every algorithm to be of low complexity:
O(n log n) or better, O(n²) only with a recorded reason, nothing cubic or worse outside a
polynomial procedure with no known algorithm of lower degree, run only on the connected part
of its input a question mentions, and nothing cut short by a budget, a threshold or a count
of steps. This file lists every place an audit of
2026-10-30 found that breaks the rule, and the state of each. An item is closed when its
fix is in and a scaling test (size n against 8n, ratio at most 24 in instructions and, since
SCALE-bytes, in bytes allocated) passes, or when the reason it stays is recorded here.

Status: `open`, `fixed` (with the test that checks it), `kept` (with the reason).

## Store (`crates/zedc/src/store`)

| ID | Where | Class | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | `preimage`: every use of a `let`-bound name transfers the bound term again (`mentions`, `close`, `fwd`, `back_at`, `lin`, `formula`, `scan`) | 2^k in `let` depth | fixed: one result per (term, scope) (the old `TRANSFER_FUEL` went in replacement 5, S15); `l01_25_preimage_catalog::scaling` (k = 14: 296 ms → 0.37 ms) |
| S1b | `preimage`: `if` in guard position and equality of tests walk and copy the guard twice | 2^k in nesting | fixed: as S1, and the translation into the linear-relation domain is remembered per (term, scope) too (`Tx::lin`, `Tx::formula`, preimage.rs:1668 and 1816), with no budget since replacement 4 removed `VARIABLES` and `ATOMS`; `l01_25_preimage_catalog::scaling::the_transfer_scales` ("branches nested in guard position", k = 6 → 48) and `preimages_within_the_depth_are_exact` |
| S2 | `product::Scope::join` builds `Or(R∧A, R∧B)`, copying the common prefix | 2^k in connectives | fixed: `Scope::join` (product.rs:987) finds the conjuncts the two sides do not share by the difference of their persistent sets (`Conjuncts`), keeps the shared ones once, and puts in place of the rest one `Formula::either` (presburger.rs:512) of the two sides' remainders, which takes out any conjunct both still hold, with the equalities of two or more atoms both sides hold (Karr's join, `Scope::hull`); the cost follows the conjuncts the sides differ in, not the relation. No cap: replacement 4 removed `Formula::bounded` and its 64 constraints and 64 conjunctions, so a scope keeps every conjunct and each join of sides that differ adds one disjunction. Precision limit: a relation with disjunctions is decided by a greedy choice with one level of lookahead (`Refusal::Disjuncts` where both fail) and projected per disjunction (S16), not split into all its conjunctions. Tests in `l01_39_reduced_product::scaling`: `a_scope_keeps_every_clause_of_a_relation` (k = 1, 4, 40 clauses give 2k constraints), `narrowing_by_a_conjunction_of_disjunctions_scales` (k = 8 → 64; 953 → 8,917 problems solved, docs/thresholds.md 4), `a_join_keeps_the_equalities_of_its_sides_and_scales` (sides that fix k = 32 → 256 names). The first fix measured about 3 ms per clause, linear |
| S3 | `iterate::stabilize` restarts inner heads' counters on each outer round | 64^h in loop nesting | fixed: first by counting head changes over the phase; since replacement 3 removed `HEAD_ITERATIONS` nothing counts them, and a head stops at its landmarks. A component whose outside inputs did not change since it stabilized is skipped (`iterate::Walk`, its `dirty` flags; no value changes; the stack-machine oracle agrees); `m0_05_widening_and_iteration::scaling::nested_loops_cost_linear_work_in_the_depth` (at most 16 evaluations per loop, depths 1 to 48) and `the_nest_scales` (about 10 evaluations per loop at depths 1 to 48, 15 since replacement 3b, which stops a head at each of its landmarks; the old code does not finish depth 48 in 600 s; phase-wide counts without the skip: 4,799 evaluations at depth 48, ratio 49) |
| S4 | `revision::forward` and `IntervalSet::lift2` multiply piece counts with no cap; `cap` is O(n²) | P^k pieces | fixed, with no piece limit since replacement 3c (`PIECE_LIMIT` and `IntervalSet::lift2` removed): `revision::forward` (revision.rs:192) and the `*` case of `revision::backward` take their images through `revision::map2` (revision.rs:284). For operands of k ≥ m pieces, the one with fewer is capped to ⌈(k + m)/k⌉ ≤ 2 pieces and split at 0 again into at most 4 parts, the other into at most k + 2, so at most 4(k + 2) pairs are taken, O(k), and each sign region of the image is capped to the most pieces either operand has in one region, so a chain of operators never multiplies piece counts (S14); the images are sorted, so the work is O(k log k). `IntervalSet::cap` (interval.rs:276) merges the closest neighbors by a heap, O(n log n). Precision limit (the Complexity tradeoff comment at `map2`): merged pieces fill the gaps between them, so an image can hold values no pair of operand values gives; on 2,000 random pairs of 1 to 6 pieces its members are within 4% (`+`) and 8% (`*`) of exact (docs/thresholds.md 3c). Tests in `l01_24_refinement_lattices::scaling`: `cap_follows_the_merge_rule`, `join_and_contains_agree_with_membership`, `a_chain_of_operators_keeps_to_its_operands_pieces` (`+` over eight-piece operands, k = 200 → 1,600), `an_image_holds_every_result_and_keeps_to_the_most_pieces_of_a_region`, `an_image_with_a_one_piece_operand_is_the_join_of_the_pieces_images`, `the_interval_operations_scale` (cap, forward `*`, join and membership at 4,000 → 32,000 pieces). Before the first fix: old `cap` ratio 65; old chain 64 pieces after one `+` |
| S5 | Omega test: real and dark shadow both recursed with no step budget | 2^V per leaf | fixed, with no count of problems and no limit on variables since replacement 4 (`INTERMEDIATE` and `VARIABLES` removed): `Omega::run` (presburger.rs:2166) is one loop with no recursion into the shadows, which eliminates only while the rows hold no more terms than the input gave, exact eliminations first. At the first inexact elimination the real shadow is tested once (`relaxed_empty`, presburger.rs:1907, called at 2235: real-shadow eliminations while each adds no terms, then the box test); empty, it shows the problem empty, and from there the dark shadow is solved. When the dark shadow has no point, `search` (presburger.rs:1369) tries the splinters of each dark elimination one level deep, those of the side with fewer bounds, and stops at the first point or refusal. Cost: steps polynomial in the rows, not in the bit length of the coefficients (module comment); the cases still above n log n are in S18. Precision limit: two refusals, each a property of the system's shape and each keeping the written constraint: `Refusal::Shadows` when a splinter leaves a gray shadow of its own, and `Refusal::Growth` when every elimination left would add terms and the box test decides nothing. Tests in `l01_38_presburger::scaling`: `chains_of_inexact_eliminations_are_answered` (1 to 15 variables), `a_chain_of_inexact_eliminations_with_a_point_scales` (32 → 256 variables), `an_empty_chain_scales` (256 → 2,048), `a_rising_chain_scales` (128 → 1,024), `a_long_chain_with_a_point_scales` (4,096 → 32,768), `a_long_empty_chain_scales` and `a_long_rising_chain_scales` (2,048 → 16,384). The first fix measured 15 variables: 1.03 s → 2 ms |
| S6 | `preimage::Cx::constant` walks the subtree at every node | O(n·depth) | fixed: bottom up from the parts' remembered values |
| S7 | `presburger::project` per name: DNF built once per name, hull by 128 bisection steps | O(V·C·128) | fixed (`project_each`, galloping hull) |
| S8 | `product::walk`/`reduce`: whole scope cloned per `let`, constraints printed per round; premise sets copied whole at each narrowing | O(n²) | fixed: a binding changes the scope in place and restores it (`Scope::bind`/`unbind`), written constraints keep their free names, premise sets share their parts (`cell::Premises`, constant-time union); `l01_39_reduced_product::scaling` (`let` chain linear, 42.6 ms → 3.4 ms at 200; `if` nest about 2.1 per doubling past 64 levels, old code ratio 24.8 and 45 at `let`s). The in-place binding also fixed a soundness error: a shadowing `let` kept the outer name's facts (`a_binding_forgets_what_held_of_the_name_it_shadows`) |
| S11 | `preimage`: the call depth counted every term, so past 64 nested terms the memo was skipped and a `let` chain of 22 took 0.8 s before the visit budget stopped it (the CI failure of `the_transfer_scales`) | 2^k past a threshold | fixed: the depth counted calls only; since replacement 5 removed `CALL_DEPTH` no depth is counted, and a call stops only when its lambda is already being read (S12); `l01_25_preimage_catalog::scaling::a_long_chain_of_lets_is_not_cut` (exact for every length 1 to 64; 0.4 ms at 22, 19.6 ms at 384) |
| S12 | `preimage::fwd_at` cut at the depth without reporting a stop | correctness | fixed: the stop is reported. Since replacement 5 there is no call depth: a call of a lambda already on the stack of lambdas being read stops with `StopKind::Recursion` and is reported (`Tx::through`, preimage.rs:1062); `l01_25_preimage_catalog::stops::a_call_that_never_returns_stops_where_it_calls_itself` |
| S9 | `wto`: components revisited at each nesting level | O(d·(V+E)) | fixed: one depth-first traversal and the loop-nesting forest, each edge read once, O((V+E) log V); checked against the declarative order on 2×`RANDOM_INSTANCES` graphs of up to 40 vertices (two mutants caught); `m0_05_widening_and_iteration::scaling::the_order_of_nested_loops_takes_time_linear_in_the_depth` (depth 4,000: 79 ms → 4.8 ms; old ratio 64.3). Phase 1 on the same nests: 0.56 ms at 250 to 8.4 ms at 4,000, linear. `Element::vertices` copies each vertex once (was once per level) |
| S23 | `wto::ordered_by`, `wto::ranks`, `iterate::order` (OSP-order): the traversal visits vertices, and each vertex's successors, in the order of their keys (binding name, content address, canonical form), never by source position | O(n log n) comparisons of keys, each at most the key's length, plus O(E log E) to sort the successor lists | measured, n log n: one sort of the vertex numbers by key, n − 1 comparisons of neighbors to refuse two equal keys (`SameKey`), the graph renumbered by rank and traversed as in S9, the order mapped back with an explicit stack. A comparison reads the name, then the 32-byte address, and the form only when both are equal (a reference to the same form is equal at once), so the cost is O(n log n) times the key length, and the forms are read only among nodes of one binding with one address. `m0_05_widening_and_iteration::order::the_order_of_a_long_chain_scales` (20,000 → 160,000 scrambled names: 8.78 times the instructions), `the_order_of_many_small_loops_scales` (21,000 → 168,000 in loops of three: 8.62), `the_order_of_one_bindings_nodes_scales` (21,000 → 168,000, one name and one address, forms of eight items: 8.15). Negative controls, run once and not committed: a selection sort in `ranks` took 55 ms at 2,500 keys and 6.46 s at 20,000 (117 times; the sort 0.33 ms and 7.7 ms) and the scaling tests did not end in 300 s; `ranks` giving source position fails 7 tests (the six orders, the rotations, the rename, the refused equal keys, the address-and-form order, the widening point and the declarative order) |
| S10 | `affine::Bounds::add`, `IntervalSet::join` re-sorts, `contains` linear | O(k²), small k | fixed: `join` (one merge), `contains` (binary search) and `widen`'s endpoint checks with S4; `Bounds::add`, `normal`, `joined` and `leq` compare only bounds with the same terms, a range of the set (`same_terms`), O(k log k); `l01_24_refinement_lattices::scaling::bounds_on_many_symbols_scale` (old ratio 60.8: 8 ms at 200, 484 ms at 1,600) |
| S14 | `revision::map2` without the piece limit (replacement 3c) | O(k·m) pairs, k^n pieces along a chain | fixed: the operand with fewer pieces is capped to ⌈(k + m)/k⌉ ≤ 2 pieces, so after both are split at 0 at most 4(k + 2) pairs are taken, and each sign region of the image to the operands' largest region, so a chain never grows; measured at 64 → 1,024 pieces: 52 µs → 856 µs, linear (every pair: 0.6 ms → 155 ms). `l01_24_refinement_lattices::scaling::a_chain_of_operators_keeps_to_its_operands_pieces` and `the_interval_operations_scale` (`*` of two sets of n pieces); mutant without the region cap fails the chain test |
| S15 | `preimage` through calls without `CALL_DEPTH` and `TRANSFER_FUEL` (replacement 5): a chain of k functions each calling the one before twice | 2^k when each call is read in place | fixed: a call whose input-reading arguments are integers is summarized once per lambda, scope, landmark-snapped argument intervals and snapped demand; `l01_25_preimage_catalog::scaling::the_transfer_scales` (k = 6 → 48, arguments alike and apart, within the linear bound) and `nested_calls_are_summarized` (exact for k up to 48); the mutant with summaries off did not finish in 600 s. The linear-relation translation also read each call in place: fixed by remembering a call's translation by lambda, scope and the linear forms of its arguments (`the_linear_translation_of_calls_scales`, k = 6 → 48 with arguments alike and apart: k = 16 3.0 s → 12 ms, k = 64 55 ms; without the memo the test does not finish in 900 s). Still in place: calls with input-reading arguments that may fail or are not integers, or that have no linear form |
| S16 | Presburger relations without `VARIABLES`, `ATOMS`, `INTERMEDIATE`, `KEPT_CONJUNCTIONS` (replacement 4): a scope narrowed by k clauses, each a disjunction of two, and a nest of k tests | quadratic in k when every part of the relation is projected again in every round | fixed: a part is projected again only when the intervals or congruences of its names changed, and a join keeps the records both sides hold; `l01_39_reduced_product::scaling::narrowing_by_a_conjunction_of_disjunctions_scales` (problems solved 953 → 8917 for k = 8 → 64) and the nested-test scaling (0.72 s at k = 64, by merging constraints with the same terms); the mutant with every part projected each round took 64–74× for 8× k, past the 24× allowed |
| S13 | `iterate::phase1` with landmarks (replacement 3b): a head may stop at every landmark under its fixpoint | O(L) evaluations of a head per landmark set, L landmarks | measured, linear: 3L + 1 evaluations for L = 1 to 128 (`m0_05_widening_and_iteration::scaling::a_head_stops_at_most_once_per_landmark`). Fixed for the program's other literals: each cell takes the landmarks of its loop only (`iterate::Scopes`: those of every equation of its innermost component, nested ones included), so a head's evaluations do not grow with literals outside its loop: 5 for L = 1 to 128 literals beside a counter, against 3L + 1 when every literal was a landmark (`scaling::a_head_takes_only_the_landmarks_of_its_loop`; the mutant giving every cell all literals fails it, and one that leaves out inner loops' literals fails `iteration::a_loop_takes_the_landmarks_of_the_loops_inside_it_only`). Building the sets costs the sum over components of their landmarks, at most L times the nesting depth. A widening finds its target by two searches, O(log L), where it copied the set, O(L) |
| S17 | `preimage::witness` and `preimage::exact_preimage`, `@exact` (thresholds.md replacement 8a): branch and prune over the box of the open names, after Granger's class arithmetic at the root; a scope searches each group of constraints sharing names for one point and keeps it | fewer than 2P boxes for P points, each of the term's size; memory axes × log(range); a kept point still within its names' bounds costs one membership test per name | measured, linear in the points: `x * x mod 10 == 9` over `0..=n`, 8.0 ms at 1,024 to 79 ms at 8,192; `x * y mod 7 == 3` over `1..=n` squared, 49 ms at 64 to 731 ms at 256; `x * y <= n` over `1..=n` squared, 2.7 ms at 64 to 44 ms at 1,024, linear in n because exact sub-boxes are accepted whole (every point evaluated: 0.29 s to 74.8 s). `l01_25_preimage_catalog::scaling::the_exact_search_scales_with_the_number_of_points` |
| S18 | `presburger.rs` Omega test: recursed once per eliminated variable and built each step's rows anew; refusals from 128-bit coefficients; Bland's rule from the first pivot | cubic in the length of a chain; Bland's rule took 453 to 3,132 pivots on xv9's shifted sparse family, not an exponential number (SYNTH_R2:39) | fixed (T4): one loop over rows kept by coefficients with each variable's rows and bound counts; unbounded integers (`store::int`, no coefficient refusal); a system splits into parts that share no variable, each decided alone (two-variable parts exactly, `twovar`; unit two-variable rows by the zero-weight cycle parity test, `utvpi`, on integer potentials found by Bellman–Ford–Moore with subtree disassembly, worst case nodes times edges; since UTVPI-GOR by Goldberg and Radzik's search, same worst case, C24); the splinters of a dark elimination are those of its side with fewer bounds, and the first refused splinter is the answer; since XT-R there is no simplex (design R, below): `store/simplex.rs`, renamed `store/linear.rs`, holds no simplex, only the type `Term`, a coefficient at a variable (used by `presburger`, `twovar` and `utvpi`), and the rationals `Q` (used by `twovar` for the slopes, intercepts and crossing points of its lines). Tests in `l01_38_presburger::scaling`, n and 8n: the shifted sparse family 128 → 1,024 variables (refused, Growth: 45 ms → 455 ms; 4,096: 2.5 s), unit two-variable rows 256 → 2,048 (3.9 ms → 46 ms), an odd cycle 256 → 2,048 (4.2 ms → 47 ms), disjoint pairs of Pugh's example 256 → 2,048 (8 ms → 96 ms; refused before parts), and the checker's questions over 64 → 512 functions (`checker::the_linear_questions_of_a_unit_scale`). Since T4 repair 4 (load 4 to 19): random unit rows with a point, 256 → 2,048 variables and 4n rows (`random_rows_of_two_unit_coefficients_scale`; the probe at 1,024 variables and 4,096 rows went from 75 s with the UTVPI simplex to 0.06 s), and Pugh's example in a chain, 512 → 4,096 links (refused, Shadows: 18 ms → 230 ms; 3.5 s at 1,024 with every lower splinter searched). Negative controls, run once and not committed: the UTVPI simplex fails both random unit row tests by passing 60 s, and the old splinter search fails the chain test by passing 60 s. Not fixed: on rows with three or more terms and coefficients up to 5 whose integer point is tight (`tight` probe family, refused with Growth), `decide` takes 0.2 s at 32 variables, 0.7 s at 36 and 5.2 s at 48, with about 2,700 pivots in 16 simplex runs at 48: the time per pivot grows with the coefficients the dark-shadow eliminations build, and is spent in i128 `gcd` of the rationals. Since T4 repair 3 (measured at load 2 to 5): the splinters of a refused dark shadow are not searched, which removed a factor of about 13 (tight 48: 4.4 s to 0.34 s; `a_chain_into_a_refused_block_scales`, 64 → 512, catches the old search at 33.5 times the work). What is left is the simplex itself, which is not O(n log n) on these rows: at 64 variables one run takes 1,153 pivots on 99 rows with 19-digit entries (6.7 s), at 96 one takes 2,042 pivots with 30-digit entries (59 s); the tableau fills, and the entries pass i128, so each pivot costs 6 to 29 ms. Running the simplex on the input rows instead is worse (64: 383 pivots, 25 digits, 7.2 s for that run alone). Removing the general simplex (keeping only the UTVPI one) refuses far more: the oracle's 400 four-variable systems go from 229 sat, 21 unsat, 150 Growth to 163, 2, 235, and four step tests fail. No near-linear exact LP exists, so this needs a design decision or a cross-review: a structural criterion that refuses before the simplex, or acceptance of a polynomial simplex outside the O(n log n) rule. Measured in T4 repair 4 and left to the cross-review (the simplex and the Growth criterion are not changed in T4): the family is `l01_38_presburger::tight`, ignored (64 variables: 9.7 s at load 19); a fill rule (a pivot that leaves the tableau with more terms than the rows were given is refused, Growth) with violations kept in ordered sets brings it to 2 ms at 64 and 0.22 s at 4,096 and leaves the oracle's tally at 229, 21, 150; the patch is `/home/luke/Work/wt/xt-t4-simplex-fill-rule.patch`. **Design R (XT-R, 2026-11-03, from the xt round-2 synthesis):** the simplex and `Q::mul`, `lt` and `abs` are removed from `store/simplex.rs` (now `store/linear.rs`), which keeps `Term` and the rest of `Q`. In their place: box stages, O(T) in the terms of the rows (each variable's bounds tightened once per row from the original bounds; an empty box shows the rows empty; the point of the box nearest zero, checked against every row, is a model; else Growth); the column quotient, O(T log T) (proportional columns merged by a unimodular substitution, models lifted by the Bézout factors); and UTVPI potentials in topological order when the graph of unit rows is acyclic, O(V+E), with Bellman–Ford–Moore, O(V·E), left for the cyclic case (since UTVPI-GOR Goldberg and Radzik's search, C24). Scaling tests, 8n against n in instructions (limit 24): tight three-term rows 12.4, shifted sparse 12.3, block 9.6, coefficient bits 64 → 512 at 64 variables 8.1, two aggregates 14.3 (decided, Ok(true)), the acyclic hub through `utvpi::integer_empty` 8.0, three variables in many rows 9.0 (refused, Growth). The cyclic hub measured 42.5 and was not a test; since UTVPI-GOR it is (`stages::the_cyclic_hub_scales`, 7.9). Negative controls, run once and not committed: without the DAG path the acyclic hub fails at 52.6; without the quotient 3 tests fail (Growth on aggregates and mixed-sign proportional columns); with the box test reverted to Growth 4 tests fail and the 12,000-system corner oracle goes from 2,550 sat, 9,282 unsat, 168 Growth to 2,518, 4,393, 5,074. Option A (STATUS.md, L1.38): decisions only the linear program made are now refused; the two L1.38 asserts that counted them were changed, since they measured the LP's strength, not correctness. **BOX-5pt (XT3 B3, 2026-11-03):** the box stage tries five points of the box in turn, the point nearest zero, the lower corner, the upper corner and the midpoints rounded down and up, each taken variable by variable; each costs one pass over the terms, so the stage stays O(T) (five times one point). The oracle goes from 213 sat, 3 unsat, 184 Growth to 255, 3, 142, and the corner oracle from 2,550, 9,282, 168 to 2,586, 9,282, 132. Scaling test `stages::three_variables_in_many_rows_scale`, 64 → 512 rows: 9.0 times the work (1.88M → 16.9M instructions; every point misses, so all five are tried). Negative control, run once and not committed: recomputing the bounds once per row in `box_point` fails it at 50.6. Since BOX-5pt repair 1: in that family every point misses within the first two rows, so no pass over all rows is measured; `stages::a_box_point_held_by_many_rows_scales`, three variables in [-10, 10] and 256 → 2,048 rows with coefficients up to a million that hold at the upper corner, answers Ok(true) after a pass over every row: 9.0 times the work (8.86M → 79.3M instructions); negative control, run once and not committed: a row check that re-reads rows 0..=j at row j fails it at 44.5 (load 77). **HULL3 (XT3 B4, 2026-11-03):** with three live variables, one of which (`z`) has a unit coefficient in every row on one side, `store/hull3.rs` replaces `z`'s rows by the pairs of a lower and an upper bound that cut out the real projection on the other two, the edges where the top of the polyhedron meets its bottom: O(m) pairs in O(m log m) comparisons of `Int` (planes sorted by slope, then divide and conquer with Chan's kinetic merge in each node, ties broken by simulation of simplicity); with the unit side the integer projection is the real one, so `twovar` decides the rest exactly and `choose` lifts `z`. The dispatch on three live variables is a structural class like the one on two (`twovar`), not a size switch. `stages::three_variables_in_many_rows_scale`, 64 → 512 rows, is now Ok(true) with a model that holds: 12.1 times the work (89.2M → 1,082M instructions; 8 · log 512 / log 64 = 12); `hull3::the_shadow_scales`, tangent planes of two paraboloids, 128 → 1,024 pairs of planes: 13.1 (947M → 12,353M). Negative control, run once and not committed: all m² pairs (Fourier–Motzkin) fail the first at 68.2 (22.3M → 1,518M). The oracle goes from 255, 3, 142 to 261, 3, 136; the corner oracle from 2,586, 9,282, 132 to 2,611, 9,306, 83; a new oracle of 400 systems over three variables with a unit side has no refusal. Still refused: three live variables with no unit side, or four or more (three variables since covered by LIVE3; four or more stay refused). Open: cross-review xt round 3 (XR3) for the 18 rationally empty oracle systems still refused **LIVE3 (2026-11-03, author decision: a fixed-dimension linear program at three live variables only):** `store::live3` decides a part with three live variables completely: Megiddo's prune-and-search linear program in dimension three, O(m) in the rows with a constant of the dimension; a simplex of six such programs rounded with a fixed ratio; exact LLL on three vectors, giving Babai's point or a direction of width at most w(3), a constant of dimension three; at most w(3) planes, each two variables by a unimodular completion and `twovar::point`, O(m log m) each. Work O(m log m) in the rows. It runs at the box stage. **HULL3-subsumed (2026-11-05):** the `hull3` stage and `store/hull3.rs` are removed, so `live3` decides every part left with three live variables, with a unit side or not. Measured on `decide` (instructions; load 47 to 57): rows of `stages::three_rows` with coefficients up to a million, summed over 3 seeds, at 64 / 512 / 4,096 rows, 261M / 3,705M / 38,929M with the stage against 203M / 501M / 1,369M without it; the tangent lens of `live3::tangents` over (5000, 5000), 64 / 256 / 1,024 pairs, 355M / 2,198M / 11,712M with it against 941M / 4,092M / 7,163M without it. The stage was faster on small lenses (at most 3.5 times the time) and at worst 28 times the work, and grew 10 to 14 times per factor 8. Its tests moved to `l01_38_presburger::live3` with every case; `a_lens_of_tangent_planes_away_from_the_origin_scales`, 128 → 1,024 pairs: 4.8 times the instructions, 6.9 times the bytes; negative control, run once and not committed: all pairs of the bounds of `z` (Fourier–Motzkin) in place of the stage fail it at 53.2 times the bytes (load 45). `stages::three_variables_in_many_rows_scale` moved to 128 → 1,024 rows, since at 64 `live3` finds the point by a shorter path (6.7M instructions against 210M at 128): 1.9 times the instructions, 3.2 times the bytes. `live3::point` alone on the lens over the origin grows irregularly (595M, 1,461M, 42,807M, 32,633M instructions at 128 to 1,024 pairs; 41.6 times the bytes), in the rational arithmetic of its linear programs; `satisfiable` does not reach it there, since a box point holds that lens; ledger LIVE3-lp-tangents, test `a_lens_of_tangent_planes_scales_in_point` ignored until then. Tests in `l01_38_presburger::live3`, 8n against n in instructions, on Reeve's tetrahedron of height 7 shrunk (no integer point) with m rows that each hold the whole body with slack 0 to 5, so every stage runs (22 planes): rows 256 → 2,048 with coefficients up to 50, 8.18 times (1.92e9 → 1.57e10, exponent 1.01, limit 24); coefficient bits 64 → 512 at 8 rows, 12.81 times (1.61e9 → 2.07e10, exponent 1.23, power 1, limit 24), both sizes past the i128 path of `Int`; schoolbook products are B² but at these sizes the sums and products by small numbers cost more. The same body under a shear with entries of B/2 bits, 64 → 512: 10.28 times (probe, not committed). Negative controls, run once and not committed: an all-pairs `Lin2::minus` pass in each round of `solve2` fails the rows test at 33.1; an extra factor of B in `Q::mul` (a product per bit of the numerator) fails the bits test on bytes, 35.0 times (instructions 21.7 times); an all-pairs pass of additions over the rows of each plane stays under the limit (10.2), since it is small next to `twovar`. Growth and Shadows now need four live variables or more; the 18 rationally empty systems are decided where they have three live variables (the small-coefficient oracle 261, 3, 136 → 274, 4, 122, the corner oracle 2,611, 9,306, 83 → 2,631, 9,331, 38, on top of HULL3). Since LIVE3-slices, `slices` builds each row of the planes once and moves its constant by an addition per slice (0.2% fewer instructions and 1.2% fewer bytes on the rows test; order unchanged). Since LIVE3-tests: `live3::slabs_of_million_coefficients_scale`, the parity slab c·x = 1 with c = (1,000,001, 1,000,003, 1,000,005) in the cube [-5, 5]^3 (no integer point, a real one) with m extra rows each holding the cube with slack 0 to 5, 512 → 4,096 rows, every stage runs with flat direction c and one plane: 7.9 times the instructions (5.00e9 → 3.94e10) and 8.4 times the bytes (200.4M → 1,685.2M), 20 s, load 26 to 44. The test starts at 512 rows, since at 256 → 2,048 one all-pairs `Lin2::minus` pass in each round of `solve2` gave 22.9, under the limit of 24. Negative controls, run once and not committed, at 512 → 4,096: that pass fails it at 31.2 (8.80e9 → 2.74e11, LIVE3-tests verify 1), and a pass over all ordered pairs at 40.2 (1.26e10 → 5.07e11, load 17 to 38). Since SLABS-count: the test runs at 192 → 1,536 rows and first holds the calls of `Lin2::minus` (counted by feature `counts` of `zedc`, on only in the test build) within 9 times, which does not depend on the load: 4,645 → 38,315 calls, 8.25 times; 7.49 times the instructions (1.96e9 → 1.47e10) and 8.86 times the bytes (70.3M → 622.5M); 13.2 s at load 51 to 55, against 25.3 s at 512 → 4,096 and load 45 to 49. Negative control, run once and not committed: the all-pairs `Lin2::minus` pass in each round of `solve2` fails the count at 60.5 times (260,568 → 15,775,080), while its instructions, 19.3 times (2.51e9 → 4.84e10), stay under 24 (load 58 to 62) |
| Q1-ROWBOX | `presburger.rs` box stage, `box_empty_in` and `row_box_empty`: a test that two rows bound a variable past each other must not compare every pair of rows, nor every row with every other row of a shared variable (a hub) | O(T) in the terms of the wide rows | fixed (cross-review XR3 Q1, 2026-11-03): one pass over the rows; each row is read twice (its largest value over the box, then the rational bound it gives each variable with the other terms at their largest) and each variable keeps the greatest lower and least upper bound seen so far, so a crossing is found at the later of its two rows whatever the order. Exact: `Q` with unbounded `Int`, no rounding. Tests in `l01_38_presburger::scaling`, n and 8n in instructions (limit 24): HUB-SCALE `the_box_stage_with_a_hub_in_one_row_per_block_scales`, Gemini's relaxed blocks with a hub in one row of each block, refused (Growth) after a pass over every row, 64 → 512 blocks (9.3; 9.1 from 16 to 128); `the_row_test_of_the_box_scales`, the same with a crossing block, Ok(false), 64 → 512 (9.2; 8.7). Negative controls, run once and not committed: `row_box_empty` run again over every row of each variable of a row fails both (175.2 and 209.5 at 64; 44.6 and 46.5 at 16); the bounds rebuilt for each row from the rows before it fail the hub family (49.6 at 64, 32.8 at 16) and not the crossing family (21.3), where the crossing is found after about a third of the rows. Precision limit: a system with no rational point that no single pair of rows shows over the tightened box is refused; the resolvent stage that finds more was not adopted (XR3 Q1) |
| S19 | `preimage`, the transfer through summaries: each summary had its own memo, so `mentions` and `fwd` walked all the terms below it again; a box held every enclosing parameter and was copied at each meet and union; a scope was a chain walked by name. Found when `eval` lost its thread stack and the tests at 48 and 384 functions, each calling the one before twice, failed at a ratio of 25.8 against a limit of 24 | about n^1.8 in the functions: 42 ms at 48 and 727 ms at 384 | fixed: one memo per transfer, sound since (term, scope) fixes the value and a parameter is read through a scope made once per reading of a call; a scope is an `imbl::HashMap`; a box is an `imbl::OrdMap` of the names it narrows and meets and unions take the time of the smaller box. 5.9, 68 and 645 ms at 48, 384 and 3072, about n^1.2. Tests: `long_*` in `tests/steps/l01_25_preimage_catalog/scaling.rs`, at 48 and 384. Negative control: on the old transfer `long_calls_apart`, `long_calls_alike` and `long_tests_of_many_inputs` fail. Not fixed here: `i != a && i != b && …` is above n log n (each test meets a set of as many pieces as there are tests before it; ratio 38 from 384 to 3072 on the new code, 37 on the old); the linear stage on sums and nested `max` is S18, T4 |
| S20 | `preimage::lin` named each `if`, `min`, `max` and `abs` auxiliary, and each `div` and `mod` pair, by printing its whole subterm | O(n·depth) for nested `if`; 2^depth on a shared term | fixed (T11-preimage): the name shows one level, its parts by their linear forms and its guard by its formula, in which an earlier auxiliary is its name `q<i>`, so a name costs what its definition already costs. Tests in `l01_25_preimage_catalog::scaling`: `relations` on `if`s nested in arms (`long_nested_values`) and on `if`s whose arms read one shared term (`long_shared_values`), at 256 and 2,048, and the preimage of the first; `auxiliary_variables_are_named_one_level_deep`. Negative control, the old names, run once and not committed: nested 431M and 26.9G instructions, 62.4 times (limit 24); shared killed at 60 s (verify: 19 ms, 383 ms, 1.64 s at depth 12, 16, 18) |
| S22 | `preimage`, the backward transfer through a term shared by both arms of `if`s, one arm adding a constant (`tj = if (i >= j) { t } else { t + 1 }`) | O(k²): the shared term below level `j` is asked for `t >= -d` for each `d` up to `k - j`, and each term and demand is transferred once | fixed for one input whose domain has no finite end past `i128` (S22a, after cross-review xs22 round 1; ends past `i64` since S22-WIDE, below): a term of one input built from `if`, `+ c`, `- c`, `0 - x`, `c * x` and negation is summarized once as a persistent treap of affine pieces, and a demand is inverted by descending only into subtrees whose values meet it; each integer demand is first met with the hull of the term's values. `long_shared_values_preimage` checks its answers and now takes 7.4 times at 256 and 2,048 (was 58.7). `l01_25_preimage_catalog::s22` checks the families probe 7.41, live 10.11, alt 7.55, `c_j = j` 7.46, `== 0` 7.41, range 7.45, gt 7.41, swapped 7.41, constfirst 7.43, sub 7.41, twoin 7.41, conj 7.38, conj_live 10.29, cross 8.21, `abs(i) >= j` 7.38, `i != j` 7.02, `2^j` 7.48, j−t 8.47 and min 8.36 (instructions, load about 38 to 52), and the probe with `i` over all integers 11.75 and over `[-100, inf)` 11.73 (instructions, load about 10), and compares the whole `Preimage` with the transfer before (`store::preimage_ref`, test only) on 22,628 cases: 0 differ, except one in the random guarded chains, where both boxes are inexact and the new one is a sound subset (the hull narrows a result the `map2` piece cap widened; its own test, `meeting_a_demand_with_the_hull_may_narrow_a_box_the_map2_cap_widened`). Since S22b the comparison also accepts the cases of a defect the transfer before shares: it inverted `c - x` as the forward image of `{c} - z`, whose `map2` cap merges the pieces it moves across zero, and kept the merged box exact (`abs(12 - x) >= 5` gave every integer, exact). `c - x` is now inverted as `c - z`, each piece reflected and moved by `c` in one step in `i128` (`reflect`, `preimage.rs`), O(p log p) for p pieces (test `l01_25_preimage_catalog::constant_minus::a_comb_of_pieces_scales`: 8.91 times at 4,096 and 32,768 pieces, instructions, load 74; a reflection that joins one piece at a time took 62.6 times). The comparison finds 16 such boxes, each narrower and checked by the interpreter (2 corner cases, 6 and 7 random guarded chains, 1 random term), and one more inexact box made narrower in the random guarded chains; the counts are held by the tests. Since S22b-rest the summary also reads `c - u` for every constant `c` (as the affine map `-u + c`; before only `c = 0`), and the clip reads `c - x` by `reflect` too: the family j−t over a domain with no finite end (`chain_jt_range_live_scales`) took 66 ms, 0.29, 3.36 and 8.64 s at k = 64, 128, 256 and 512 when `c - u` fell to the transfer before, now 1.1, 2.4, 8.8 and 21 ms, and 10.6 times the instructions at 256 and 2,048 (63.4 times on main 4a2686f; `5 - t` live 10.6, j−t narrow 7.5; load about 85 to 111). On one interval the forward image the clip used gave the same set as `reflect` in all 2,925 cases tried, but raised the rounding flag in 16 (`c = i64::MIN` with the demand unbounded above, `c = i64::MAX` unbounded below), so `MIN - x` with `x >= 0` gave up and listed the box (`constant_minus::the_clip_of_a_reflection_at_the_ends_of_i64`). The comparison counts are unchanged. Since PA-affine-i128 the summary keeps its coefficients and constants in `i128` and gives up an affine step only where one leaves `i128` (each step still O(1)); `pa_if` and `invert_piece` gave it up where a value it is read at left `i64` (until S22-WIDE, below). So `i64::MIN - (u + 3)`, `i64::MAX - (u - 3)`, `-(u + c)` and `2 * u + c` at both ends of `i64` are summarized, checked against enumeration through the test hook `store::summary_preimage` (`l01_25_preimage_catalog::wide_constants`). Until S22-WIDE `preimage` itself took the transfer before on these terms: `Tx::fits` measured the literals by their values. `Tx::reach` summed the magnitudes of the literals, so a constant built as a product (`-2147483648 * 4294967296`, which is `i64::MIN`) passed `Tx::fits`, and the summary read the `i64` end that stands for an infinite domain end as an input: `if (u <= -3) { ((-2147483648 * 4294967296) + 5) - u } else { u }` over all `u` with demand `[0, 10]` gave `u` in `(-inf, MIN + 5] ∪ [0, 10]`, marked exact, though `u = MIN - 100` gives 105. `reach` now also counts each constant part by the magnitude of its value (one cached `constant` call per primitive, linear), and the answer is `[MIN - 5, MIN + 5] ∪ [0, 10]`, exact (`a_product_of_literals_near_an_end_of_i64_is_measured_by_its_value`). The clip also reads `min` and `max` (their hull is the operator on the operands' ends): min took 0.20 s at 256 and 20.8 s at 2,048, now 0.099 and 0.903 s. Cross below the domain's width was exponential because `enumerate` (`preimage.rs:3421`) called `stage::ends`, which walks the term as a tree (`2^k` steps on a chain whose arms share their term), before the O(1) test that refuses a box of two ranges; the box is now read first (cross at k = 22: 1.01 s, now 0.06 s; `a_chain_on_two_inputs_below_its_domains_width_ends` at k = 40 runs in 0.21 s, and with the old order it is killed at 60 s). An empty box got the same walk (S22d): the `3 * t - 1` chain on `i` in `[-24, -21]` and `[-15, -4]`, whose guards all fail, leaves `i64` at `i = -24` from k = 37, so the transfer rounds and calls its empty box inexact, and `enumerate` asked `stage::ends` before it saw the box was empty: 0.66 ms at k = 36, killed at 30 s at k = 37 (main, the transfer before too). `enumerate` now reads an empty box as exact first (it holds no point to run, so whether the term ends does not matter): 0.86 ms at k = 37, 30 ms at 256; `chain_t3_gaps_scales` and `chain_t3_gaps_five_no_scales` take 8.15 and 7.84 times the instructions at 256 and 2,048 (load about 50; the first is killed at 60 s on main), and the comparison with the transfer before shows 0 differences up to k = 36 (from 37 the transfer before takes `2^k` steps). Open, pending xs22 round 2: inputs that share terms on wide domains. Exact preimages there are NP-hard by a reduction from SUBSET SUM (xs22 SYNTH_R1 section 2). The backward transfer of cross_live and cross is fast (cross_live: 13 ms at k = 64, 36 ms at 128); their cost was in `linear` (`preimage.rs:319`), the Presburger relation of the inexact box, and within it in `presburger::pick` (LIN-cross): when the point found so far fails a disjunction, `local_repair` solved the disjunct's variables again with every constraint taken so far that mentions them, and the `k` guards `i >= j` all mention `i`, so each of the `2k` disjunctions read up to `k` constraints (134,152 in all at k = 256, 8,968 at 64). `Near` now keeps the constraints of one variable as that variable's least and greatest value and indexes only those of two or more variables, so a repair reads the constraints linking its variables to others and two bounds each. cross_live now takes 0.70, 1.74, 4.57 and 12.7 G instructions at k = 32, 64, 128 and 256 (`chain_cross_live_scales`, 32 to 256: 18.2 times; the old `pick`: 39.4 times, fails); what is left is the bisection of `project_each`, about log w questions of O(k log k) each. Also `l01_38_presburger::repair::a_chain_of_guards_on_one_input_scales` (64 to 512; the old `pick`: 34.0 times, fails) and `chain_cross_within_its_width_scales` (6 to 48). After the merge of ST-arms-quad (fa57422) both failed again: `presburger::settle` now joins a disjunction left with one disjunct to the base within the round (ST-arms-quad repair 2, 873f1a8), and on the chain the base becomes one group as long as the chain, which each later disjunct was solved with (k = 256: 1022 solves of 261 constraints in one call; cross 6 to 48 26.2 times the instructions, cross_live 32 to 256 48.6 times the bytes). `settle` (CATCHUP-cross) keeps its point a point of the growing base and first asks `local_repair` whether the disjunct has a point near it and `local_refute` whether its constraints, its atoms' bounds and the base's constraints over its atoms alone have none; each answer is exact, and only when both fail are the groups solved. Now cross_live 12.9 times the instructions and 11.5 the bytes, cross within its width 8.8 times, cross 256 to 2,048 8.2 (load about 63 to 76); with `settle` as merged, 26.3 and 48.6 times (negative control, run once). Cross 12 to 96, above the inputs' width (LIN-probe's ignored `chain_cross_above_its_width_scales`), fails on the bytes at 43.8 times with this (97 MB to 4.26 GB) and at 74.4 times without it (89 MB to 6.6 GB, main 79ad9e6); what is left there is `probe`, ledger LIN-probe-refute. `chain_cross_dead_scales` (8 to 64), which fails on main 79ad9e6 at 41.2 times the instructions, passes with this, and so does l01_38 `repair::a_chain_whose_point_is_at_an_end_scales` (32 to 256), which fails on main 7e296bf at 29.9 times the bytes; LIN-probe added both (01e79bd) on a main that already had 873f1a8. The whole `Preimage`, relation included, is equal before and after on 375 cases of all 39 families. Guards above the inputs' width, which no input meets (cross from k = 51 to 99, and `cross_dead`, whose inputs lie in `[2-k, k-2]`), fixed in LIN-probe: the only point outside the demand is at an end of both inputs, the greedy `pick` keeps `i` where the first solution put it and fails, and `probe` (failed-literal probing) tested each of the `2k` disjuncts with a full propagation, about k^2.4 (0.26, 1.40, 7.2 and 39.4 G instructions at k = 10, 18, 34 and 66; cross 1.09 G at k = 48 and 22.9 G at 52; this was the jump between k = 48 and 56 that LIN-cross reported, and no size switch: from k = 100 the transfer's box is exact and `linear` is not called). `probe` now keeps without its test each disjunct that a point found so far holds: removing a disjunct that meets the rest nowhere leaves the formula's points as they were, and propagation removes a disjunct only on a proof that it has no point with the rest, so the result is the one the tests give. The points come from `pick` on a test's parts with the disjuncts no point holds yet tried first, and each is checked against every part (`point_with`). `cross_dead` takes 0.177, 0.434, 1.05, 2.57, 6.63 and 18.18 G instructions at k = 8, 16, 32, 64, 128 and 256 (`chain_cross_dead_scales`, 8 to 64: 14.5 times; the old `probe`: 158 times, fails; 32 to 256: 17.3 times, the ratio per doubling rising 2.45, 2.58, 2.75), 2.88 G at k = 66. `probe` makes 9 calls of `settle` and 11 of `point_with` per call at every k from 16 to 256 (verifier's counters), so the growth left is outside `probe`; cross_live, which never calls `probe`, grows the same way (0.68 to 11.11 G from 32 to 256, 2.44 to 2.63 per doubling). That it is the bisection of `project_each` is inferred, not measured. `l01_38_presburger::repair::a_chain_whose_point_is_at_an_end_scales` (32 to 256) holds the same for `decide`. Checked identical: a test build that ran the old and the new `probe` on every call and compared their results found 0 differences in 5,047 calls from `l01_25` and about 5,500 on random formulas of 4 variables. A disjunct that no point holds was still tested by one propagation of the whole formula, so `r` disjuncts that propagation removes cost `r` propagations; on cross from k = 51 to 99, `r` is about k - 50, and each settle of the whole formula costs about k, so about k (k - 50) in all (9, 89 and 185 settles per `probe` call and 2.0, 15.1 and 52.8 G instructions at k = 52, 72 and 96, against 1.05 G at 48). Fixed in LIN-probe-refute: at k = 96 the formula implies `i >= 47`, and the disjuncts without a point are the bounds `i <= j - 1` below it. When propagation removes a disjunct, `presburger::learn` tests each bound of one atom it writes, `x <= v`, for weaker values `w` taken from the bounds the other disjuncts write, by an exponential and then a binary search, and records `x >= w + 1` for the greatest `w` the formula has no point with; each later disjunct that breaks a recorded bound is removed without a propagation, and the bounds are added to the parts `pick` searches. A bound is tested first by `bounds_refute`, which propagates bounds of single atoms through the constraints and through the hulls of the disjunctions in two readings, in reverse breadth-first order over the parts that share atoms (leaving out `x`) and then in that order, so a chain of definitions is read from its far end to its start and back, in `O(n log n)`, and then by `settle`. Trail-based undo was measured out first: each refutation on a chain needs every level below decided, so its own propagation is O(k) and the total stays O(k²); `learn` with `settle` alone gained 3 levels per search (36.6 times the bytes), since `settle` does not carry bounds through open disjunctions. `chain_cross_above_its_width_scales` (12 to 96, no longer ignored) passes at 10.1 times the bytes (99.5 M to 1.00 G; 3.98 G and 40.0 times with one propagation per refuted disjunct, the negative control) and 12.3 times the instructions, and l01_38 `probe::guards_beyond_the_inputs_scale` (k guards on inputs 3k/4 wide, symbols whose names do not sort in chain order, 16 to 128) at 11.0 times (41.7 times with one propagation each). cross_live 11.4, cross within its width 8.1, cross_dead 12.5 and cross 7.2 times the bytes, unchanged. The whole `Preimage` is equal with and without `learn` on 2,851 cases of all families (k up to 96 for the cross families, three random domains each). Still polynomial (`// Complexity tradeoff:` at `probe` and at `bounds_refute`): disjuncts without a point that share no bound of one atom are each tested by one propagation, and `learn` adds, for each bound of one atom in a refuted disjunct, an exponential and a binary search over the `m` bounds the other disjuncts write on that atom: up to about `2 log2 m + 1` tests, each one propagation of bounds (`bounds_refute`) and, when that does not refute, one `settle` (the search usually ends on a bound that has a point, which always costs a `settle`), so up to `O(r log m)` settles for `r` refuted disjuncts when no learned bound removes another, against `r` before (verifier's counters: `guards_beyond_the_inputs_scale` 5 calls of `learn` and 16 settles in them, cross 2 and 2); bounds are not propagated until none moves, which can take rounds that grow with the bounds on a cycle. `stage::ends` (`stage.rs:165`) still walks a shared term as a tree in `run_closed`, `exact_preimage`, `witness` and the build stage (ledger ENDS-dag). A summary keeps its values in `i128` and checks that the values each `if` arm keeps lie in `i64`, so `t + 1` over all integers, which leaves `i64` only at `i64::MAX` where the guard keeps `t`, is summarized: the probe over all integers took 0.23, 0.82, 3.9 and 25.0 s at k = 256, 512, 1,024 and 2,048 when every value had to lie in `i64` (repair 1 of S22a, verifier's probe: 121 times from 256 to 2,048), now 10, 20, 36 and 92 ms; over `[-100, inf)` 78 to 889 ms, now 4.4 to 69 ms (debug build, load about 17 to 33). An arm that kept a value outside `i64` was not summarized until S22-WIDE (below); now one that keeps a value outside `i128` is not. S22c: a comparison with a constant at an end of `i64`, read back through a sum that passes it (`y + (MAX - 3) == MAX` false over `y` in `[-10, 10]`), returned `[-10, 2]` as exact, missing `4..=10`: `complement_in` (`revision.rs`) dropped the integers past `i64::MAX`, so `!= MAX` was read as `<= MAX - 1`. The gap is now kept, rounded outward, when the set it is taken in holds it; the result is `[-10..=2, 4..=10]`, exact by the linear relation. Linear in the pieces: 7.6 times at 1,024 and 8,192 pieces, and the preimage of `y + 1 + … + 1 != MAX` 7.0 times at 64 and 512 ones (`l01_25_preimage_catalog::overflow`; a quadratic complement, run once, 61.4 times). Fixed by S22-WIDE: a domain with a finite end near an end of `i64`. `Tx::fits` turned the summary and the clip off when the widest finite end of a domain plus the widest of the demand passed `i64::MAX`, since the summary held its values in `i64` and read `i64::MIN` and `i64::MAX` as the infinite ends of a domain; the clipped demand of the probe ends near the domain's end, so a domain end beyond about 2^62 was enough, and the transfer before then cost about k^2.8 (the probe over `[-100, 2^62]` 0.154, 0.477, 1.16, 6.7 and 30.3 s at k = 128 to 2,048 on main 43742125, load 40 to 49; over `[-100, i64::MAX]` 19.5 s at k = 128). The summary now holds its values in `i128` with the infinities as the sentinels `i128::MIN` and `i128::MAX`, the limit each piece's line tends to at an infinite end (`value_at`, `map_vals`), inverts a piece exactly at an infinite end of the demand (`invert_piece`), and reads every domain, arm set and demand whose finite ends lie strictly inside `i128` (`in_range`); `Tx::fits`, `Tx::reach` and `Cx::widest` are removed, and the clip is always applied. The probe over `[-100, 2^62]` now takes 5, 7, 17, 37 and 90 ms at k = 128 to 2,048, and over `[-100, i64::MAX]` 6, 13, 30, 81 and 191 ms at k = 256 to 4,096 (load 22 to 36); `chain_probe_wide_scales` (no longer ignored) and `chain_probe_max_scales` pass; with the `fits` refusal put back (negative control, not committed) they allocate 39.4 and 39.5 times the bytes from 256 to 2,048 (limit 24). The 551 of 5,709 corner cases that differed when `fits` alone was dropped came from the `i64` stand-ins (an infinite end read as an input at `i64::MIN`, and the `enumerated` flag on empty preimages near the ends of `i64`); with exact infinities `ends_of_i64_and_other_corners_keep_their_preimages` passes unchanged: no case differs but the two S22b reflections it already counted. `wide_ends::chains_over_wide_domains_keep_their_preimages` compares 6,930 chains and reflections over domains with ends near 0, the ends of `i64`, 2^62, 2^70 and unbounded with the transfer before and the interpreter: 25 differ, all `MAX - i` under a demand of two pieces on both sides of zero, where the transfer before kept the merged box of `{c} - z` exact and the interpreter refutes it (the S22b defect above); the new boxes are sound and exact. Past `i128` the summary is still given up (`finite`, `in_range`, each with a `// Complexity tradeoff:` comment; `values_past_i128_take_the_transfer_before`, `ends_past_i128_take_the_transfer_before`), and the `if` is read arm by arm. The `mod7` family: row S22e. |
| S22e | `preimage`, the `mod7` chain of S22 (`tj = if (i mod 7 == j mod 7) { t } else { t + 1 }`, `i` in `[-50, 50]`): the guard's preimage spans more than two periods of 7, so `mod_pre` is not exact (C17), the `Pa` summary refuses the `if`, and the inexact box goes to `linear`, whose Presburger relation holds `k` disjunctions | about k^2.5 with a large constant, not exponential (the item was filed as exponential): 1.4 to 2.3 s at k = 8, 3.75 to 5.0 s at 16, 24.4 s at 32, over 60 s at 48 (main ea2e48f, load 40 to 60); the answer is inexact from k = 11 (`[-10, 50]` where `[-9, 50]` is exact) and refused (Shadows, then Disjuncts) | fixed in O(G n log n): `split_residues` (`preimage.rs`) runs before `linear` when the transfer is inexact and the terms compare `x mod m` with constants for one input and one modulus. The constants cut `[0, m)` into `G <= 2d + 1` classes for `d` distinct constants; in each class every such comparison is true throughout or false throughout, so `back_arith` reads `x mod m` exactly there and the summary takes the chain. The preimage is the union over the classes; the other inputs must agree, and a stretch where some classes keep points and others do not may span at most two periods. `chain_mod7_scales` passes at 256 and 2,048 (0.20 to 0.35 s and 2.4 to 3.3 s, load 40 to 77); negative control with the split off: 174.5 times the instructions from 2 to 16 (limit 24). `a_chain_of_guards_on_remainders_is_read_class_by_class` (k = 11, `[-9, 50]` exact) fails on main. The comparison with `store::preimage_ref` changes 100 cases, each checked against the interpreter (`Diffs` counts them as found exact): 7 `mod7` chains (k = 2 to 8, same box, refusal Shadows now none) and 93 random guarded chains with `i mod 3` guards (inexact, refused or listed before; now exact, inside the old box and equal to it where it was exact). Kept, recorded: `d` growing with the term costs `O(d n log n)`; `remainders_many_classes_cost_one_transfer_each` (`i mod 1024 == j`, `j` up to `k`, `i` in `[-5000, 5000]`) takes 4.5 ms, 17 ms, 63 ms, 0.31 s and 0.91 s at k = 8 to 128 (about k^1.9), against 3.0 s and 20.7 s at k = 8 and 16 with the split off. A complete solution would hold the congruence facet in the backward transfer's sets, so that one transfer carries every class. |
| S22e-quad | `preimage`, the split by remainders of S22e on a chain of `k` guards `x mod m == c` with `k` distinct constants (`many_remainders`: `tj = if (i mod 1024 == j) { t } else { t + 1 }`, `i` in `[-5000, 5000]`): each of the `2k + 1` classes ran the backward transfer over the whole chain, and the first transfer before the split read the chain unsplit, where the guards are inexact (C17) and the demand shifts at each level | O(k²): 58.0 times the instructions from 32 to 256 (200,279,152 against 11,615,272,274; limit 24) | fixed in O(k log k) for chains whose arms `Tx::by_class` reads: what a class's run computes without reading its class is kept for the next class (`Kept`), and an `if` on the remainder is read from one summary over every class (`Tx::by_class`, a persistent segment tree over the class indices holding a `Pa` per range, with a lazy affine tag; the yes arm is spliced into the ranges where the guard holds, `O(log n)` each), checked against `i64` on the bounds of all classes at once so that each class's summary equals its own run's. When the split over the whole box is exact it is the preimage, and the first transfer is not run; that split is given up after at most one class, and the transfer runs first as before, when the box holds `x` within two periods of `m` (the transfer reads every guard on the remainder exactly) or when a class's run reads a guard on the remainder itself, not from `Tx::by_class` (`min` arms, boolean arms). Run first in those cases (repair 1, verifier's measurement), the split cost one transfer per class where the transfer alone was linear: from 8 to 64 a `min` chain over `[0, 1000]` 45.3 times the instructions (300.7M at 64 against 15.1M on main), from 16 to 128 a chain of `i mod 1024 != j` 45.7 times (603.2M against 14.6M), and from 16 to 128 `k` tests `i >= 7j` above `k` remainder guards 64.9 times (1,361.6M against 50.3M); now 15.3M, 14.8M and 50.5M at the larger size, as on main (`remainders_read_by_each_class_keep_the_transfer_first_scales`, which fails at 44.9, 53.3 and 64.9 times with the split always first). `many_remainders_scales` passes at 32 and 256 (5,256,083 against 44,525,167 instructions, 8.5 times; 322,506,865 at 2,048). The comparison with `store::preimage_ref` is unchanged; two cases in `remainder_chains_outside_the_shared_summary_keep_their_preimages` (`m = i64::MAX` and `m = i64::MIN + 1` over `[i64::MIN, i64::MIN + 40]`) are exact and empty as before, now without listing the box. Not fixed, measured, recorded at `split_residues`: a term the summary does not read (an `if` on another test, `min`, values that may leave `i64`) is read by each class's run, `G` times one transfer of it: over `[-5000, 5000]` a `min` chain is about k^2.7 (21.7M to 5.25G at k = 8 to 64; main 29.8M to 5.69G), and `k` tests `i >= 7j` above `k` remainder guards k^2 (22.4M to 358M at k = 16 to 64; main 112M to 1,667M). Also not fixed and the same on main: where the split is given up for a mixed stretch over many periods (C17) the linear stage is slow and inexact, for example `k` guards `i mod 1024 != j` over `[-5000, 5000]` (0.86G and 3.0G at k = 8 and 16) and the chain of `many_remainders` below `if (i mod 1024 == 0) { false }` (11.3G and 84.1G at k = 8 and 16); open: S22e-wide (ledger). |
| S22e-wide | `preimage`, the split by remainders of S22e over `i` in `[-5000, 5000]` on four programs: (A) terms `Tx::by_class` did not read, a chain of `k` guards `i mod 1024 == j` with arms `min(t + 1, 2^40)` and `k` tests `i >= 7j` above `k` such guards; (B) splits given up for a stretch where some classes keep their points and others do not that spans many periods (C17), `k` guards `i mod 1024 != j` and `many_remainders(k)` below `if (i mod 1024 == 0) { false }` | (A) each class's run read the term, `G` transfers: the `min` chain about k^2.7 (21.7M to 5.25G instructions at k = 8 to 64), the tests above k^2 (22.4M to 358M at k = 16 to 64). (B) the linear stage, slow and inexact: 0.86G and 3.0G at k = 8 and 16, and 11.3G and 84.1G | fixed, design at `split_residues`. (A) a term that reads no remainder has one transfer in every class, so `Tx::by_class` summarizes it once over the box and uses that summary in every class (`min` and `max` with a constant, an `if` on a test that reads no remainder whose arms add constants to one tree, the affine maps): 2.24M to 17.6M from k = 8 to 64 (7.8 times) and 6.44M to 53.7M from 16 to 128 (8.3 times), both exact. (B) cause: the linear stage's translation gives each `x mod m` its own pair of auxiliary variables, so `k` guards project `k` pairs, above linear, and its exactness check decides the negated body, `k` disjuncts. The split now answers itself (`Split::Wide`): one relation with one pair for the split input, a flat disjunct per piece (`residue_relation`), projected over the box's hull (`project_classes`), exact when every member of the box lies in its class's set (`covers`, a sweep with a Fenwick tree, O(P log P), no Presburger decision); a merged stretch of periods is read once more by `mod_pre`, which lists its first and last; an answer found before the first transfer is kept, and the transfer and the split over its box run as without it, exact there for `many_remainders`. 17.3M to 20.6M from k = 4 to 32 (1.2 times) and 85.9M to 498M (5.8 times), both exact. Test `l01_25_preimage_catalog::s22::remainders_over_many_periods_scale` (the four, n and 8n, limit 24) and `remainders_over_many_periods_keep_their_preimages` (all four exact, checked by the interpreter). Negative controls, not committed: `Tx::by_class` returning `None` fails the scale test (`min` chain 28.1 times the bytes, the tests above 56.8 times); the split given up on a mixed stretch, as on main, gives 248M to 10.9G and 1.59G to 85.6G from k = 4 to 32. Comparison with `store::preimage_ref`: in the random guarded chains 84 more cases are made exact (79 against 39, and 100 against 56), one narrowed and one reflected box among them; 96 are inexact boxes with the classes' relation, checked exact on the box against the interpreter (`split_relation_exact`), three of them wider than main's box (the hull of a class's members joins pieces main's projection kept apart, where the relation holds no point; listed in `more_random_guarded_chains_keep_their_preimages_3000` and `_3750`); `a_split_by_remainders_is_given_up_where_it_cannot_be_exact` and two `entries` cases (`i mod 10 == 3` over `[0, 40]`, now exact, and over `[0, 10^9]`, one more period listed at each end) change, as do two cases outside the catalog: `l01_38_presburger::preimage::the_z3_fixture_reproduces_exactly` (`x mod 7 != 3` over `[0..=49]`: 3, 10, 17, 38 and 45 leave the box, 24 and 31 stay in the merged middle, still necessary, the relation one disjunct per piece and class) and `l01_39_reduced_product::spec::an_arm_that_fixes_n_mod_4_within_0_to_10_holds_1_to_9` (the else arm also loses 5, since the preimage of `n mod 4 == 1` over `[0..=10]` is now exactly `{1, 5, 9}`; the arm DESIGN L1.39 states, `int[1..=9]`, is unchanged). Corner cases: `remainder_splits_at_their_corners_keep_their_preimages` (m = 1, 2, 3, 1024, `i64::MAX`, `i64::MIN + 1`; empty, single-point, within-two-periods, unbounded-end and comb boxes). Not fixed, recorded at `split_residues` and `Tx::by_class`: terms the summary still does not read (an `if` whose arms differ by more than a constant, a test of a term that reads a remainder, `min` across its constant, values that may leave `i64`) are read by each class, `O(G n log n)`; and over many periods the box keeps a mixed stretch's middle periods as one piece, inexact with the exact relation, since a piece per period has no bound in the term's size. A complete solution would hold the congruence facet in the backward transfer's sets. |
| S21 | `preimage`, T11-preimage: results were remembered by the addresses of a term and a scope, with a list that kept every scope alive so that no address was reused; every memo was a `RefCell`; a product read as a power compared its factors part by part, so each product of a long name cost the name's size | O(k·m) for k products of a name of m parts | fixed: the terms of one transfer are numbered once (`Terms::add`, an explicit stack with a visited set over the shared parts of the given term), scopes are numbered once for each scope and binding they extend, and every memo is keyed by `(term number, scope number)`; the transfer is `&mut self`, with no `RefCell` and no list of kept scopes; equal factors are found by their number, or by a structural walk over pairs of numbers when they are separate copies. Tests: `long_squares_of_one_name` at 256 and 2,048, 8.5 times the work (old code 39.9, and 55.1 at 1,024 and 8,192); `long_shared_arms`, `k` branches whose arms are one shared term, 6.6 times at 48 and 384 (without the visited set 18,554.7 times at 2 and 16, and the test does not finish in 60 s). `exact_search` passes `branch` a `Search` trait object (`skip(&self)`, `all(&mut self)`) in place of two closures sharing a `RefCell`. Not changed: each box of `branch` and `within_classes` copies its `Domains`, a stop prints its term, `close` copies the term it closes, and the transfer still recurses on depth (T2-mid) |
| S24 | `product::Scope::close`, T-zone: an arm test that calls a function in scope is closed over that function and those it calls, as `let`s in binding order, before its preimage | O(functions reached + their sizes) per test, each function once however many paths reach it | linear: `l01_39_reduced_product::scaling::tests_read_through_the_functions_they_call_scale`, an `else if` chain of k tests each calling a function, 8.0 times the work at 64 and 512, and one test through k functions each calling the two before it, 8.5 times at 32 and 256 (limit 24). Control: without the visited set the second does not finish in 120 s. The closed test is kept as a written constraint, so its size counts once per arm. Sizes stop at 256 because the preimage recurses on the depth of the term (T2-mid): a test of 2,000 nested additions overflows a test thread's stack without any function. Since TD-ends (ends of any size) a level of the nest takes about 4.1 KB of stack against 3.8 KB, so 256 levels pass the 2 MiB of a test thread and the nest test runs on a 256 MiB thread |
| S25 | `product::Scope::narrow` and `reduce_with`, `presburger::project_each` (ST1, ST2; cross-review xv Q8): a test with nested disjunctions and `if`s was split into cases by recursion, each case copying the scope's names and reducing them all; `project_each` projected each disjunct of a part onto every symbol of the part; a reduce re-projected after `project` without need | O(k²) to O(k³) in the nested tests (`pairs` 7,875 → 50,130 M instructions at k = 50 → 100; `cond2` 1,232 → 173,325 at 50 → 400) | fixed: the tree of a test is normalized once (sides sorted, the heavy side expanded into one list of cases), its relation is taken before narrowing; a scope's names are an `imbl::OrdMap`, a join walks the `diff` of the two maps, and a reduce sweeps only the names changed since the last one; `project_each` projects a disjunct only onto the groups of symbols (the existing `Linked` union-find) that it touches. Measured, M instructions at k = 50/100/200/400, branch against main 78ddc7e: or-nest 15/35/78/173 against 42/169/709/3,050; `if` chain 15/34/77/171 against 45/175/720/3,070; thresholds 8/20/48/111 against 44/172/718/3,084; `pairs` 153/357/805/1,803 against 7,875/50,130/over 60 s; `cond2` 139/308/676/1,456 against 1,232/5,683/29,474/173,325; ping (box 50 to 400) 111 to 151 against 119 to 161. Tests: `l01_39_reduced_product::nests`, at k and 8k. Negative control, run once and not committed: on main the or-nest test reads 72 times the work (limit 24), the walk 721 (limit 192), and B1, B2, ping and the grouping tests fail. Kept, hard: the walk `v_i + w <= 0 \|\| v_(i+1) - w >= 1` through a shared `w` is quadratic (855/3,176/10,911/37,940 at n = 12/25/50/100; main 3,798/38,451/327,492/over 60 s), because each case of a clause fixes `w` and so changes the parts of all earlier clauses, which must each be projected again: the per-case output is Θ(i), Θ(n²) in all (`a_walk_through_a_shared_name_reaches_the_fixed_point_in_quadratic_time`, order 2). Known precision limit (B4): `x - y >= 5 \|\| y - x >= 5` then `-2 <= x - y <= 2` gives a sound but non-empty scope, since the band moves no single name's bound and the part is not projected again; in the other order the scope is empty, so this result depends on the order of the tests. An exact fix in O(n log n) is open (xv Q8 report) (`a_band_after_a_disjunction_is_a_known_precision_limit`). Repair 2 (verifier): narrowing by tests on names apart was still Θ(k²), since `atom` conjoined each test into the whole relation as one formula, `join` ran `stated` and `either` over both whole relations, and `project` rebuilt the blocks over all conjuncts each time. Fixed: the relation is a persistent set of conjuncts (`conjuncts::Conjuncts`, `imbl::OrdSet`) with an index from each name to the conjuncts that mention it and one for the tighter-bound rule; a reduce projects only the connected components reached from the names and conjuncts changed since the snapshot taken before the last `project`, and drops the cached parts of a removed conjunct by a range query; a join diffs the two conjunct sets and runs `stated` and `either` on the differing conjuncts alone, and takes the Karr hull only over the equalities reachable from them; enumeration visits only the written terms, names and enumerated entries that changed. Measured, M instructions at k = 100/200/400/800, against 78ddc7e: k disjunctions on names apart in one conjunction 621/1,275/2,601/5,326 against 1,702/5,943/22,050 at 100/200/400; a conjunction of k tests 102/208/425/867 against 125/323/966; k disjunctions assumed one by one 457/932/1,911/3,942. Test `l01_39_reduced_product::scaling::tests_on_names_apart_narrow_in_time_near_their_number`, at k = 50 and 400. Negative control, run once and not committed: at 78ddc7e the test reads 53.1 times the work (867 to 46,082 M; limit 24). Precision against the previous commit on 3,000 random nested tests: 2,998 equal, 2 narrower, both checked sound by enumerating every integer point in a box. Still O(written) per reduce: `enumerate_exactly`, under `@exact` only. B4 and the walk stay open as above. Repair 3 (verifier): tests assumed one at a time on one linked component were Θ(k²), since each `project` rebuilt the blocks of the component and projected all of them (M instructions at k = 50/100/200/400 on e658011: chain 100/308/1,099/4,128; star 112/372/1,372/5,423; or-chain 479/1,347/4,411/15,699). Fixed: `Conjuncts` keeps its block-cut forest up to date as conjuncts come and go (`conjuncts::Blocks`, persistent maps; a union-find of trees, the smaller tree rerooted on a link, a merge along the tree path moving the smaller blocks into the largest; a leaf block is removed in O(log n), any other removal rebuilds its component, O(component)); `project` runs a worklist over the due blocks in the order of their least conjuncts, and a block that moves a name puts the other blocks that mention it on the worklist. When a block moves the name whose move put it there (an echo, as with a parity the intervals do not hold), the tree's conjuncts are projected together once, O(tree), and the worklist stops. Measured on the branch: chain 52/108/223/459, star 55/115/241/496, or-chain 213/437/904/1,876; a chain as one conjunction and the tightening of one pair unchanged. Tests `l01_39_reduced_product::linked`: chain, bounded chain, star and or-chain at k = 50 and 400 (order 1), the bounds along a bounded chain, and 200 random runs of 24 operations whose blocks must equal those found by the definition and those built afresh in reverse order. Negative control, run once and not committed: at e658011 the chain reads 41.0 times the work (100 to 4,126 M; limit 24). Precision: on 300 random three-name tests none is unsound; 14 are wider than at e658011 and none narrower is guaranteed (the mechanism of e658011 that gave the narrower results is not identified). Re-projecting every part of a block that gained a constraint closed those 14 and B4, but made `narrowing_by_a_conjunction_of_disjunctions_scales` quadratic (66 times at 8 and 64), since each case of each clause re-projects all earlier parts; it is not kept. Kept, hard, the same order at e658011: one block grown one test at a time on two names is quadratic by constraints (22/80/297/1,201 at k = 12/25/50/100; e658011 23/83/313/1,279) and cubic by alternating constraints and disjunctions (e658011 126/1,073/7,765/68,357), since each new conjunct of the block is projected with all its constraints; a fix needs incremental projection Repair 4 (verifier): (a) k `if` statements joined one after another on a linked chain were Θ(k²) (M instructions at k = 50/100/200/400: 326/876/2,733/9,719), since `Scope::join` removed each conjunct of the changed block first, which cut the chain's tree, and `Blocks::remove` then built the blocks of the whole component again. Fixed: `Conjuncts::swap` takes the old conjuncts out of the indices only and gives each new conjunct with the same atoms the place of an old one (`Blocks::replace`); only old conjuncts left over are removed: 241/497/1,026/2,095 (8.7 times for 8 times; `l01_39_reduced_product::linked::if_statements_joined_one_after_another_on_a_linked_chain_take_time_near_their_number`; negative control, the old join: 270 M to 9,240 M, 34.2 times). (b) `assume(a && b)` with `a` and `b` on names apart was wider than `assume(a)` then `assume(b)` (37 of 1,500 random cases, seed 7). Three causes: a conjunct of no atoms (`!(0 == 0)` from `x != x`) was kept and never projected, so even `x != x` alone did not empty the scope; `!(t >= 0)` stayed a negation, so it and an equation on the same names were split into different parts; `relation_of` of the whole conjunction fails when one side has a `mod`, so the side with an `if` lost its relation. Fixed: a conjunct of no atoms is decided when it joins and a false relation empties the scope; `!(t >= 0)` becomes `-1 - t >= 0`; `narrow_linear` takes the sides of a conjunction at the root one by one when the whole has no linear relation. Now 0 of 7,500 (seeds 1–4, 7) are wider; some are narrower, since inside a conjunction a test such as `y != 3` joins the relation (`nests::a_conjunction_of_tests_on_names_apart_narrows_as_the_tests_one_after_the_other`, `nests::a_test_no_value_meets_gives_an_empty_scope_however_it_is_written`; both fail on e60c977). (c) A bound that echoes no longer stops the worklist: it projects its tree once, and the tree's other blocks are skipped while the blocks of other trees go on. Open: the tree sizes kept in `Blocks` are never reduced when conjuncts leave; they only choose which tree is rerooted on a link, so this affects balance, not results. Repair 5 (verifier): after the chain `x_i - x_(i+1) >= 1`, k `if x_i - x_(i+2) >= 3` statements joined one after another cost about k^4 (M instructions at k = 5/10/15/20: 141/1,867/8,743/28,333 at 78ddc7e, 117/1,779/8,489/27,903 at b54d2a6), since `Formula::either` kept the disjunction of the test and its complement, `x_i >= x_(i+2) + 3 \|\| x_i <= x_(i+2) + 2`, which holds at every point, and each later test split into cases on all of them. Fixed: `Formula::or` gives `true` when two disjuncts are complements, `g` and `!g`, or `t >= 0` and `s >= 0` with `t + s` a constant of at least -1, found by one hash lookup per disjunct, so in time linear in the disjunction and with no satisfiability question: 11/27/44/60 M. The joins were then still near k^2 (join alone 83/356/1,599/6,948 M at k = 50/100/200/400), since removing the test's conjunct from the cycle it closed with `x_(i+1)` built the blocks of the whole chain again. Fixed: `Blocks::split` removes a conjunct from a block of names by placing the block's other conjuncts again, in time near the block, when they still link all its names; the tree stays one tree, and only the pieces of the names below the block are rerooted. Join alone 11/26/56/120 M; the whole family 180/375/776/1,592 M at k = 50/100/200/400 (8.8 times for 8 times; was 253/713/2,346/8,552 with the first fix only). Tests `l01_39_reduced_product::linked`: `if_statements_on_skip_links_of_a_chain_take_time_near_their_number` (k = 50 and 400, order 1), `a_disjunction_of_a_constraint_and_its_complement_is_true` (non-complements, including a gap of one value and twice the same constraint, are kept), `an_if_on_a_chain_whose_arms_cover_every_value_leaves_no_disjunction`, `a_join_whose_arms_leave_a_gap_keeps_the_disjunction`. Negative controls, run once and not committed: without the complement rule the scaling test runs past 120 s and the two tautology tests fail; without `Blocks::split` it reads 33.4 times (254 to 8,492 M; limit 24). The random block test passed 12,000 further cases (4 seeds); the verifier's soundness probe gives 0 unsound of 1,500 nested sequences and 0 wider of 1,800 `a && b` on names apart. Kept, hard, the block grown one test at a time as above: a ladder, the chain with every `x_i - x_(i+2) >= 2`, is one block of 2k constraints, and one test on it projects the whole block, O(k^2) (19/80/359/1,680 M at k = 10/20/40/80); k `if x_i - x_(i+2) >= 2` statements on the chain, each implied, so each join keeps the true arm and grows the ladder, cost O(k^3) (16/89/608/4,869 M at k = 5/10/20/40). A fix needs the bounds of all names of a block of difference constraints in one shortest-path pass, or incremental projection; open. Repair 6 (verifier): the complement lookup of repair 5 put every disjunct in a `HashSet<&Formula>`, which hashes it through its whole depth; `Formula::or` runs once per `Or` node of `map_cons`, `nnf`, `forget` and the preimage builders, so building and mapping a nested or/and formula of depth d cost O(d^2) (verifier: 25/102/397/1,573 ms at d = 500/1,000/2,000/4,000, against 1.7/3.3/6.7/16 ms at b54d2a6), and `product::arms` on `if x >= k \|\| (y >= k && …)` nested d deep 2.2/11.5/71.7 s at d = 250/500/1,000 (b54d2a6 0.38/1.5/6.6 s). Fixed: only constraints and negations of constraints are looked up (`HashSet<&Con>`), O(1) per disjunct; a compound `g \|\| !g` is not found and stays a disjunction, sound (Complexity tradeoff comment at `Formula::complementary`). Verifier with this fix: 5/8/25/29 ms at d = 500 to 4,000; program 0.58/1.5/5.5/20.6 s, as at b54d2a6. Test `l01_39_reduced_product::linked::a_nested_or_and_formula_is_built_and_mapped_in_time_near_its_size` (d = 250 and 2,000, order 1); correctness `a_nested_or_and_formula_keeps_every_case` (every integer point of an 8x8 window), `a_compound_disjunct_and_its_negation_are_kept_as_written`. Negative control, run once and not committed: the deep `HashSet<&Formula>` restored reads 62.6 times the work (191 to 11,977 M instructions; limit 24) and the compound test fails. Open, pre-existing: `map_cons` and the other traversals of `Formula` recurse, and a nest of depth 4,000 overflows the 2 MB stack of a test thread, so the test uses d = 250 and 2,000; `product::arms` on a deeply nested condition is quadratic at b54d2a6 as well (0.38 to 27.3 s at d = 250 to 2,000), to be its own item. |
| S26 | `hull::Hull::and`, `Hull::join`, `hull::used`, `product::Scope::reduce_domains`, `meet_ints`, `Scope::bind` (T11-fix-store; T11 audit sites hull.rs:129, :198, product.rs:299, :325, :691 at f425b32) | `Hull::and` copied the whole hull (`self.clone()`) and `used` copied each inner map of `uses` before changing it, a refcount pair per row and per atom; `reduce_domains` copied the interval set before narrowing it | fixed or kept, site by site: `Hull::and` and `Hull::join` take the hull by value and change its rows and uses in place (`join` collects the differing rows from the `diff` first, then removes them); `used` changes the inner map through `entry` and `get_mut`; an absent key is looked up with `contains_key` first, since `imbl` 7.0.2 `remove` and `get_mut` copy the path to a key of a shared map even when the key is absent (without the check `a_join_costs_near_the_rows_that_differ` took 21% more instructions); `reduce_domains` borrows `ints()` and `meet_ints` takes its set by value. Kept: the snapshot in `Scope::bind` (`Unbind::Saved(Box::new(self.clone()))`), since every field of `Scope` is a persistent map or a small value, so the copy is about 20 reference-count increments, O(1), and it is what `unbind` restores after a shadowing binding; and `d.clone()` in the caller that reads `d` from the premises' domains. Measured, instructions at n and 8n against main 03d3d41: a hull built row by row (512/4,096 rows) 11.5/98.3 M, 8.6 times, against 18.0/167.9 M; joins in a loop 3.94/48.7 M against 3.92/48.5 M; a chain of 100/800 shadowing `let`s 16.5/136.7 M, 8.3 times, against 16.7/137.9 M. Tests: `l01_39_reduced_product::hull::a_hull_is_built_row_by_row_in_time_near_its_rows`, `names_equal_to_one_name_and_then_fixed_are_all_fixed`; `l01_39_reduced_product::scaling::a_chain_of_shadowing_lets_scales`, `a_binding_that_reads_the_name_it_shadows_keeps_what_held_of_it`.
