Negative controls, run once and not committed: a deep copy of the rows in `Hull::and` reads 71.5 times the work (334 to 23,873 M; limit 24); a copy of the name map in the `Saved` snapshot reads 42.6 times (31 to 1,320 M). Open, pre-existing (T2-mid): `arms` recurses on the depth of nested `let`s and overflows a 2 MB test thread near 800, so the shadowing test runs on a 256 MB stack. Possible follow-up: the copy `before` of each domain in the reduce loop of `bind`, kept for change detection, could go if `reduce_domains` returned whether it changed anything. |
| S27 | `presburger::Formula` (ST-deep-stack): `map_cons`, `forget`, `holds`, `cons`, `nnf` (through `satisfiable`), and the derived `Drop`, `Clone`, `PartialEq`, `Ord`, `Hash`, `Debug` and `Display` recursed on the depth of a formula | stack O(depth): a formula nested 4,000 deep overflowed a 2 MiB thread (ST repair 6) | fixed, linear, no depth limit, memory the only bound: each walk keeps its work on the heap (`store/presburger/deep.rs`): a post-order `fold` with a context passed down (`map_cons`, `forget`, `holds_as`, `nnf`, `Clone`), work lists for `Drop`, `PartialEq`, `Ord`, `Hash`, `cons` and printing; the order, equality and printed text are those of the derived forms (`l01_38_presburger::deep::small_formulas_print_compare_and_hash_as_before` passes on the old code too). Tests `l01_38_presburger::deep`, each on a 2 MiB thread: a chain of 100,000 guards (`&&`, `\|\|` and `!` in turn, about 133,000 deep) mapped, copied, compared, hashed, printed, decided and dropped; scaling at 12,500 and 100,000 guards, inputs built before timing: map, copy, compare, hash, print and drop 193.4 M and 1,547.9 M instructions (8.0 times), `satisfiable` 159.2 M and 1,273.3 M (8.0 times). Negative controls run: the old code (03d3d41) aborts with a stack overflow on both tests at 100,000; a quadratic `PartialEq` (printing each node's subtree) fails the scaling test at 61.2 times (250 to 2,000, limit 24). Cost: whole test groups take 1 to 6 per cent more instructions than at 03d3d41 (`l01_39_reduced_product` 5.8, `l01_38_presburger::random` 4.3, `::omega` 2.6, `l01_25_preimage_catalog` 1.0), from the extra lists of the fold and the walks. Still recursive, bounded since `Formula::and` flattens nested conjunctions: `lits` and `Conjuncts::and_held` descend only through `And` inside `And` |
| S28 | `product::Scope::refusals` and `Scope::warnings` (TD-refusals): each question the integer solver refuses while a scope is narrowed or projected is kept as a `Refused` (the constraint as printed, how it was asked, the cause) and reported as one warning; a join unites the two sets | O(log k) per refusal kept, the constraint printed once per refused test or part; a join's union by `imbl::OrdSet::diff`, which skips what the arms share; the warnings O(k) in the k refusals, in the set's order (by constraint, how asked, cause), so independent of the order the questions were met in | linear: `l01_39_reduced_product::refused::warnings_of_many_refused_tests_scale`, measured at load 29 to 30 in instructions: narrowing by k refused tests 16 → 128, 8.5 times (mostly the solver, about 11M instructions per refused test); keeping k refusals alone through `Scope::refuse`, 4096 → 32768, 9.4 times; the join of two arms that share k − 2 refused tests and add one each, 256 → 2048, 1.1 times (each arm has k − 1 refusals and four conjuncts per test, the join k refusals, which the test checks; below 256 a fixed cost of about 2.8M instructions per join hides a quadratic union); the warnings of that join, 256 → 2048, 8.4 times (limit 24). Negative controls, each run once and not committed: warnings ordered by counting, for each refusal, the refusals below it (O(k²)), fail the warnings check at 28.7 times; a linear scan of the refusals in `Scope::refuse` (O(k²)) fails the keeping check at 64.3 times; the join's union by a linear scan for each refusal (O(k²)) fails the join check at 31.8 times (load 35 to 39). Not measured here, fixed by ST-join (S29): the join of two arms that each add k tests on names apart the other lacks is near k³ whether the tests are refused or not (k = 16/32/64 per arm: refused 0.14/1.3/6.9 s, decided 0.03/0.20/1.28 s; profile: comparisons of the one disjunction the join builds over all of them); `warnings()` on that join stays linear (19/48/78 µs). The warnings carry no line and column: kernel terms have no source positions, and no checker path builds product scopes yet (cross-review xv synthesis item 1), so the site is the printed constraint |
| S29 | `product::Scope::join` through `conjuncts::Blocks::remove`, `Blocks::place` and `Blocks::split` (ST-join): the join of two arms that each narrow k groups of four names the other does not. The join's relation is one disjunction over all their names, which takes the place of one side's k groups in a single block | O(k³): `remove` split that block once per conjunct removed, each split placing the block's other conjuncts again (O(k) splits of O(k) conjuncts); and `place` and `split` looked the new disjunction up in `of` once per name it mentions, each lookup comparing the whole disjunction (O(k) lookups of O(k)) | fixed, near linear: `remove` groups the removed conjuncts by block and splits each block once for all it loses, unlinking a block's one conjunct as before; `place` and `split` keep the block that holds the conjunct they place (`unite` returns it) instead of looking the conjunct up again. Join alone at k = 8, 16, 32, 64, decided groups: 26 ms, 152 ms, 1.12 s, 6.26 s before; 1.5, 3.8, 7.6, 17.2 ms after (refused groups: 25 ms to 10.3 s before; 2.2 to 17.9 ms after). Test `l01_39_reduced_product::disjoint::the_join_of_arms_on_many_disjoint_groups_scales` at k = 16 and 128, arms built before timing: join 16.8 M and 171.5 M instructions (10.2 times) on decided groups, 18.5 M and 189.3 M (10.2 times) on refused groups; join and reduce 9.6 and 10.8 times (load 39 to 55). Same results: 600 random joins (names a to f), each printed with its reduced form and the join the other way, are identical byte for byte on the old and new code; new tests check the join's blocks against their definition (`disjoint::the_blocks_of_a_join_are_those_of_the_definition`, `linked::the_blocks_stay_those_of_the_definition_when_many_conjuncts_go_at_once`) and the edges (an empty side, a side with no relation, a nested side, a side joined with itself, k = 1). Negative control run: the old `conjuncts.rs` fails the scaling test at k = 8 and 64 with 304.9 times (178.5 M and 54,444 M instructions, limit 24) |
| S30 | `preimage::Tx::power` (CAP-power): a product of more than `POWER_FACTORS` (64) equal factors was not read as a power (a size switch); and each product the transfer reached gathered its factors again, walking down to its first factor | O(n·depth): a chain of n factors cost n² | fixed: each product is read once, bottom up on an explicit stack, into `Tx::powers` by its term and scope; it joins its two operands' readings by comparing their first factors, and counts any number of factors (since CAP-pow32, S30b, a count past `u32::MAX` is read as a power too). `l01_25_preimage_catalog::powers::reading_a_product_as_a_power_is_linear_in_its_factors`, 512 and 4096 factors: a power 5.48M to 43.3M instructions (7.9 times), alternating factors 54.7M to 451M (8.3 times); before, 85.2M to 2652M (31.1 times) and 94.7M to 2671M (28.2 times) |
| S30b | `revision::Op::Pow` (CAP-pow32): the exponent was a `u32`, so a product of more than `u32::MAX` equal factors (reachable through about 32 nested squaring names) and `x ** k` with `k > u32::MAX` were read by the entry for `*` or not at all (a size switch) | a size switch, not a cost | fixed: `Op::Pow` holds an `Exponent`, a solver `Int` or the kernel's integer as the kernel holds it (`10 ** 10 ** 9` as an unbuilt prime power, never built); every entry reads only whether it is 0, its parity and its value when it fits in `u32`, each O(1), since for a base other than -1, 0 and 1 an exponent past 127 overflows `i128` and the bound is infinite whatever its size. The power reading of `preimage::Tx` holds its count as a `Count`: the value while it fits in `u32` and the parity, which is all an entry reads, so each name costs O(1) (`Exponent::Past` past `u32::MAX`). Before CAP-pow32 repair 1 the count was an exact `Int`, `d` bits at depth `d`: peak RSS 248, 633 and 1800 MB at depths 32768, 65536 and 131072 (verify 1, not committed); with `Count` 177, 361 and 730 MB, and 580M to 4936M instructions from depth 16384 to 131072 (8.51 times; probe, not committed, load 75 to 85). `l01_25_preimage_catalog::powers::reading_nested_squares_as_a_power_is_linear_in_their_depth`, depths 512 and 4096 (check_order, power 1); a probe, not committed, at load 114: 14.7M, 127M and 1103M instructions at depths 512, 4096 and 32768 (8.66 and 8.65 times). Since TD-cong (S36) the end is exact for every exponent held as a `Num` that is not kept; only `Exponent::Past`, a kept exponent and a kept base that is not bare raised past `u32` are still bounded outward |
| S31 | `product::arms` on a test nested d deep (`x >= 0 \|\| (y >= 0 && (x >= 1 \|\| …))`), ST-arms-quad: `preimage::Tx::translated` copied each term's formula into the memo, so each part was copied once for each term above it; `presburger::project_each`, at each level, brought the disjunct and the whole chain below it to negation normal form again, copied it, rebuilt the atom set of the whole chain below, and `Formula::holds` walked all its atoms before testing it | O(d²) | fixed: a formula is kept in the memo only from the second time it is asked for (`Tx::shared`), so a term is translated at most twice (superseded by ST-formula-share below: a formula is now shared, so `Tx::translated` keeps it the first time and `Tx::shared` is gone); `project_parts` keeps each disjunct as shared parts (`Rc<Parts>`), so no level copies the nest below it, and computes each part's atom set once, bottom up, from the largest child's set (a persistent `imbl::OrdSet`, cloned in O(1)) with the smaller ones inserted; `settle` filters disjuncts by their own constraints. Tests `l01_39_reduced_product::deep_condition` (instructions, limit 24): chain d = 60 → 480 9.2 times, 250 → 2000 9.2 times (5.0 G at 2000); two disjunctions per level 9.4 and 10.0 times (8.5 G at 2000); `preimage::relations` 250 → 2000 about 8 times. Controls on f6d11c5: old `presburger.rs` 26.0 (chain 60 → 480), 43.5 (chain 250 → 2000), 27.4 (two, 60 → 480); old `preimage.rs` 59.7 (relations), 30.4 (chain 250 → 2000), 30.0 (two 250 → 2000). Also fixed (repair 2), both also O(d²) at the base commit 03d3d41 and both in `presburger::project_parts`, the projection every caller of `project_each` uses: (a) a nest that names new symbols at each level (`x0 >= 0 \|\| (y0 >= 0 && (x1 >= 1 \|\| …))`) was 3.3 to 3.7 times per doubling of d (26.3 G at d = 480), since each level read every atom below it and asked the level below for a hull of every symbol they touch, though a symbol that a disjunct with a point does not touch keeps the base's hull. Now the disjuncts are read smallest first and each is asked only for the symbols that every disjunct with a point before it touched, and the groups a disjunct touches are found from its atoms or from the base's, whichever are fewer. (b) a nest with two disjunctions and a hull with two finite bounds at each level (a cap `x <= 2d`) was 3.4 to 4.0 times per doubling (82.9 G at d = 480), since each level rebuilt the formula below it and tightened each hull by O(log w) calls of `decide` over all of it. Now a level tightens a hull with two finite bounds over a summary of itself: its base and, for each disjunction, each disjunct with a point as its own constraints and, when it has disjunctions, its projections in their place (`presburger::summarize`); only the top level tightens over the whole formula. The summary has more points than the level, so a hull stays sound but can be wider where only the disjunctions inside a disjunct, jointly with the rest of the level, push a bound inward (tradeoff comment at `summarize`). Tests `deep_condition::arms_of_a_chain_of_accounts_take_time_near_linear_in_its_depth` (d = 60 → 480) and `arms_of_a_capped_test_take_time_near_linear_in_its_depth` (d = 30 → 240). Controls, not committed: the code of 904eeab fails both (43.6 and 51.6 times). Repair 2, second attempt, three more O(d²) or worse costs on nests whose hull of `x` moves at each level, found by the verifier: (d) `moving`, `(100 <= x <= 200 && w >= i) \|\| (0 <= x <= 10 && (x >= 5 \|\| y >= 0) && (y <= -1 \|\| x >= 7) && level i + 1)`, cubic at af39546 (0.54 G to 888 G at d = 30 to 480): `settle` flattened one level of disjunctions left with one disjunct per round, each round a solve over a base that kept every bound written again at each level. Now `settle` reads the disjunctions as a worklist, adds a disjunct left alone to the base at once with its disjunctions queued, and the base keeps one bound per left-hand side and comparison (`Linked::meet`, a `HashMap` from the terms to the constraint). (e) `heavy`, `(100 <= x <= 200 && v >= i) \|\| ((y >= 0 \|\| z >= 0) && ((20 <= x <= 30 && w >= i) \|\| (0 <= x <= 10 && level i + 1)))`, 29.5 G at d = 480, and (f) `pick`, which tested each disjunct with `Parts::holds` over the whole nest below it once for each level: `pick` now takes a disjunct that holds at the model together with the first disjunct that holds in each disjunction below it, found in one walk (`Parts::witness`), keeps one bound per left-hand side in what it has taken, marks each part found not to hold with a stamp that changes only when the model does (`Parts::failed`), and takes a disjunct whose own constraints hold without changing the model, so no part is walked again at each level; the `Around` shortcut is gone. Measured in instructions after the fix, d = 120, 240, 480, 960, 1920, 3840: `moving` 1.18, 2.36, 4.68, 9.38, 18.7, 37.5 G (2.00 times per doubling); `heavy` 0.72, 1.42, 2.82, 5.61, 11.2, 22.4 G (2.00 times); a nest whose model changes at each level (`w >= i + 1 \|\| z <= -1 - i` beside each level, either order) 2.00 times up to d = 960. Hulls unchanged: `moving` then `x` in `[5..=10, 100..=200]`, `heavy` `[0..=10, 20..=30, 100..=200]`. Tests `deep_condition::arms_of_a_test_whose_bound_moves_at_each_level_keep_the_inner_bound`, `arms_of_a_test_whose_small_choice_misses_the_bound_keep_every_range` and the two `..._take_time_near_linear_in_its_depth` tests (d = 60 → 480). Controls, not committed, on af39546: the `heavy` scaling test fails (0.71 G → 29.5 G, 41.7 times, limit 24) and the `moving` one does not finish in 60 s. Repair 3, after verify#3 on 873f1a8: (g) the summary lost precision that af39546 had: probe P2, `100 <= x <= 200 \|\| (0 <= x <= 10 && (q >= 0 && (x <= 0 && y >= 1 \|\| x >= 10 && y <= -1) \|\| q <= -1 && 3 <= x <= 4) && (y >= 1 && x >= 5 \|\| q <= -1))`, gave `x` in `[3..=4, 10..=10, 100..=200]` for `[3..=4, 100..=200]`. `summarize` now also keeps each of a disjunct's disjunctions with every disjunct cut to its own constraints, so each part's constraints are read at its own level and the level above, twice in all; P2 is exact again. A choice two disjunctions or more inside a disjunct is still read by projections only: the hull stays wider there (test `a_choice_two_levels_inside_a_branch_is_read_by_its_projections_only`, tradeoff comment at `summarize`). Tightening below the top over the level's own formula instead, measured on this code, makes `arms_of_a_capped_test_take_time_near_linear_in_its_depth` fail at 51.1 times (d = 30 → 240, limit 24); in general the exact bound decides whether a nest of disjunctions has a point, NP-complete. Control, not committed: without the cut disjunctions the test `a_choice_one_level_inside_a_branch_is_read_with_the_rest_of_the_branch` fails (`10..=10` back). (h) A change of any value in `pick` drops every failure mark, but a marked part is reached again only after the disjunctions that the marking walk found to hold, with the model unchanged (argument in the comment at `pick`), so no part is tested again below each level; the search counts the parts it tests (`presburger::parts_tested`), and test `the_search_for_a_point_tests_each_part_of_a_deep_test_a_bounded_number_of_times` asserts at most 48 per level at d = 60 and 480 for `heavy` and `moving` (measured 40 and 24). Over an `arms` call the model changes 2 to 10 times, independent of d. Control, not committed: with the marks disabled `heavy` tests 516 parts per level at d = 60 and 3876 at 480, and the test fails. Repair 4, after verify#3: two more O(d²) costs on nests that name a new symbol at each level. (i) The accounts chain was 0.85, 1.99, 4.99 and 13.19 G at d = 240, 480, 960 and 1920 (2.3 to 2.6 times per doubling): `reduce_with` looks each conjunct up in `conjuncts::Blocks` (an `OrdMap` keyed by `Formula`), and the derived `Ord` compared the looked-up formula with the key, the very same formula, in full, a cost of its size per lookup. `Formula`'s `Ord` and `PartialEq` now return equal at once for a formula compared with itself (`std::ptr::eq`, presburger.rs), and otherwise compare as the derived order did. (j) The paired chain, `100 <= x <= 200 \|\| (…(q_i >= 0 && … \|\| q_i <= -1 && 3 <= x <= 4)…)` with a fresh `q_i` at each level, was 3.3, 8.2, 22.2 and 67.9 G at d = 240 to 1920 (2.5 to 3.1 times): each case of a test that no state reaches rebuilt every name in scope as bottom. Now `Scope::cases` and `join_cases` reduce a case with `reduce_case`, which only sets `bottom`; `reduce` and `reduce_whole`, whose scopes are shown, still empty the names (`empty_if_bottom`). After the fix, accounts 0.76, 1.65, 3.63, 7.84 G (2.15 to 2.20 times per doubling), paired 2.07 to 2.10 times per doubling. Tests `deep_condition::arms_of_a_long_chain_of_accounts_take_time_within_n_log_n` (d = 240 → 1920, 10.2 times), `arms_of_a_chain_of_paired_choices_take_time_within_n_log_n` (d = 120 → 960, 8.8 times), both with limit 8·ln(8n)/ln(n) (11.04 and 11.47), and `a_chain_of_paired_choices_keeps_only_the_small_choice` (d = 1, 2, 20, 200: then-arm `x` in `[3..=4, 100..=200]`). Controls, not committed: without (i) the accounts test fails (0.85 → 13.20 G, 15.5 times) and the paired test passes; without (j) the paired test fails (1.45 → 22.16 G, 15.2 times) and the accounts test passes. Fixed, item ST-formula-share: `let t = chain(d)` used beside each of d levels was O(d²) (0.94 to 42.6 G at d = 30 to 240; on the test family `named_test`, 1.25, 4.04, 14.35 and 53.99 G at d = 30, 60, 120 and 240 at 456873c), since `Formula` was a tree and a `let`-bound formula was copied at each use. Now `Formula`'s children are `Arc`s (`Not(Arc<Formula>)`, `And` and `Or` of `Arc<Vec<Formula>>`), so a clone is O(1) and a part used in several places is one node. Each pass reads a shared node once: `fold` (`map_cons`, `forget`, `nnf`) keeps a node's result per context, `holds_as` keeps its value, `walk` (`atoms`, `cons`) keeps a visited set, `Ord` and `PartialEq` skip a pair on the same node and keep the shared pairs found equal, and `Hash` hashes each node once, bottom up, so equal formulas hash alike however they are shared. These memos are keyed by `deep::Id`, an owned clone of the node compared by its `Arc` address, and only nodes held more than once (`Arc::strong_count > 1`) are kept. `Drop` frees a part only when it holds the last handle. `nnf` sorts a node in place only when it is the only handle (`Arc::get_mut`); a node held elsewhere is already sorted. `Parts::of` makes a shared disjunction used as a conjunct, or a shared disjunct, once, as an `Rc<Parts>`. `unbounded`'s disjunction search keeps a visited set, and its pairing of each constraint with its cone is `map_cons`. Printing (`Display`, `Debug`) writes the formula out, so its text is the size of the formula written out; a shared conjunction used as a conjunct and a shared disjunction used as a disjunct, flattened into each place at first (O(d k) for k parts used at d places), are now kept whole where the program names them (S31b, fixed by ST-formula-wide). After: 0.49, 0.96, 1.90 and 3.77 G at d = 30, 60, 120 and 240 (1.97 times per doubling), hulls unchanged. Tests `l01_38_presburger::shared` (300 random formulas with shared parts answer every question, equality, order, hash, printing, atoms, evaluation, decision, model, projection, `forget`, `map_cons`, as the same formula written out; 60 by 60 orders; empty conjunction and disjunction shared; a part under `Not` beside itself; a part in both arms of a disjunction; a part doubled 40 levels deep, 2^40 constraints written out, compared, hashed, evaluated and listed once per part) and `l01_39_reduced_product::named_test` (hulls at d = 1, 2, 5, 30 equal those of the test written out, and for the wide forms of S31b at d up to 5; scaling of `arms` at d = 30 → 240, 7.7 times, limit 24). Control, not committed: copying a `let`-bound formula at each use (the `Node::Var` case of `preimage::Tx::formula_at`) gives 1.13, 3.58, 12.59 and 46.92 G and the scaling test fails (43.9 times the bytes, limit 24). The 2 MiB stack of a test thread overflows at d = 2000; the tests run on a 512 MiB stack (ST-deep-stack) |
| S31b | `presburger::Formula::and` and `Formula::or` (found by ST-formula-share verify#1): a shared conjunction used as a conjunct, or a shared disjunction used as a disjunct, was flattened into the node around it, copied one level deep at each place (`into_parts`), and `Parts::of` and the solver after it (`settle`, `pick`, `witness`, `project_parts`) read its k parts once per place; a shared disjunction used as a conjunct (found by verify#2) was made once, but `Parts::of` copied its list of k disjuncts into each place and the solver read them once per place | O(d k): quadratic in the program's size when k is about d | Fixed by ST-formula-wide. `Formula::and_keeping` and `Formula::or_keeping` keep a part whole where the program names it: the translator keeps the formula of a name (`Node::Var`) used as an operand of `&&` or `\|\|` or in an `if` arm (`preimage::Tx::formula_at`), and `rebuild` and `nnf` keep a kid that was a kept node of the same kind, so keeping never depends on how many handles hold a part. `Parts::of` assumes by key only the parts reached through two or more parents inside the formula (`deep::Id::reached_twice`), not by reference count, so its parts follow the formula alone. Two readers read a kept part at each path to it, which made a chain of names each joining the two before it cost about 1.618 to the d (82 s at d = 28 before the fix): `project_parts` now answers a disjunct once per base and set of symbols (`Below` memo, keyed by its `Rc` address, the base constraints it is read with and the symbols), and `Conjuncts::and_held` reads on an explicit stack and reads a shared conjunction at its first place alone; `Formula::chooses` keeps a visited set. Measured with `product::arms`, d = 30 → 240, instructions and bytes: (A) k = d credit limits named once and required at each tier, 10.0 and 8.8 times; (B) k exemptions any of which approves at each tier, 13.9 and 12.3 times; (C) any one of k credit limits required at each tier, 9.7 and 8.9 times; chains of named conjunctions 10.6 and 11.1 times, of named disjunctions 7.6 and 7.6 times (limit 24). Controls, not committed: origin/main ae9b63e9 fails (A) at 36.4, (B) at 41.6 and (C) at 54.1 times the bytes, and the chain of disjunctions does not finish in 150 s; this branch with the `Below` memo off does not finish either chain in 100 s. Tests: `l01_39_reduced_product::named_test` (the three wide tests, no longer ignored; `arms_of_a_chain_of_named_{conjunctions,disjunctions}_take_time_near_linear_in_its_length`; `a_chain_of_names_narrows_as_if_written_out`, d up to 10) and `l01_38_presburger::shared`. Repair 4: `Formula`'s `Ord` and `PartialEq` compared a kept part as flattened, at each path to it, so comparing a chain of d names, each joining the two before it, with an equal or extended chain built apart cost the number of paths, about 1.618 to the d: 229 ms (conjunctions) and 74 ms (disjunctions) at d = 28 on 33ecd783. Now `cmp` reads the two sides in step (`deep::Side`, `deep::Level`): two kept parts of the level's own kind met at one point of the two flattened sequences are entered together, and when their parts run out together the pair is kept as equal, keyed by the two nodes' `deep::Id`s, so the pair is passed over when met again; the order given is that of the sequences written out. 0.02 ms for both at d = 28. Tests in `l01_38_presburger::shared`: `comparing_a_named_chain_with_one_built_apart_takes_time_near_linear_in_its_length` (d = 30 → 240, `scaling::check`), `chains_grouped_alike_or_otherwise_compare_as_their_constraints_written_out` (d up to 9, chains grouped alike and otherwise, against the order and hash of their constraints written out) and `parts_entered_together_that_end_apart_are_not_taken_as_equal`. Control, not committed: on 33ecd783 the scaling test does not finish in 60 s. Verify 5: a pair entered together whose parts ran out at one place was not kept as equal when, on the other side, a pair entered inside it had run out at another place and its level was still open, since `settle` left the first side's level first; so chains whose level i is `[[t, t], ci]` on one side and `[[t', t', ci]]` on the other cost 2^d (9.2, 33.7, 127 and 481 ms at d = 14, 16, 18, 20 on 604f00a7). `Level::settle` now leaves the level of the later entry first, whose partner is no longer open; after it, 7.3 times the instructions and 8.2 times the bytes at d = 30 → 240. Tests `chains_whose_inner_parts_end_apart_compare_in_time_near_linear_in_their_length` (d = 30 → 240; does not finish in 60 s on 604f00a7), `formulas_grouped_and_shared_at_random_compare_and_hash_as_written_out` (400 rounds of formulas over and, or and not, empty parts, grouped at random and shared at random at several depths, against an order computed apart on the formula written out and flattened) and `one_sequence_grouped_at_other_places_on_each_side_compares_as_written_out`. Left: (1) printing (`Display`, `Debug`) still writes a kept part out at each path (`deep::Flat`), so a chain of names prints in size about 1.618 to the d (57.8 KB at d = 16 to 18.6 MB at d = 28, 2.0 to 483 ms, on 33ecd783 and unchanged): messages have no syntax that names a part of a formula, and `Formula` holds no names, so the text cannot refer to a part printed before. (2) Fixed by ST-formula-shift, kept at O(n²) on one family with this reason. `cmp` on two sides that group one sequence at other places on each side, at many shared places, read a part entered on one side alone, or with a partner that runs out at another place, at each place, the size written out: `shared::shifted_chains`, 0.89, 3.5, 13.4, 52.3 and 211 ms at d = 12 to 20 (2^(d+1) - 1 constraints), measured by verify 5. Now `cmp` keeps a visited set of the shared parts of a level's own kind it enters, and when one is entered a second time it compares the parts both sides have left at that level as two sequences parsed bottom up in one arena per call (`deep::canon`; the parse of Mehlhorn, Sundar and Uhrig 1997: runs of one symbol, then chunks cut by deterministic coin tossing, so one sequence has one parse however it is grouped, and equal symbols are one arena entry; no hash decides equality). Until a shared part is entered twice the walk is the old one, linear and stopping at the first difference; a first version that parsed at the first shared part entered alone failed `named_test::arms_of_a_wide_named_disjunction_at_each_tier_take_time_near_linear_in_the_depth` at 48.0 times the bytes. Cost O(e log N) for e distinct edges and N parts written out; on `shifted_chains`, where log N is d, O(d²): 2.4 ms at d = 20, and 5.7, 25, 108 and 440 ms at d = 30, 60, 120 and 240, nearly all of it in building the parses (70,652 symbols and 374 levels at d = 240; the walk of the two roots about 1 µs); from d = 30 to 240, 74.5 times the bytes, under the limit of 192 for order n² (`one_sequence_grouped_at_other_places_on_each_side_compares_in_time_near_quadratic_in_the_depth`, `scaling::check_order` of power 2). Reason for O(n²): the exact methods known for comparing two such compressed sequences (this parse; signature encoding from a straight-line program, Nishimoto et al., MFCS 2016; recompression, Jeż 2015) cost at least n log N, and a hash of each part's sequence, linear, cannot decide equality alone; any locally consistent parse of `shifted_chains` has Θ(d²) distinct symbols (ST-formula-sig, measured on 9e80c1c5: 1,423 → 71,027 symbols, d = 30 → 240). The author accepted this on 2026-11-04: O(n log N) is always acceptable where no O(n) algorithm is possible, so this family stays at power 2 and ST-formula-sig builds nothing more. The parse is built once per call, so a pair compared again is parsed again; keeping each part's parse with the part would remove that. Control, not committed: on 6b01e145 the scaling test does not finish in 60 s, and at d = 2 → 16 it allocates 11,094.7 times the bytes. Tests also: `formulas_shared_and_regrouped_at_random_at_several_depths_compare_and_hash_as_written_out` (layered specs of depth 2, 4, 6 and 8, 60 rounds each; the parse is reached in it), the order of `shifted_chains` against the formula written out at d up to 12, and the unit tests `store::presburger::deep::canon::tests` (3,000 random sequences joined at random places against the parse from scratch, and compared against their order written out). (3) A named conjunction used as a conjunct of each of d disjuncts side by side, not nested, is read at each place, O(d k): k = d credit limits in each of d tiers (`named_test::approval_any_tier`) allocate 208.8 MB at d = 30 and 10.59 GB at d = 240, 50.7 times (limit 24). Conjoining the part common to every disjunct once, before the disjunction, was tried three ways and each changes answers: by node (`deep::Id`), a refusal's text then differs between the shared formula and the one written out; by content, `project_parts` hulls each disjunction of a part apart from the rest, so `rule && (x <= 0 \|\| y <= 0)` projects `x` to `[..]` where `(rule && x <= 0) \|\| (rule && y <= 0)` gives `[..=0, 5..]`, and `l01_25_preimage_catalog::s22` makes 15 boxes exact instead of 16; by content and only for conjunctions of constraints, s22 still 15, and hashing each conjunct at each level makes `deep_condition::arms_of_a_deeper_test_take_time_near_linear_in_its_depth` 25.1 times (limit 24). Both this shape and the nested one also grew about as k² in k: at d = 30, k = 240, 480, 960, 2.16, 8.25, 35.0 G instructions side by side and 3.42, 10.5, 39.2 G nested, near linear in d at fixed k (k = 30, d = 240, 480, 960: 1.18, 2.50, 5.31 G). Fixed by ST-formula-k2: the cause was the translation of the chain `l0 && l1 && …` itself, not the solver. `preimage::Tx::formula_at` joined it two at a time, and `Formula::and_keeping` of the prefix and the next limit flattened the prefix (`into_parts`, a copy, since the memo holds the prefix), cloned each constraint's terms and tightened the whole prefix again, O(j) at the j-th limit, so O(k²) time, and the memo held every prefix, O(k²) bytes; a chain of `||` likewise through `or_keeping` and `complementary`. Profiled (perf) at d = 1, k = 1920: `formula_at`, `and_keeping`, and the `BTreeMap` of terms and comparison. At d = 1, k = 240 → 1920 took 2.31 → 153.8 G instructions (66 times), 18.3 GB at 1920. Now `Tx::chain` reads the whole chain, each inner `&&` with no constant value read through on an explicit stack, into one `presburger::Joining`, which `and_keeping` and `or_keeping` also use; `Joining::close` gives an inner operand that has one part the treatment `finish` and the operand after it gave it, so the formula built is the one joined two at a time, node for node. O(k log k). After, at d = 1, k = 960 → 7680: 3.71 → 38.0 G side by side (10.2 times), 4.71 → 40.2 G nested (8.5 times); at d = 30, k = 960: 6.56 G nested (was 39.2), 30.5 G side by side (was 63.2; the rest grows with d k, (3) above, ST-formula-sibling). Tests `named_test::arms_of_a_named_conjunction_of_k_limits_take_time_near_linear_in_k`, `arms_of_a_named_disjunction_of_k_limits_…`, `arms_of_k_named_exemptions_…` and `arms_of_a_tier_needing_k_named_limits_…` (d = 1, k = 120 → 960, 7.5 to 10.4 times the instructions, 7.2 to 8.7 times the bytes), and `store::presburger::tests::a_chain_joined_in_one_pass_is_built_as_joined_two_at_a_time` (40,000 random chains of `&&` and `||` over constraints, `true`, `false`, negations and conjunctions and disjunctions of one to three parts, kept or not, against the old `and_keeping` and `or_keeping` joined two at a time, compared as built trees). Controls, not committed: on origin/main 64781908 the four scaling tests fail at 27.7, 25.3, 38.8 and 36.5 times the bytes (limit 24); with `close` doing nothing the unit test fails at round 15. Verify 1 (ST-formula-k2): `store::preimage::tests::the_translator_joins_a_chain_as_it_joined_it_two_at_a_time` runs the real translator on 4,000 random programs (nested and mixed `&&` and `||`, names of tests, `let`, `if` arms that share terms, calls of lambdas and of `&&` and `||` as functions, recursion by self-application) once as now and once with the arm of 64781908 and the old `and_keeping` and `or_keeping` (a test-only switch, `presburger::TWO_AT_A_TIME`), and finds the same formula as a built tree, the same definitions, cut, stops and preimages; the memos differ only by the entries the old code kept for inner `&&` and `||` nodes. Controls, not committed: with `close` doing nothing, or with no operand kept, it fails at once. A chain is a tree: an operand is reached from its chain alone, and a part used from several places is reached through a shared slot, which is memoized; test `l01_25_preimage_catalog::scaling::long_shared_chains` (chains read twice through `if`, k = 256 → 2048, 7.9 times the instructions, 8.0 times the bytes; control, not committed: with no memo for `&&`, `||` and `if` it does not finish in 60 s). The conjunction and disjunction tests now run at k = 240 → 1920 (8.3 and 7.7 times the instructions, 7.6 times the bytes), where 64781908 fails at 39.8 and 35.7 times the bytes (limit 24), for a wider margin than 25.3. ST-formula-set (2026-11-05; the choice of compare is Q55 in `wt/zed0-review/pass2/QUESTIONS.md`, unanswered, so no compare changed): the two prototypes were rerun on this machine (load 50 to 70, so times are rough) and not committed. `setform.rs` (plain set form of each run, and a set form shared through hash-consed treaps): on `shifted_chains` unequal at the last part, today's `cmp` takes 10.5, 72, 267, 1,029 and 6,584 ms at d = 30 to 480, and a hash of both sides 0.06 to 1.26 ms, the gain of a hash check before the parse on `==` (answer A); on `staircase`, the plain set form grows as d² (0.5 to 1,709 ms) and the treap reaches 56 ms at d = 1,920; on pairs of k shared sets, k = 16 to 256, today 0.27 to 151 ms, the plain set form 3.4 to 20,102 ms and the treap 1.9 to 7,492 ms; the treap agrees with the plain set form on 320,400 random pairs. `acform.rs` (an AC hash, a set at the top run, a full set form): AC and top-run near linear at d = 240 to 7,680 on every family (for example `staircase_regrouped` 2.2 to 98 ms); the full set form 22.5 to 1,637 ms on `staircase` at d = 240 to 1,920 and 3.4 to 2,863 ms on pairs at k = 16 to 128. On 179,700 random pairs, no form calls two formulas equal that differ at a point; today 514 equal, AC 657, top-run 662, full set 662 (`a && b` against `b && a` equal under all three new forms; `a && a` against `a` under top-run and full; `(a && a) \|\| c` against `a \|\| c` under full alone). With the fixed prime 18446744073709551557 a crafted pair is called equal by AC and top-run and is not equal; with a prime chosen from the input (0.012 ms) neither is. Today's `cmp` on `staircase` against `staircase_regrouped`, equal, takes 5.4 to 1,426 ms at d = 15 to 240, 43 times from 30 to 240, power 2 as accepted. New tests, which hold under every answer of Q55 (they assert only `==` and that `cmp` is antisymmetric): builders `staircase`, `random_dag` and `pairs`, with a last top that differs; `tops_sharing_parts_compare_as_written_out` (d up to 9, k up to 6, equality and hash against the formula written out; the order of an unequal pair is not asserted, since it depends on the answer); `tops_sharing_parts_compare_in_time_near_quadratic_in_the_depth` (d = 30 → 240, `scaling::check_order` of power 2, limit 192; 15 s); `pairs_of_shared_sets_compare_in_time_near_linear_in_the_parts` (k = 32 → 256, n = O(k²), power 2 in k, limit 192; 3.6 s); `a_deep_chain_of_single_parts_compares_without_running_out_of_stack` (200,000 levels). Control, not committed: at power 1 (limit 24) the two scaling tests fail at 67.7 times the bytes (105 MB → 7.13 GB) and 63.9 times the bytes (9.9 MB → 631 MB).
 Its family is checked against the formula written out (`named_limits_and_exemptions_at_each_tier_narrow_as_if_written_out`); no scaling test is committed for it, since it fails. ST-formula-k2-rec: under recursion the memo kept nothing. `Tx::cached` stored a result only where its cut was at or above the stack height at which it was made, so where a call's argument stopped at a call of a function already being read, every chain, `let` and `if` in the body was read again at each place it was reached: a chain of k tests read at r places cost O(r k), and `if` arms that share one term, nested d deep, cost 2^d (relations 1.8, 5.2, 20.6, 82.9 ms at d = 8, 10, 12, 14, the lowest of the runs). Now each memo entry holds its cut (`preimage::Entry`). An entry made with its cut below the stack height is kept while the stack stays at least that high (`Memo::held`), a hit on it passes its cut to the caller as a new reading would, and `Tx::through` drops such entries when it pops below the height at which they were made. Tests `l01_25_preimage_catalog::scaling::a_chain_read_at_many_places_under_recursion_is_translated` (the relation at four sizes), `long_chains_read_at_many_places_under_recursion` (r, k and both 64 → 512; both: 5.51 M → 43.4 M instructions, 7.9 times, 1.28 → 10.15 MB, 8.0 times; on d00a1346 16.8 M → 723.3 M, 43 times, and 8.1 → 461.3 MB, 56.8 times) and `long_shared_arms_under_recursion` (d = 32 → 256: relations 7.5 and 7.7 times, preimage 6.7 and 6.5 times). Control, not committed: with the `preimage.rs` of d00a1346 the first fails at 56.8 times the bytes and the second does not finish in 60 s; the relation test passes there too, so the results are unchanged. Verify 1 (ST-formula-k2-rec): an entry held below a stop depends on nothing that changes before it is dropped. Its key fixes its term and scope, and so the names of the parameters it can read, and each such name belongs to one summary key and so to one interval, so a read under other inputs differs only in names it cannot read; entries made within a call are dropped when that call returns, so none leaves it; and while an entry is held, the lambdas below the height at which it was made are still read at the same places. Test `store::preimage::tests::entries_made_below_a_stop_give_what_reading_the_term_again_gives` (1,000 random programs of functions that call themselves and each other, nested, with `let` parts read again within calls whose arguments read the inputs) finds the same translation and preimages as the memo before e75ff904; at each of 13,090 reads of a held entry (9,284 on a higher stack, 6,232 under other inputs) it checks that the lambdas below it are still read at the same places; it reads 6,194 held entries and 1,058,960 others again in place and finds the same value and cut; and it checks each preimage against the interpreter at 152,973 points. Five hand-written cases are in `store::preimage::tests::written`. Controls, not committed: with held entries never dropped, or dropped one level late, the random test and `an_entry_made_within_one_lambda_is_not_read_within_another` fail; with a hit that does not pass on its cut, the random test and `a_hit_passes_its_cut_to_the_reader` fail; with every entry stored as holding everywhere, all six fail. No defect found. The differential test now counts the log entries of inner chain nodes apart, since such an entry may be held and then dropped. Tradeoff comments at `Formula::and_keeping`, `Formula::or_keeping`, `Parts::of`, `deep::Flat` and `Formula::cmp` |

| S32 | `presburger::canonical` (CANON-stable): the variable order the solver takes came from `usize::BITS - atoms.len().leading_zeros() + 1` rounds of hashed color refinement, a count of rounds that depends on the size; a pipeline of n stages needs about n/2 rounds to separate, so at 64 stages it stopped at 17 colors and at 512 at 23, and the hashed `int_key` makes coefficient k and -k-1 collide (2 and -3 merged in a random system) | O((n+m) log n) per round count but wrong; refining until stable with hashed rounds is O(n²) on the pipeline (125 → 1000 stages: 32.2M → 2526.9M instructions, 78.5 times) | fixed: `stable_colors` builds the graph of variables, rows and terms (rows colored by relation and constant, terms by coefficient, colors numbered by sorting, no hash) and `Refine` runs Berkholz, Bonsma and Grohe's canonical color refinement (Theory Comput. Syst. 60, 2017, section 3, Algorithms 1-3: a stack of refining colors, new colors numbered by degree, the largest new class skipped by Hopcroft's rule) to the coarsest stable partition, O((n+m+t) log(n+m+t)). Tests in `tests/steps/l01_38_presburger/canonical.rs`: pipeline at 8, 64, 512 fully separated; rings and a shared budget stay one class; 40 random 5-variable systems under all 120 namings with the rows reversed; 300 random systems against a plain refinement oracle; Codex's 6-variable system from XR3 with the box -3..=3, whose coefficients 1 and -2 the old `int_key` merged, gets 6 colors under all 720 namings (control with k and -k-1 merged: that test and the oracle test fail); corner cases; `refinement_scales` on a pipeline and on a random sparse system with 2n rows (numbers below). The solver takes the variables and the rows breadth first over the rows in the order of their colors (`canonical`), and the row hash `row_key` is gone: sorted by color alone, the order broke three tests (the `6x + 10y + 15z == 7` chain 333 times the work from 64 to 512, a 1024-step model past 128 bits, and the `mod7` refusals of S22). Measured after: pipeline 2000/16000/128000 32.1M → 270.4M → 2274.4M instructions (8.42, 8.41 times), random sparse 67.3M → 585.4M → 5113.4M (8.70, 8.73 times). Control on main 1ab9e33 (old rounds): 3 of 6 tests fail. Control with hashed rounds run until stable: `refinement_scales` does not end in 120 s |
| S33 | `interval::End`, `affine::Bounds::offset`, `presburger::hull_from` (TD-ends): ends of any size, `End::Fin(Num)`, one machine word while the value fits in `i64` and a big integer past it; a value outside `i128` built from a power is kept as `(c_1 * core_1 + ... + c_n * core_n + d) / den` (`store::int::Kept`), where each core is a product of powers of integers and of floor roots `floor(r^(1/k))` of kept values `r` with integer cores; each integer base is reduced to its least root (`least_root`: 9 ** e is kept as 3 ** (2e)); the terms are collected (equal cores by form, else over a coprime base of the integers on which they differ, `Core::same`) and the powers of one integer folded (`fold`: each lower coefficient brought into `(-b^g / 2, b^g / 2]`, so that a group is zero exactly when no term is left); compared by one pass at 64 bits, by a root against the power it came from, else by the sign of the kept difference at doubling precision; the approximation of a root is kept with its atom and shared by its copies | O(1) per operation on one-word ends; O(digits) past `i64`, O(digits²) for a product; `hull_from` O(log d) questions for a bound at distance d; a kept value: built in O(bits of e) word operations; sums, differences and products of kept values and of a kept value with any value, negation, powers (by squaring, up to `C(n + k, k)` terms for n terms, the size of the result), `div` and `mod` by a value that is not kept (a modular power of each core modulo `den * n`), a gcd with a value that is not kept, the floor and ceiling root of a power (exact when the index divides every exponent, else a kept root), the floor root of a bare root and of any kept value with integer cores are all kept, at a cost polynomial in the number of terms and the bits of the coefficients, bases and exponents: O(n log n) core comparisons to collect n terms, a power of each base of at most about twice the bits of its coefficient to fold; compared in O(bits of e) products of 64-bit numbers when the values differ in their leading 64 bits (more bits, doubling, only as the values come closer; at most the bits of the larger); hashed in O(1), by value inside `i128` and by the sign outside it (consistent with equality: a value inside `i128` has one form, and equal values outside it have one sign; `l01_24_refinement_lattices::boundary` checks every operation that lands at the ends of `i64` and `i128`, KEPT-neg-hash); the least root of a base of n bits costs one integer square root and, per odd prime p below n, a 2-adic p-th root of n / p bits with a check of its bit count and of three residues, O(M(n) log n) for M(n) the cost of a product of n bits, plus one p-th power for each index that passes those checks (each root found; a non-power passes only if its candidate agrees in bit count, in its low n / p bits and modulo three primes near 2^64); comparing two cores that differ in form costs a coprime base of the m integers on which they differ, O(m²) gcds of their bits, each replacement taking every power of the shared factor out at once (O(log k) divisions and squares for multiplicity k), nothing for the atoms they share | measured, linear: `l01_24_refinement_lattices::scaling::ends_in_one_word_scale_linearly` (2.20M to 13.5M instructions from n = 200 to 1600), `ends_of_2_to_the_200_scale_linearly` (10.9M to 82.3M) and `kept_ends_scale_linearly` (ends a little past 2^(10^8): 254M to 2048M, 8.1 times); all arithmetic through the big integer costs 1.35 to 1.46 times as much on one-word ends (negative control, not committed); kept powers: `scaling::power_bounds_scale_with_the_bits_of_the_exponent` (x ** k for k = 10^6 and 8 * 10^6: 94863 to 101568 instructions, 1.1 times, limit 3), `scaling::kept_arithmetic_scales_with_the_bits_of_the_exponent` (x ** k + 1, k * x ** k - 1, the cube root of 2 ** k, 3 ** k + 5 ** k, 3 ** (k + 1) - 3 ** k and (2 ** k + 1) ** 3 for k = 10^6 + 1 and 8 times it: 8.69M to 9.59M instructions, 1.1 times, limit 3), `scaling::equal_cores_scale_with_the_bits_of_the_exponent` (`3 ** e * 9 ** (e + 1) - 3 ** (3e + 2)` and `6 ** e * 5 ** (e + 1) - 2 ** e * 3 ** e * 5 ** (e + 1)` for e = 10^6 and 8 * 10^6: 736527 to 826527 instructions, 1.1 times, limit 3; `ends::equal_cores_over_bases_that_share_factors_cancel_without_their_digits` under 1 s at both, 30.6 s at e = 10^6 before repair 4; negative control with least roots and the coprime base off, not committed: past 60 s), `scaling::the_cube_of_a_wide_end_scales_with_its_bits` (the cube of 2^n + 3 and the image of x ** 3 up to it, n = 4096 and 32768 bits; the cube took 1.4, 2.2, 4.4, 9.7 and 89 ms at n = 4096, 8192, 16384, 32768 and 262144, against 69 s at 32768 with repair 4's least root, which tried an integer root of all n bits per prime index; negative control with that least root, not committed: 440M to 361G instructions, 820 times, limit 24), `scaling::equality_over_a_wide_base_scales_with_its_bits` (`(2^n * 3) ** 1000` against `2 ** (1000 n) * 3 ** 1000`, n = 65536 and 524288 (both past the size at which num-bigint divides by the method of Burnikel and Ziegler: 9.83M to 102M instructions, 10.4 times, and 4.46 MB to 76.8 MB allocated, 17.2 times, where 8192 to 65536 gives 26.3 times the bytes from that change of method; the bytes grow as about n log² n, from the O(log k) divisions of n bits each); 0.10 and 0.15 ms at n = 1024 and 8192, against 8 and 135 ms with one factor divided out per step; negative control with that coprime base and those divisions, not committed: 116M to 4.0G instructions, 34.5 times at 8192 and 65536; at 65536 and 524288, 1.07 GB to 68.7 GB allocated, 63.9 times, 103 s), `ends::a_wide_end_is_kept_as_a_power_of_its_least_root`, `ends::equal_values_in_different_forms_hash_alike`, `ends::the_bound_of_a_large_power_is_built_and_compared_without_its_digits`, `ends::arithmetic_and_roots_on_a_large_power_keep_its_form` and `ends::sums_of_unrelated_large_powers_keep_their_form` (k = 10^8 under 1 s), `l01_25_preimage_catalog::kept_ends` (the preimages of `x ** 100000000 + 1 >= 50` and `x ** 3 <= 2 ** 100000000` under 1 s; before repair 2, 17 s and past 60 s); `zedc test` of `x ** (3 ** 100000000 + 5 ** 100000000)` 0.01 s and of `x ** (3 ** 100000001 - 3 ** 100000000)` 0.03 s (before repair 3, past 60 s and past 30 s); negative controls with values written out, not committed: powers, 983387946 to 20434712863 instructions (20.8 times) and k = 10^8 past 60 s; the sum of a kept value and a scalar, 3.56G to 96.5G instructions (27.1 times), k = 10^8 past 60 s, and the preimage of `x ** 100000000 + 1 >= 50` 15.3 s; sums of two kept values with different first cores, the kept scaling test and the k = 10^8 sums test past 60 s. Open, each written out at the cost of the digits, each with a `// Complexity tradeoff:` comment in `store/int.rs` and tested in `ends::kept_values_with_no_kept_form_are_written_out` or `ends::kept_forms_compute_as_their_values_written_out`: printing a kept value (`num-bigint` radix conversion); equal cores holding roots of different radicands with one floor (the floor cube roots of `2 ** (3e + 1)` and `2 ** (3e + 1) + 1`), found equal only at the bits of the value; sums of products over several integers that carry into one another (`2 ** (e + 1) * 5 ** e + 1 - 2 * 10 ** e`: equal cores collect, but folding by base does not carry between cores of different integers); a quotient, remainder or gcd of a kept value by a kept value, and of a value holding a kept root; a root of a value holding a root other than the floor root of a bare root; deciding that a kept value that is not a bare power is a perfect power when it is one (for its ceiling root). Comparing two values that agree in their leading bits costs the bits on which they agree, and for a root `O(bits)` products at that precision (bisection in `root_approx`), so `5^(k/2)` against the square root of `3^k + 5^k` at k = 10^8 does not finish. A hash table keyed by many values outside `i128` of one sign puts them in one bucket. Since the rebase onto main (66945e0): the piecewise-affine summary of S22a (`preimage::in_range`, `in_i64` until S22-WIDE) is given up when a domain, an arm's set or a demand has a finite end past `i128` (past `i64` until S22-WIDE), and the `if` is read arm by arm, exact; `kept_ends::an_if_chain_over_ends_past_i64_is_read_arm_by_arm` (ends past `i64`, now summarized), `wide_ends::ends_past_i128_take_the_transfer_before`. A kernel integer held as prime powers (CAP-bits `Rat`) becomes a kept `Num` power, with no digits written. Bit words on kept ends (BIT-kept-ends): `Num::bits` of a kept value from its approximation at 64 bits and then twice as many until both bounds have one bit count, and for bounds that straddle a power of 2 (as for `2 ** k - 1`) one kept comparison with that power, so `b ** e` costs O(bits of e) products at the precision where `e * log2(b)` is decided; `Num::trailing_zeros` from the residues of the odd parts of the terms modulo `2^j`, j = 64 and doubling while they cancel (`b ** e`: `e` times the trailing zeros of `b`, at j = 64); `Num::div_floor` by a kept power of 2 (`shr` by 127 or more) divides the terms that have it as a factor in their form (`Kept::shr`); `Num::pow2` keeps a large power kept. `l01_39a_bit_words::transfer::the_transfer_is_sound_on_random_interval_pairs` 2.2 s (past 60 s before, from `bits` of `2 ** w - 1` with w near `i64::MAX`); `l01_39a_bit_words::kept_ends::bits_and_trailing_zeros_of_kept_ends_do_not_grow_with_the_exponent` (bits, trailing zeros and `shr` of 8 kept ends and the `leadingZeros` and `trailingZeros` rules at width k, k = 2^20 and 2^23: 1.54M to 1.77M instructions, 1.15 times, 38.8 KB to 44.0 KB, limit 3; negative control with `bits` and `trailing_zeros` writing the value out, not committed: 2.19G to 46.0G instructions, 21.0 times, and 22.9 times the bytes); `kept_ends::bits_and_trailing_zeros_of_kept_ends_are_those_written_out` (powers of 2 and their neighbors near the ends of `i128` and past them, `3 ** e`, `12 ** e`, sums, a quotient by 2, and their negations). Open, written out, each with a `// Complexity tradeoff:` comment: the trailing zeros of a value holding a kept root (`Num::trailing_zeros`); `div_floor` by a power of 2 of the terms that do not have it as a factor (`Num::div_floor`, so `shr(3 ** k, j)`); the exponent of `pow2` when it is itself kept (`Num::pow2`); a bit word whose operands are all single values, given to the kernel as values (`interval::bit_word`) |

| NAME-ties | `presburger::solve` (XR3 P3, fix B): the next elimination took the least `(fill, color, index)`, so among variables of one color and least fill the index, which comes from the names, chose; the five-variable system of XR3 (claude5) gave `false` under 96 namings and Growth under 24, Codex's six-variable system true or Growth, and seed 103 of the regular family one system whose answer depended on the names | O(1) per step, but the answer depends on the names | kept as a refusal: a step takes the unique least `(fill, color)`; when several variables tie there and two of them share a row, the question is refused with `Refusal::Tie`, whose message names the tied variables and the rows they share ("variables that no structure tells apart would be eliminated in an order chosen by name"); tied variables that share no row are eliminated together, which no order changes (proof at `System::least`). Equalities are solved in an order given by structure: written by the latest substitution first, then fewest variables, then degree, then shape (`EqKey`); tied equalities are solved together when no two share a variable or when each defines its own variable, of unit coefficient, that no other holds (proof at `Equalities::least`; the substitutions of one batch share one stamp), and otherwise kept as pairs of opposite rows (`loosen`). Kept as rows, the three equalities of `i mod 7` read three times gave Shadows, and the transfer before of S22 refused `mod7` at k = 3 to 8 (`s22::chains_of_slow_families_keep_their_preimages` failed, 7 boxes made exact for 1); test `names::tied_equalities_that_share_only_a_variable_they_do_not_define_are_solved_together`, control with the rule off: Shadows for `true`. The splinter search reads the dark eliminations a batch at a time and ends at the first batch with a refusal (tradeoff comment at `search`), so ending is the same under every naming. Tradeoff comments at `run` (the tie refusal) and `search`. A complete solution would search every order of the tied variables, exponential in their number, or label the rows canonically, with no known polynomial algorithm. Tests in `tests/steps/l01_38_presburger/names.rs`: the regular family, seeds 101–108, one answer under every naming (seed 103 fails on the old rank); claude5 Tie under all 120 namings, codex6 `true` under all 720, coupled4 `true` under all 24, Frucht's edge system, the orientation of joined equalities, the splinter batches (all 720 namings of a 5-stage Pugh chain, 200 of an 11-stage one, all Shadows); a tie that shares a row is refused with the full message; batch and single-file checking agree. Losses against the base 129accb, system by system: five-unit family 1 of 1,591 decided (`true` became Tie; XR3 bound 106 of 1,547), `0x38_0a` 0 of 264, `0x38_3b` 0 (tally unchanged, no ties). Scaling (`decide_scales_over_copies_of_*`, bytes from 16 to 128 copies, limit 24): copies of a tie, of codex6 and of tied equalities pass; control with the measured work made quadratic fails at 64.4 times. Timings, this branch against the base at load 32–38: the 3-term equality chain 64/128/256/512 at 7.2/15.5/59/162 ms against 4.5/11/32/99 ms; the Pugh chain 64/128/256/512/1024 at 4.1/8.2/23.7/45/53 ms against 1.6/2.6/5.1/11/24 ms. Searching every splinter of every batch made the Pugh chain quadratic (511 Omega runs at 512 stages); the batch search keeps it linear. Controls at f129ec7, not committed: claude5, codex6, coupled4 and seed 103 fail; Frucht, the orientation test and the splinter test pass there too |
| NAME-ties-or | `presburger::satisfiable`, `decide`, `project_each` on a formula with a disjunction or a negation (NAME-ties repair 1, after the verifier's probe): the search through the disjunctions (`pick`, `probe`, `project_parts`) takes in each disjunction the first disjunct that serves, in the order `nnf` sorts them, which compares atoms by name, and at points from `solve`, so 7 of 4000 random 3-variable formulas with disjunctions (verifier) and 10 of 4000 (seed `0x5eed`, this repair) gave answers that depend on the names (system 202: `Disjuncts` under 2 namings, `true` under 4; system 797: `Growth` under 2) | O(1) per choice, but the answer depends on the names | fixed, with a refusal where two atoms share a color (repair 2): `Renamed::of` colors every atom by `stable_colors` over all the formula's constraints read as one system, with a row `p + x + y == 0` joining each product to its factors; when each atom that is not a product has its own color, the atom of color rank `k` is renamed `#k` and products keep their factors renamed, so every naming gives the one formula; models, projections and refusal messages are renamed back (`back`, `back_text`). After: 0 of 4000 random formulas (tests `every_naming_of_a_formula_with_disjunctions_gives_one_answer_0_to_2000` and `_2000_to_4000`). Repair 2, revised in repair 3: when two atoms share a color (`Naming::Tied`) the formula is no longer searched as written. `decide` and `satisfiable` settle it by `settle_as_sets` (each round tests every disjunct against the base as the round began, and adds the disjuncts left alone after the round); a formula with disjunctions left is then answered `true` when no two of them are linked through rows of the base or each other's disjuncts (`alone`, union and find over the atoms: it takes in each disjunction a disjunct that settling showed to have a point with the base, and points of separate groups combine), or else tested once with the disjuncts their colors choose (`by_color`: in each disjunction the disjunct with the least sorted list of keys (comparison, constant, sorted (color, coefficient) of each term), none on a tie), and refused with `Refusal::Unordered` when that gives no point; the message names the tied atoms and the disjunctions left. `project_each` projects it by `project_parts` with `sets`: no `pick` or `probe` first, each disjunct settled before any is asked, those that settling shows empty skipped, every other one asked for the symbols all of them touch, and hulls tightened by `decide`. After: 0 of the 1,000 regular systems with disjunctions depend on the names (`disjunctions_whose_atoms_share_a_color`). Loss, under one naming: the regular family with disjunctions decides 682 of 1,000 (311 `true`, 371 `false`) against 814 searched as written (440, 374), and refuses 256 as Unordered. Other losses, in `a_formula_whose_atoms_share_a_color_is_settled_or_refused`: every such formula with no point that settling does not show empty is refused (three tasks in two slots, four in three); two shifts in `[0, 9]`, each `<= 2` or `>= 7`, with `x + y <= 10` are refused, since the colors choose both late, though both early is a point; in `deep_condition::a_branch_with_no_point_does_not_widen_the_other` the tied form (`then_arm(1, 0)`) keeps the scope, the narrowing a search would show is lost. The repair 2 probe (`probe_parts`, `probe_as_sets`: each disjunct tested with every other disjunction) was removed in repair 3: it decided 631 of the 1,000 and was cubic on a star of tied leaves with a disjunction each (8/16/32/64 leaves 22 ms/109 ms/1.6 s/10.8 s, then refused). The conjunction families are unchanged (a conjunction takes neither path: `0x38_0a` and `0x38_3b` keep their tallies, and the five-unit family, all conjunctions, is unchanged by construction). Tradeoff comments at `Renamed` and `settle_tied`. Cost, measured (load 37 to 47): on `star_with_choices` (two hubs, leaves tied, each leaf in a disjunction whose two disjuncts differ by color) `decide` at 16/32/64/128 leaves 4.6 ms/12.3 ms/33.5 ms/198 ms and `project_each` 0.30/0.96/7.9/20.1 s; with the leaves told apart (renamed and searched) 4.6/13.8/81.5/196 ms and 0.37/1.46/12.6/23.8 s; on the base 129accb, which broke ties by index, 9.5/10.8/41/202 ms and 0.40/1.25/6.9/24.6 s. So the tied path costs what the searched path costs, and both are quadratic: settling tests each disjunct that the base's point misses against the whole group of rows linked to it, here every row through the hubs (`settle`, `settle_as_sets`), and the projection settles each disjunct with that group. Open, not caused here, owner ledger DECIDE-disj-chain. Tests at power 2: `decide_on_tied_disjunctions_scales` and `satisfiable_on_tied_disjunctions_scales` (16 → 128 leaves), `project_each_on_tied_disjunctions_scales` (4 → 32); at power 1: `decide_on_separate_tied_disjunctions_scales` (64 → 512 separate tied pairs, the `alone` path). Controls, not committed: the repair 2 probe fails `decide` and `satisfiable` (Unordered) and `project_each` at 201.9 times the bytes (limit 192); one extra settling per disjunction in `settle_tied` fails `satisfiable` at 420.0 times (limit 192) and `decide` did not finish in 120 s; a clone of the base per disjunction in `alone` fails at 54.9 times (limit 24). `decide_scales_over_one_system_with_many_tied_variables` (one connected star of tied leaves sharing no row, 64 → 512, power 1) passes. Local suite: `l01_38_presburger::omega` (the pigeonhole case tells its tasks apart by a row of its own, and the tied form is refused as Unordered), `l01_38_presburger::random` (one entailment of 60, from `x != y` in the window to `-x == y - 1`, is refused as Unordered; it was answered), `checker::a_decided_question_over_four_names_is_accepted` unchanged, and the S22 random chains, whose boxes are checked against the interpreter: `random_guarded_chains_keep_their_preimages` 55 boxes exact (39 before NAME-ties), `more_random_guarded_chains_keep_their_preimages` 70 exact, 2 narrower, 4 changed (58, 1, 4 after repair 2). Time of `more_random_guarded_chains_keep_their_preimages`: 12.5 to 29.4 s at load 26 to 38 (24 to 38 s with the probe; 8 to 9 s on the base); the names group 30.3 s in the suite (37.7 s with the probe). Scaling: `decide_scales_over_a_renamed_chain_with_disjunctions`, a chain with one disjunction, 64 → 512 variables 2.0 M → 16.1 MB (8.0 times, limit 24); control with a quadratic allocation in `Renamed::of`, not committed: 45.9 times, fails. Found, not caused here, open: a chain with a disjunction at every fourth stage is quadratic in the bytes of `decide`, 64 → 512 variables 8.1 M → 445 MB (55.0 times) with the renaming off and 52.9 times with it on. Repair 4 (verifier's probe): `settle_as_sets` kept a disjunct whose `solve` was refused, and `alone` and the `!want` answer in `settle_tied` took every kept disjunct as one with a point, so `decide((C4 && x <= 4) \|\| (C4 && x <= 3))`, `C4` a cycle of four tied variables `x_(i+1) * 3 - x_i >= 10` with no point, gave `true`, and with a disjunct `1 <= y <= 2` beside `C4 && x <= 3` the answer of `satisfiable` depended on whether `y` sorted before the cycle. Now `settle_as_sets` returns `AsSets`, the parts with a flag per kept disjunct, set only when `solve` found a point or the constraints hold at a point of the base (the last round adds nothing to the base, so the flags hold for the base returned), and `alone` takes in each disjunction the first flagged disjunct with no disjunction of its own, or gives `None`; a refused disjunct is never taken. Tests `a_disjunct_the_solver_refuses_is_not_taken_for_a_point` and `a_refused_disjunct_beside_one_with_a_point_gives_one_answer`, red before; control without the flag check, not committed: both fail. Cost unchanged: one flag per disjunct tested, read once by `alone`. Also found in repair 4: the renamed atoms `#k` sorted as text (`#10` before `#2`), so `nnf` gave `pick` the disjuncts of a chain out of rank order, `pick` failed (`Disjuncts`, 511 disjunctions at k = 256) and `probe` ran, quadratic: `s22::chain_cross_live_scales` 34.9 times the bytes from 32 to 256 (limit 24) where main passes. Now `k` is written with leading zeros to one width; the counts of `solve` calls then match main (96,982 against 96,961 at 256) and the test passes. S22 random chains after both: 97 and 114 boxes exact in the two halves of 4000 (79 and 99 on main), no box wider than the transfer before (3 on main), one inexact box narrower (cases 3250 to 3500) |
