## Kernel (`crates/zed-kernel`)

| ID | Where | Class | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| K1 | `types.rs` `Dnf::not` (De Morgan by full product); also reached from `Display for Type` | 2^C | fixed: the tuple and morphism parts are reduced ordered decision diagrams with shared nodes (`types/bdd.rs`), so a complement is linear in the nodes; a union or intersection whose diagram would have more nodes than its two operands keeps them as an expression, built in constant time, and emptiness walks its paths exactly, expanding first on a subexpression two operands share and passing over paths whose taken atoms are already empty (docs/thresholds.md 9, which removed the 4,096 and 65,536 budgets; Kept). `l01_23_subtyping::scaling::the_parts_scale` (union of C differences `m(i) - m(i+1000)`, whose diagram has 2^C nodes: build about 48 ms at every C from 16 to 128 and complement about 1 µs; old DNF 18 s at C = 12). Since replacement 9 both queries are decided and asserted: `b <: b \| a` 4 µs at every C from 4 to 14 (87 ms at 14, ×4 per +2 C, without the shared expansion), `!b & a` on tuples 1.7 ms at 14 (181 ms without pruning). The walk is checked against evaluation of all 64 assignments on 3,000 random parts with shared operands by `types::bdd::tests::the_paths_of_a_part_are_its_assignments` (catches a shared expansion that pairs a cofactor with the wrong side), and against the value oracle by `l01_23_subtyping::oracle::deep_combinations_are_decided_exactly` |
| K2 | `types/empty.rs` `Search::product`: no pruning of disjoint negatives | n^k | fixed: the product is split outside each negated type in turn into disjoint pieces (outside it first in component `j`, inside it before `j`), and a negated type disjoint from a piece is passed over, with no cap on the splits since docs/thresholds.md 9 (Kept). A union or intersection of many types is joined in pairs (`Type::union_all`, `Type::meet_all`, used in `rec.rs`, `store/product.rs` and `zir/text.rs`), since one at a time `k` tuple types, each ordered after the last, rebuild the diagram `k` times. `l01_23_subtyping::scaling::a_product_covered_by_its_cells_scales` (an `n × n` product less its `n²` cells: 4.3 s at `n = 5` before, 67 ms at 4,096 cells after; the cells built one at a time took 6 s at 1,024 cells, joined in pairs 45 ms), `negated_types_disjoint_from_the_product_are_passed_over` (13 decoys that would make `2^13` pieces, decided both ways), `passing_over_disjoint_negated_types_scales` (2 against 16 decoys over a tuple of bits, `O(k²)` allowed; without passing over, ratio 1,540 to 2,342 against 192) and `a_cell_left_out_is_found`. Mutants caught: no passing over (decoy scaling test), cut answers kept (a memo test, removed with the budget), overlapping pieces (grid tests), a wrong cover test (grid, oracle, `rec`), joining one at a time (grid). A check for a negated type containing the piece was dropped: with disjoint pieces it gives an empty component in every piece, and no test told the two apart |
| K3 | `Dnf::normal`: all pairs, restarted after each merge; unions folded pairwise | O(C³) | fixed with K1: the clause lists are gone; a diagram is canonical with no merging step |
| K4 | `print.rs` `Doc::render` calls `flat` at every node | O(n³) | fixed: the one-line text is written into one buffer; `render` asks `fits(room)`, which stops once the room runs out, so each node visited reads at most a line's width; a call's head is a doc written on one line, not a copy of its text. Output identical to the old on 3,000 random terms and the nested shapes at six widths (26.5 MB). `l01_15_kernel_terms::scaling::the_printer_scales_with_depth` (old: ratio 84.5 for broken nested lambdas, 1.5 s at depth 4,000; head copy alone: ratio 57.7; new: 8 to 12). Broken calls nested in argument position have text quadratic in depth, from their indentation, and are measured by its length |
| K5 | `eval.rs` definedness `fix` in rounds: domain and map copied per attempt | O(D³) | fixed: every attempt shares one domain and one map, each entry stamped with the round that defined it; a Jacobi attempt sees the entries of earlier rounds, a chaotic or pull attempt sees all. `m0_10_finite_model_harness::scaling::the_definedness_fix_scales` (old: ratio 61 to 63 for two rounds over 1,000 against 8,000 keys; new: 8 to 9); `a_jacobi_round_reads_the_family_as_the_round_began` catches a round that sees its own entries. A chain of `D` entries takes `D` Jacobi rounds; since K7 an entry postponed on an undefined key waits on that key and is tried again only once it is defined, so the chain takes `D` attempts under every schedule (old: `D²/2` in rounds, ratio 79 for 1,000 against 8,000 keys) |
| K6 | values deep-copied on every variable, literal and read (`Family`, `Tuple`, `Path` own their contents) | O(n²) | fixed: a family's entries sit behind an `Arc`, so copying a family is O(1). Tuples and keypaths stay owned vectors: their length is fixed by the term that builds them (a tuple's arity, a keypath's depth), not by the data, and a family inside one is shared. `m0_09_kernel_evaluator::scaling::reading_a_family_once_per_entry_scales` (old: ratio 63 to 70 through a literal, a variable and a tuple, 2,000 against 16,000 entries; new: 8 to 10) |
| K7 | family update and `Prim::Union` rebuild the whole family | O(n²) per loop | fixed: a family's entries sit in a persistent ordered map (`imbl::OrdMap`), and a union inserts the smaller family's entries into the larger, recursing only where both hold a family under one key, with the same collisions and keypaths; `Family::from_leaves` keeps a family given as a leaf whole where nothing else is written beneath it, rather than writing its leaves one by one. `m0_09_kernel_evaluator::scaling::a_union_adding_one_entry_per_step_scales`, a `foldl` adding one entry per step (old: 75 s at 8,000 steps, ratio 75; with the union alone, ratio 77, since the family of the accumulators was built from all their leaves; new: 98 ms at 8,000); `the_union_agrees_with_the_union_of_the_leaves` and `a_family_built_with_families_as_leaves_agrees_with_its_leaves` compare 20,000 random cases each with building from the leaves, collisions included |
| K8 | environment lookup is a linear walk of frames | O(n·depth) | fixed: the names in scope sit in a persistent ordered map (`imbl::OrdMap`), so binding and lookup cost O(log n) in the names in scope and a closure's copy of its environment O(1). `m0_09_kernel_evaluator::scaling::a_name_read_past_many_bindings_scales`, a chain of `n` lets each reading the first name (old: ratio 39 to 41 for 2,000 against 16,000 lets; new: 9.5). The environments of a chain stay live while its body runs and each shares all but O(log n) nodes with the last, so memory is O(n log n), 1.2 GB peak at 16,000 lets against 0.6 GB before. `an_inner_binding_hides_an_outer_one_within_its_scope_only` catches a binding that keeps the outer one |
| K9 | provenance copies call and keypath stacks per fact | O(S·depth) | fixed: the call sites and the keypath the run is inside are persistent vectors (`imbl::Vector`, re-exported as `zed_kernel::provenance::Vector`), which each fact's address shares; a push or pop costs amortized O(1) and a fact O(1) more. `l01_20_provenance::scaling::recording_a_deep_recursion_scales` (old: ratio 36 to 40 for a recursion of depth 1,000 against 8,000; new: 8.5); `the_facts_of_a_recursion_are_at_their_call_sites` catches a call that does not push its site |
| K10 | `Family::from_leaves` re-flattens built families; `discard` clones subtrees | O(L·d²) | fixed by K7: `from_leaves` keeps a family given as a leaf whole when nothing else is at or above its keypath, so each level of a discard costs its own entries. What remains is the selector's keypath, which a discard is defined to give each entry: `O(Σ depth)`. `m0_09_kernel_evaluator::scaling::a_discard_scales_with_its_entries_and_the_keypaths_it_gives` (a family of n pairs: 8.7; a caterpillar of depth 200 against 1,600 at order 2: 46.5, and 710 with the flattening `from_leaves`) |
| K11 | `types/empty.rs` `done.retain` per non-empty result | O(T²) | fixed: the types decided empty are kept in a stack in the order decided, and a non-empty result drops only those decided since its query began, each once: amortized O(1) per result. `l01_23_subtyping::scaling::many_negated_types_passed_over_scale` (a six-component product less n tuples passed over, n = 1,000 against 8,000: old 35, new 11.2; at one component the time to build the types hid the scan, 20 against 11.7 at n = 32,000) |
| K12 | `Type` compared by contents; `empty`/`top` rebuilt per call | O(s·depth) | fixed: each type, diagram node and kept expression holds a deterministic hash of its contents, made from its children's when it is made, and they are ordered by it first, so contents are read only on equal hashes; `empty` and `top` are made once per thread. The printer sorts each part's clauses as text, since the diagrams' order is now that of the hashes. `l01_23_subtyping::scaling::nested_tuples_scale` (a tuple nested 100 against 800 deep: old 76 to 89, new 11.8; the base is now 400, new 10, since a run of a millisecond read 43 under load); `a_type_built_on_another_thread_is_the_same_type` catches ordering ties by pointer |
| K13 | `Atoms::zip`, `Lits::zip` rebuild both sides | O(N² log N) in folds | fixed: literal sets and atom maps are persistent (`imbl::OrdSet`, `OrdMap`); each union, intersection and difference walks the smaller operand and changes or filters the larger, `O(s log l)`; each set keeps the wrapping sum of its elements' hashes, so a type's hash no longer reads them. `l01_23_subtyping::scaling::literals_and_atoms_joined_one_at_a_time_scale` (old 62, new 8.3 to 9.7); `literal_and_atom_sets_combine_exactly` catches a wrong rule for the larger operand |
| K14 | `define_on_demand`: `Vec::contains` on the demand stack; entries rerun from the start | O(D²), O(m²) | fixed: under `Schedule::Pull` a read of an undefined entry evaluates it where it is read, with the call sites and keypath of the `fix`, so an entry reading `m` undefined entries is evaluated once; the entries being demanded and those stalled are sets in the `fix`, so the cycle check is `O(log D)`. A read of an entry on a cycle stops the attempt, and the entry and every entry waiting on it stall, each once, so a chain of `D` entries ending in a cycle stalls in `O(D log D)` (T2-eval). `m0_10_finite_model_harness::scaling::an_entry_reading_many_undefined_entries_scales_under_demand` (entry 0 summing 1,000 against 8,000 others: old 62 to 66, new 8.8; entries reading the ones after them, new 9.9); `a_demanded_entry_has_the_facts_it_has_in_rounds` catches a demand under the reader's keypath and an undo left out |
| K15 | `fix_lattice`: two clones and a deep compare per round | O(R·n) | fixed: the clones are of shared values, `O(1)`; the compare was `Value::eq` as `cmp == Equal`, which reads two families in key order in full. Families, tuples and keypaths are now equal by their parts, and two families by `OrdMap`'s test, which compares sizes first and, by imbl's documentation, skips the nodes two maps share. `m0_10_finite_model_harness::scaling::a_lattice_ascent_by_one_key_a_round_scales` (1,000 against 8,000 rounds: old 40 to 44, new 8.2). A step of the whole iterate (`x ∪ {c}` joined to `x`) is an `O(n)` union per round by its own definition, and stays `O(R·n)` |
| K16 | `Prim::Concat` copies both strings | O(n²) per loop | fixed: a string value's text is `zed_kernel::Text`, which keeps every join as a balanced tree of its parts' blocks, made in O(log n) (row C29), and reads it block by block; equality, order and printing read the text, so a rope never enters identity (DESIGN.md sec:joins). The tree's height is O(log n), so a chain a million long is read and dropped without a deep recursion. `m0_10_finite_model_harness::scaling::a_string_joined_by_a_fold_scales` (a `foldl` of `++` over 8,000 against 64,000 pieces: old 26.5, new 9.2, measured before C29); `text::tests::a_chain_a_million_long_either_way_is_balanced_read_and_dropped`. Since T14-rope there is no size switch for short joins, each node keeps its character count (R4), and the native runtime holds the same `Text` |
| K17 | `rec.rs` flags recomputed per node; `contractive` DFS per definition; provenance `term`, `origins`, `holds`; `Type::values` product before cap; `Type::contains` payload copy; `Family::get` key copy | O(n·depth) | fixed: a group's bodies are translated once into a shared tree whose nodes record whether they mention the group, so a part kept unevaluated is shared, not copied, and no flag is computed twice; a recursive node's name is made when first printed; `contractive` is one pass of Tarjan's components (`first_on_cycle`, the lowest definition on a cycle, as before); `contains` tests an atom's payload in place; `values` compares a product with the cap before building it and keeps a running total over the paths; `origins` walks each fact's value once for the members it holds; `Provenance::terms` gives the term of every position in one walk (`term` walks to one position, and crates do not call it). `Family::get` copies a key only when it holds `-0.0`: the copy was `O(k)` for a key of size `k`, below the lookup's own `O(k log n)` comparisons, so it is kept for that case. `l01_23_subtyping::rec::recursive_definitions_scale` (a chain of definitions: ratio 31 → 9; a body nested `d` deep: 68 → 8), `rec::the_lowest_definition_on_a_cycle_is_named` (two mutants caught), `l01_23_subtyping::scaling::listing_values_past_the_cap_stops_early` (88 → 9.7; removed with `values` in T6, see K28), `l01_20_provenance::scaling::the_term_of_every_fact_scales` (61 → 9.3), `scaling::the_origins_of_a_failure_with_many_members_scale` (50 → 14.3), `m0_11_prelude_axioms_and_limits::signed_zero::either_zero_finds_the_one_key` (catches a lookup that skips the key form) |
| K18 | `value.rs` `merge_failures` copies every member of each `#errs` set into a new set at each merge | O(n²) for a chain of n failing operands | fixed: the merge clones the largest `#errs` set, which `imbl` shares, and inserts the other members, O(log n) each. Kernel term `#e0 + … + #e(n-1)`, debug build: the old merge does not finish the scaling check in 500 s; the new one passes it in 0.26 s. Test `l01_18_scalar_interpreter::scaling::a_chain_of_n_failures_merging_scales` |
| K19 | `types.rs` `Display for Type`: each type's text was made from copies of the texts of the types inside it | O(n·depth) | fixed: a type's text is a rope made once, holding the ropes of the types inside without copying them, and read with a stack of its own. Test `l01_22_type_representation::scaling::the_text_of_a_deeply_nested_type_scales`, n = 600; with the old code put back it fails at 47 to 56 times as long for 8 times the depth |
| K20 | reference evaluator: Rust recursion on a 1 GiB thread; `Prim::Tuple` and `Prim::Path` copied their values | O(n) stack, O(n²) to build a list of n | fixed (T2-eval): an explicit-stack machine, O(1) work per step outside values and environments, frames on the heap, no limit on depth. `m0_09_kernel_evaluator::deep`: `nested_calls_scale_with_depth` (19,990 against 159,920 calls, 1.2 µs per call at both, ratio 8.1 in a release build, measured outside the tests; the old evaluator took 10 µs per call at 19,990) with a control of O(depth) work per call (regions, 29 to 41; the limit is 24); `building_a_list_scales_with_its_length` (19,990 elements 16 s to 0.03 s; with the copy restored, 500 against 4,000: 83); `m0_10` `a_chain_ending_in_a_cycle_stalls_in_linear_time_under_demand` (retrying stalled entries, 100 against 800: 72 to 75). Open: reading a variable copies its `Value`, and `Clone`, `Drop` and `Eq` of a `Value` recurse, so a program that reads each cell of a list of n is O(n²) and the drop of a list deeper than the stack allows overflows it (T2-types, value.rs; the old evaluator had both). `Prim::Atom` still copies its payload. |
| K21 | reference evaluator: `lookup` clones the value it reads, and `Value::Tuple(Vec<Value>)` clones in depth | O(n²) for an accumulator of n nested tuples | fixed (T14-value): `Value::Tuple`, `Value::Path` and an atom's payload hold `Items`, an `Arc<[Value]>`, and an atom's field names an `Arc<[Arc<str>]>`, so a clone and a variable read are O(1). Values are now DAGs, so every deep walk of `deep.rs` meets a shared part once: comparison keeps the pairs of shared sequences it has read to the end, per relation (a shared sequence equals itself under identity and order, never under `==`, which must still read a NaN), `copy` (for `key`) keeps the copy of each shared sequence, and `all_leaves` (`is_key`) the shared sequences it has read. Drop stays iterative. Tests `m0_09_kernel_evaluator::shared` (11) and two in `deep_values`: 1,000,000 levels, each a pair of one shared value, compared, keyed and freed. Scaling, in instructions: a tuple read once per element, 2,000 and 16,000, 19.8 M and 179.7 M (9.1 times; main 840 M and 52,443 M, 62.4); n copies of an n-tuple 8.0 times (main 63.8); a `foldl` cons list of 1,000 and 8,000, 46.5 M and 422 M (9.1 times; main 903 M and 54,937 M, 60.9); compare and key a doubled value 5,000 and 40,000 deep under 1 s (with the read sets removed, past 60 s). |
| K22 | `Value` and `Family`: `Clone`, `Drop`, `PartialEq`, `Ord`, `Debug`, `Display`, `key`, `surface_eq`, `surface_cmp`, `from_leaves`, `leaves`, `union`, and `Type::contains` recursed on depth | O(depth) native stack, an abort with no message past the stack size | fixed (T2-eval-repair): `crates/zed-kernel/src/deep.rs`, each O(size) with its depth on the heap, a value dropped through a thread-local list. `deep_scaling` runs each at 160,000 and 1,280,000 levels, because below 160,000 the time per level rises as the value outgrows the caches (equality: 76 ns at 20,000, 435 at 160,000, 403 at 1,280,000); union and leaves of keypaths run at 20,000 and 160,000 (64 ms and 498 ms for the union). Negative control: a walk that moved its stack every 4096 levels took 169.5 ms and 7.81 s, 46.1 times as long, and failed the test (limit 24). T14-value measured `Display` of sets nested inside tuples, d = 2,000 and 16,000: 2.27 M and 18.06 M instructions (7.9 times), and the same test passes on main, because a set test compares a key with its value and the families below are one `imbl` tree, compared by address; the O(d²) once noted here is not reproduced (`shared::showing_sets_nested_inside_tuples_scales`). Open: equality of two families with no shared nodes, whose sub-values are shared as a DAG, is exponential in the DAG's depth, because imbl gives no node identity to mark visited. VALUE-one-tuple: `Display` writes a one-tuple `(x,)`, one length test per tuple, O(1); the same scaling test passes with that text. |
| K23 | `eval.rs` reads of a `fix` and of a lookup port: the key is normalized before the lookups | O(size of k) for the check, the order of the comparisons the lookup already makes; O(n log n) for n reads | no growth (T14-bugs-kernel): `m0_09_kernel_evaluator::reads_by_a_key_holding_negative_zero_scale`, a chain of n entries read by keys holding `-0.0`, Rounds and Pull: 8.4 ms and 83 ms at 1,000 and 8,000, 10 times as long at load 60 (limit 24). Negative control: a linear scan of the domain per read took 47 to 74 ms and 2.7 to 2.9 s, 39 to 57 times as long, and failed the test. |
| K24 | `types/empty.rs` `Region`: what is known about each negated tuple type on the box was kept per tuple type and component, and each tuple type tested apart, though many share a component's type | O(N) in the `N` components written, with a large constant | fixed (T5): a table of cells, one per component and distinct type there, each tested once for every tuple type that shares it (the Cell table of cross-review xe round 3, without its scalar case or its memo keyed by address; the prefix trie it was weighed against was `c^2.93` with the columns reversed). The input of a decision tree on `c` columns is `N ≈ 2c²` components, a lower bound for any check that reads it (DESIGN.md L1.23). Instructions to decide it, old against new, forward and reversed: c = 128: 1.86 / 1.88 G against 0.553 / 0.573 G; 256: 7.52 / 7.54 against 2.23 / 2.22; 512: 30.6 / 30.3 against 9.02 / 8.77; 1024: 121.4 / 121.9 G (14.4 s) against 35.6 / 35.6 G (4.5 s): 4.0 times per doubling of `c` in both, linear in `N`, the new table 3.4 times cheaper. T5b removed three further costs (a scalar part answered before the memo lookups, refused tuple types read from their cells' counts, no rebuilt set of live components): 1024 forward and reversed, by the scaling test's counter, 30.2 / 30.4 G before against 8.0 / 8.1 G after, 64 times from c = 128 to 1024. Tests `l01_23_subtyping::cells`: `a_decision_tree_is_decided_in_time_linear_in_its_size` (c = 32 and 256, built before the runs: 58.9 times forward, 57.6 reversed, limit 192), the tree in three column orders with each arm left out, 4000 random `match` tables against listing their values, renamed values and atoms, and three orders of queries. Negative controls, not kept: one test per open tuple type on every column at each split, 338 times (0.17 to 58.2 G); the hold test skipped for a shared cell, or a narrowed cell closed as missed, each fail the listing test. The old table also grows 4 times per doubling, so the scaling test does not tell it from the new one; their difference is the constant |
| K25 | `provenance.rs` `origins` searched once per member of the failure, and `holds` walks each value it reads as a tree; `unshare` copies the term to one node per position | `origins`: O(M·E) for `M` members, `E` input edges, quadratic at a fact that reads `M` operands. `unshare`: the tree size of the term, `2^d` for a term of `d` distinct nodes whose levels share a subterm. `holds`: the tree size of each value read, `2^d` for a value of `d` levels that share a sub-value | fixed (T14-provenance, then T14-occ). `origins` is one backward pass, newest fact first (a fact's inputs completed before it), carrying at each fact the members whose search reaches it (T14-provenance; row of 500 and 4000 missing readings: 56.7 → 12.5 times). T14-occ removed the three costs left. **`unshare`** is gone: the machine carries each term's position as it enters the node, a position being its parent's and one child index (a Dewey path in a persistent list), with a run-local id per distinct position, so a run with provenance does `O(1)` more work per step and a fact address names an occurrence whatever the term shares. **`holds`** is gone: the failures each value holds are found once per distinct tuple, path, payload and family, keyed by the id each family gets when it is built (a `u64` from a global counter; a copy keeps it, a change takes a new one) and by the address of each sequence (`Items::addr`), so a walk is `O(distinct parts)`; ids and addresses are only compared, never ordered or printed, so no result depends on their values. T14-occ gave `Items` an id too; T14-occ-id removed it: every value `origins` walks is borrowed from the facts for the whole call, so no sequence is freed during the walk and its address names it for that call. **Chain of products**: a family records the id of the family it was made from by adding entries (`Family::extend`, `union`), and a family's set of failures is found from that family's set, or from the set of one made from it, by `OrdMap::diff`, which skips the nodes the two share; the diff is abandoned once it is as long as the family, and the family is then read entry by entry. The common members of two sets are likewise read from their difference when it is short. Only such pairs are diffed: diffing a family against an unrelated one (the last family read, in a first version) compares the values at each common key whole, and on the shared levels below this made the pass `O(d²)` (54.5 and 84.0 times per 8 times the depth, measured and discarded). **`repeated`** (the check that no two facts share an address) kept the addresses in a set ordered by position, and comparing two positions walks up to their common ancestor, so on a chain of `n` products, `n` deep, it was `O(n²)` (250 and 2000: 13.8 M and 754.2 M, 54.7 times; 1000 and 8000: 60.6 times; main before T14-occ 11.6 times at 250 and 2000, and its `unshare` overflowed the stack at 8000); it now finds node addresses by their cached hashes, with an ordered set of keypaths per node (a `Value` has no hash): 1.53 M and 12.27 M, 8.0 times, and 8.0 times at 1000 and 8000 (`the_check_for_repeated_addresses_on_a_chain_of_products_scales`; the old set, run once as a negative control, fails it at 54.7 times). Tests in `m0_09_kernel_evaluator::provenance`, instructions at `n` and `8n`, against the old code run as a negative control (not committed): `a_term_shared_by_both_branches_of_each_level_scales` depth 3 and 24, 18.2 k and 126.2 k, 6.9 times (old: killed at the 60 s limit; measured before at 35 k against 59.2 G, 1,660,870 times); `the_origins_of_a_failure_built_one_reading_at_a_time_scale` 200 and 1600, 16.3 M and 171.2 M, 10.5 times (old: 149.9 M and 13.69 G, 91.3 times); `the_origins_of_a_failure_read_out_of_shared_levels_scale` depth 2 and 16, 57.1 k and 410.9 k, 7.2 times (old: 37.1 k and 196.6 M, 5,294.9 times); `the_origins_of_a_failure_read_out_of_deep_shared_levels_scale` depth 128 and 1024, 3.36 M and 27.2 M, 8.1 times (old: killed at 60 s); the wide row 500 and 4000, 11.3 times. Processor time at 64n: chain 12800 10.8 times over 1600, shared levels 8192 14.3 times over 1024, shared branches 1536 7.8 times over 192 (load 40-60). Correctness: every fact's origins equal a search per member that reads each value as a tree, on 6 shapes; a term whose levels share their branches gives the same facts, addresses and origins as a copy built without sharing; an empty `#errs`, a failure read by itself, and a term shared by both parts of the root `let`; a dump of every fact, value, input and origin over 8 shapes is byte-identical on the old code and the new (the dump leaves out the address: an address now prints as a path of child indices, `.1.0`, where it printed a preorder number; the order is the same, preorder, and printing one address costs `O(depth)`, so printing every address of a deep term is `O(n²)` characters). Cost: `size_of::<Value>()` 56 → 64 bytes, from the id in `Items`; T14-occ-id removed it and `Value` is 56 bytes again (test `value::tests::a_value_is_56_bytes`). ADDR-keys (K40) gives `Items` an id again and keeps `Value` at 56 bytes by making an atom's field names a thin `Fields`; `origins` keys sequences by that id, not by address. Measured on main (594ac07) against T14-occ-id, release build, no provenance, instructions and peak resident memory, 3 runs each, every run within 0.01% of the others in instructions (load 29-34): a chain of `n` missing readings multiplied one at a time, `n` = 20,000: 411.9 M → 405.7 M (−1.5%), 47.2 → 47.3 MB; 160,000: 3,673.5 M → 3,617.5 M (−1.5%), 158.5 → 152.2 MB (−4.0%); a family of `n` keys read once per key, 20,000: 310.6 M → 310.7 M (+0.05%), 47.4 → 47.3 MB; 160,000: 2,880.1 M → 2,881.2 M (+0.04%), 112.4 → 100.3 MB (−10.8%); a tuple of `n` read once per element, 20,000: 281.8 M → 281.9 M (+0.05%), 47.4 → 47.4 MB; 160,000: 2,582.7 M → 2,587.0 M (+0.17%), 122.3 → 108.8 MB (−11.0%); a `foldl` of `n` unions, 20,000: 1,674.8 M → 1,661.0 M (−0.8%), 163.5 → 146.3 MB (−10.5%); 160,000: 14,890.7 M → 14,793.7 M (−0.65%), 1,442.7 → 1,285.4 MB (−10.9%). The runs at 20,000 under 50 MB are the test binary's own floor. The provenance scaling tests keep their ratios (6.92, 7.98, 10.52, 8.10, 7.26, 11.26), and the dump of every fact and origin over 8 shapes is byte-identical to main's; keying sequences by their length instead, run once as a negative control, fails 5 of the 21 provenance tests and changes 110 lines of the dump. Open: a family read entry by entry, or an entry added to one, costs the size of the set of failures each entry holds, so `n` entries holding one set of `m` failures cost `n·m` (no worse than the old code). The diff compares the values at a key changed from one family to the one made from it whole, so a family whose entry is replaced by a deep value at each step is `O(depth)` per step (inferred, not measured; `extend` only adds members to `#errs` sets, and `union` changes a key only to the union of the two families there, which shares the nodes of the larger). `deep.rs` keys its memos of shared parts by address (`Items::addr`, the read and copy sets), against the rule that memo keys are canonical structure or interned ids; these, like `origins` since T14-occ-id, use an address only as a lookup key while every value it names is borrowed, never across a free, never iterated and never in an order or an output |
| K26 | `types/bes.rs` `decide` (cross-review X14 `split_bdw2`): emptiness of a recursive group as a Boolean equation system of `Or`, `And` and morphism nodes; the first version counted the distinct children of a node by searching a list | O(n²) on a node of n children | fixed (T6): distinct children counted with a stamp per node, O(nodes + edges) overall. `l01_23_subtyping::bes`: the 35 families at 512 and 4,096 (ratios 7.95 to 8.88) and the five widened variants at 4,096 and 32,768 (7.97 to 8.09). Control, the list search: `exring:6:1` 36.80, over the limit of 24 (X14 measured 36.92). |
| K27 | `types/rec.rs` `judge`, the lowering of a group to that system, and the admission check `refs` | O(size of the bodies) plus the emptiness of their closed parts; `same` compares two domain bounds by structure, O(size of the bounds) each, so a body that nests differing bounds d deep costs O(n·d) | the lowering is linear (`l01_23_subtyping::judgments::deciding_a_group_scales`, a ring of 256 and 2,048 definitions); the `same` cost is open, fix by hashing each bound once when the tree is built |

| K30 | `num.rs` exact numbers: every `int` and `rat` past `MAX_BITS` (2^20 bits) was `KernelError::Overflow`, a size threshold; and a number was always its full digits, so `10 ** n` or `1e400000r` cost O(n) bits to build and O(n^1.6) to multiply | O(n) to O(n^1.6) in the value's bits; a refusal past 2^20 bits | fixed (CAP-bits): a `Rat` is `n/d * prod p^e`, the exponents unbounded and kept, not built (`num.rs`); a decimal literal with an exponent is the case of the primes 2 and 5. Products, quotients and powers add and multiply exponents; a power of a base that fits 64 bits factors it (`num/factor.rs`, Miller-Rabin and Brent's rho), a wider base is built (Complexity tradeoff at `Rat::pow`, test `a_power_of_a_wide_base_is_built`). Order and projection to f64 and f32 use bounds `m * 2^e` to `k` bits with directed rounding, `p^e` by squaring, `k` doubled until they decide (`num/bound.rs`); two Complexity tradeoffs there: a near-tie closer than the products' width is decided by building them (`close_products_compare_exactly`, `projection_matches_the_standard_reading`); a complete solution stops at a precision from Baker's lower bound on linear forms in logarithms. `MAX_BITS` and `KernelError::Overflow` are removed. Test `l01_17_value_representation::powers::ten_to_n_costs_its_exponent_and_not_its_digits` (power 0, limit 3): `10^n` built, compared, multiplied, divided and projected at n = 2^20 and 2^23, 178,753 and 203,141 instructions, 1.1 times. Negative control, the same work on built values (not committed), n = 2^17 and 2^20: 62.8 M and 1,362.9 M, 21.7 times, fails. Plain rationals (no powers) keep their fast paths: 2000 add/sub/mul/div/cmp of growing plain rationals and 5 f64 literals, least of 5, 3,846.1 M instructions before and 3,854.1 M after (+0.2%). Repair 1: `div` and `mod` by a kept divisor first divide symbolically; an integer quotient (exact division) or a dividend smaller than the divisor builds nothing, and only the rest builds the divisor (Complexity tradeoff at `Rat::rem_built` and in `Rat::div_euclid`; test `an_exact_division_by_a_huge_power_builds_nothing`, exponent 10^12). The scaling test also divides and reduces `10^n` by kept powers. Negative control, the exponent shortcuts in `div_euclid` and `rem_euclid` removed (not committed), n = 4096 and 32768: 1,827,083 and 39,194,018 instructions, 21.5 times, fails; at n = 2^20 it runs past 60 s. `1 ** e`, `0 ** e` and `-1 ** e` use the exponent's parity (`Rat::is_even`) and never build `e` (`a_unit_to_a_kept_power_builds_no_exponent`). Every build in `+`, `-`, `**`, `div` and `mod` first bounds its width as coefficient bits plus `e * ceil(log2 p)` and asks the allocator for it (`num::room`, `Vec::try_reserve_exact`, freed at once); a refusal is `KernelError::NumberTooLarge { op, bits }`, a run-time error naming the width, not an abort, and no size constant decides it (`a_sum_too_large_to_build_is_an_error_naming_its_size`, `a_number_too_large_to_build_stops_the_run`). Negative control, `room` always succeeding (not committed): both tests abort the process with "memory allocation of 274877906968 bytes failed" (SIGABRT). Open: the digits of a printed number, `to_string`, and `gcd` and division of built values are num-bigint's, O(n²). Repair 2: `String` applied to a number first asks for the memory of the number built and its digits (`Rat::text_room`), so `String(2 ** 2 ** 100)` is `NumberTooLarge { op: "String" }` in 0.02 s (`printing_a_number_too_large_to_build_is_an_error_naming_its_size`, `printing_a_number_too_large_to_build_stops_the_run`); negative control, the check removed (not committed): the kernel test ran 30 s, still building, until the timeout killed it (load 30). Repair 3 (L1.17a): a number printed inside a tuple, keypath, atom or family is checked first by `words::room_to_print`, an explicit-stack walk that reads a shared sequence or family once (test `a_shared_tuple_is_checked_once_before_printing`, n copies of one shared tuple of n numbers, n = 4096 and 32768, power 1). Order and rounding by bounds ask the allocator before each precision (`8k` bits for a comparison round, `16k` for a rounding round, the full widths before the near-tie build) and stop with `OrderTooLarge`, `KeyOrderTooLarge` or `RoundTooLarge`; the infallible `Ord` records a refusal under the evaluator's `num::Watch`, and outside a run keeps the old unchecked path. A bound reads its `k` bits from the coefficient by reference (`Bound::of`; the coefficient was copied in full each round), and the comparison borrows a coefficient whose cofactor is 1. Tests in `l01_17_value_representation::memory_check` run each refusing case in a child process whose `RLIMIT_AS` is lowered to its size plus 64 MiB after the 128 MiB operands are built; they found three copies of the operand made without asking (`aligned`'s `%` by a prime, `project`'s `abs`, and the copy `div_rem` makes of the dividend, now counted in the `16k`), each an abort before the fix. Scaling test `comparing_numbers_that_agree_in_n_bits_costs_order_n` (`2^n + 1` against kept `2^n`, n = 2^22 and 2^25, power 1). Negative control, precision grown by `k += 64` instead of doubled (not committed), n = 2^18 and 2^21: 2,213 M and 128,113 M instructions, 57.9 times, fails (limit 24). Repair 4 (L1.17a): `String` of a composite asked for each number's digits one at a time, while the text holds all of them; `words::room_to_print` now sums the length of the text over every occurrence, a shared sequence's or family's length computed once and added at each further occurrence (O(distinct parts)), and `words::print` reserves that block and asks for the widest number's build and the copy into the value with it held (`TextTooLarge`; unit test `the_counted_length_is_the_written_length`, child test `printing_many_copies_of_a_number_asks_for_the_whole_text`, 40,000 copies of a 2^14-bit number). `Rat::aligned` asks for eight times the coefficients' width before copying them (the copy, and `valuation`'s quotient, remainder and powers of p), through `try_cmp` and, for the infallible `==`, the `Watch` record (`EqualityTooLarge`); the plain-rational order uses the checked `times`. `reduce` reduces the wider operand modulo the narrower before num-bigint's Stein gcd, which costs the product of the widths even against a one-word divisor: a verifier probe could not build wide plain rationals within 60 s, and `Rat::new(2^(2^30) + 1, 3)` now takes one division (the child test builds two in under 7 s with its 128 MiB operands). Negative controls (not committed), each check disabled: the wide-rational order and the equality copy abort with "memory allocation of 134217736 bytes failed", the 40,000 copies with "memory allocation of 80838656 bytes failed", and the shared-tuple test without the memo is killed at 60 s; the real code passes all four in under 2 s. Open: the compiler's serialization of a number (`canon/serial.rs`, `zir/binary.rs`); Stein's gcd of two wide operands of similar width is still O(n²) (num-bigint has no subquadratic gcd). Repair 5 (L1.17a): the order of numbers of two kinds placed each on the extended line by a copy of a wide operand made without asking (`Value::exact`); `Exact::Finite` now borrows a wide number (`Cow`), and the order goes through the watched `Rat` order. `int` and `rat` of a wide number copied it; they now share its digits. Unary `-` and `abs` of a negative wide number copied it unasked; they now ask first (`Rat::try_neg`, `NumberTooLarge { op: "-" }` and `{ op: "abs" }`), and `abs` of one not negative shares it (`negating_a_wide_number_is_refused_when_memory_is_short`; control, the check removed: abort "memory allocation of 134217736 bytes failed"). A refusal records its test (order or equality), and a literal pattern of `match`, the fixpoint test of a lattice `fix`, and a family's leaves at one keypath each stop with their own error (`EqualityTooLarge { op: "match" }`, `{ op: "fix" }`, `FamilyEqualityTooLarge`) instead of the key-order error; an error that follows a refused test (a collision of two leaves the normal forms took as different) is reported as the refusal. Tests `ordering_a_wide_integer_against_a_rational_is_refused_when_memory_is_short`, `casting_a_wide_number_shares_its_digits`, `a_literal_pattern_refused_its_equality_test_is_an_error_of_match`, `a_lattice_fixpoint_test_refused_is_an_error_of_fix`, `a_family_merge_refused_its_equality_test_is_an_error` (a `Collision` before this repair), with the same operations answered when memory is given. ZIR type text reads the form back (`zir::text::number`). | K51 | `types/empty.rs` kept emptiness results across queries (SUB-order-corpus, tests only; the memo itself is F32's) | each query's search, its cost unchanged by the test | kept as F32 states; test added, one per seed so each has a thread and a process of its own: `l01_23_subtyping::judgments::subtype_answers_do_not_depend_on_the_queries_before_them_seed_{1,5,6}` and `_seed_{2,3}_part_{0,1,2}` and `_seed_4_part_{0,1}`, 150 types (recursive lists by length residue and by the nth item from the end, `S = (int) -> S`, `W = (W) -> int`, then unions, meets, differences, pairs and morphism types by a fixed sequence) and 1500 queries `a <: b` answered one at a time after `forget_emptiness`, in one batch and reversed, all agreeing. Times alone: seeds 1, 5, 6 at 2.3, 0.6 and 0.5 s (load 74); seed 4 at 14 to 28 s (load 72 to 83). SUB-corpus-slow profiled seeds 2, 3 and 4 (perf, DWARF call graphs): 80% of the time is the one-at-a-time pass, which by design repeats a cold search per query, and most of that is a few types whose one search meets thousands of distinct types (seed 2 type 137, 428 characters: 2562 types, 3064 regions, 69019 `settles` calls, 0.5 s; seed 3 types 59, 102, 115, 124, 128 and 132: about 17500 `settles` each). No judgment grew faster than the types its search meets (about 20 `settles` per region at every size); the cost is a large constant per type met, with types compared in full when an equal one was built apart. Fix: `Search::settles` builds each meet or difference once per pair of operand nodes in a search (`cuts`, keyed by node address; `// Complexity tradeoff:` there): keyed by contents it was 1.3 to 1.6 times faster on the corpus, but each hit on an equal operand built apart compared the two in full, and control, not committed: `a_tuple_that_shapes_hold_only_together_is_checked_in_the_size_of_the_union` then took 47.6 times the instructions from 64 to 512 (limit 24, load 60; it passes on the old code and with the node key). The one-at-a-time pass is split by query index: seeds 2 and 3 into three tests, seed 4 into two. Times alone after: seed 2 parts 11.2 to 23.5 s, seed 3 parts 7.7 to 14.9 s, seed 4 parts 7.5 and 10.4 s, seeds 1, 5, 6 at 5.0, 0.3 and 0.5 s (load 57 to 67; the machine is shared, and one part varied from 11.2 to 23.5 s between runs). Before (the same binary with the memo off): seed 2 whole 27.0 and 28.9 s, seed 3 20.7 and 28.5 s (load 58 to 67). Scaling: `nested_tuples_scale` runs `settles` many times in one search, depth 400 to 3200; control, not committed: a scan of the search's types in each `settles` fails it at 34.0 times (limit 24, load 60). A family of `k` pairs over two shared list types was tried as a new test and dropped: each first component is a class of its own, so every search stays small, and the same control passed it. Open for the author: the remaining cost is that types are not interned, so an equal type built apart is compared in full on each lookup (about 22% of the time on type 137); interning is a larger change, not taken here. Control, not committed: the answers of each search's first walk (read on assumptions still open) kept across queries fail seed 1 at query 1202 and seed 4 at query 246, and no other `l01_23_subtyping` test |
| K52 | `types/empty.rs` `find_rep`, the closure a morphism cycle is shown inhabited by (SUB-recursive-empty-part, tests only) | as the Kept row "emptiness and subtyping of tuple and morphism parts" | kept as that row states; test added: `l01_23_subtyping::judgments::a_morphism_cycle_through_an_empty_part_is_empty`: `W = (W) -> int` is inhabited (verdict `Inhabited`) and `W <: W`; `V = int -> (V, bool -> empty)` is empty (verdict `Empty`); `N = V -> empty` is not empty. Its verdict is `Refused(NoWitness)`, not `Inhabited` as the round-3 test expected, since a closure is built only when its value type has a value, although a closure whose key type is empty is never called; the test asserted only "not `Empty`" for that verdict. EMPTY-key-closure: `find_rep` now shows a closure for a meet of morphism types whose key types are each known empty without a search (the empty type, or a recursive type its group decided empty, `known_empty`), whatever its value type, since such a closure is never called; the test asserts `Inhabited` for `N`. O(1) per morphism type of the meet after the group's decision, which is made once per group and is the one `empty` reads; no search is started. A key type only a search proves empty gets no closure: `(bool -> empty) -> empty` is not empty and its verdict is `Refused(NoWitness)` (asserted in the same test; open: EMPTY-key-search). Control for EMPTY-key-closure, not committed: the old `find_rep` fails the test at `N = V -> empty is inhabited` with `Refused(NoWitness)`. Control, not committed: a closure counted as returnable when a part of its value type is the closure being built (the round-3 bug) fails only this test (at `N = V -> empty is inhabited`; 113 other `l01_23_subtyping` tests pass) |
| K53 | `types.rs` `Rec`, `types/bdd.rs` `types`, `types/print.rs` `Printer::canon`, `types/rec.rs` `Key`, `Checked::order`: a recursive type's digest and its place in the order of types came from a process-wide counter, so they depended on what the process built before (REC-id-order) | O(1) per comparison of two recursive types whose digests differ, as before | fixed (REC-id-order): a recursive node holds a `Key`, the instance's environment and the member's index or the number of the body node kept unevaluated; its digest is made of the definitions' digest, the arguments' digests and that place, and two keys are ordered by digest, place, then, for different groups that tie, by the definitions' names and parameters and their body trees node by node in a loop of its own (O(size of the definitions)), the types inside them that tie in digest and then the arguments left as steps of `bdd::order`'s loop, so no comparison recurses. One environment, or one group, decides in O(1). The printer breaks a tie of names by the type's order. Tests `l01_23_subtyping::rec_order`: `checking_a_program_alone_or_after_another_agrees` (one program's types in a child process alone and in one that built 45 other recursive groups first; digests, order, texts and three verdicts for each pair; before the fix 10 of 121 lines differed: 9 of 10 digests and the order) and `the_members_of_a_group_are_ordered_in_n_log_n` (a group of n members built, sorted in a `BTreeSet` and their 1-tuples united, n = 512 and 4,096: 44.1M and 438.5M instructions, 9.95 times, 8.92 times the bytes, limit 24, load 62). Both pass in the `short-addresses` build too. Control, run once, not committed: members' keys tied in digest and each comparison walking the whole group gives 127.7 times the bytes at 64 and 512 (72.8 MB and 9,300 MB) and runs past 60 s at 512 and 4,096 |

## Front end (`crates/zedc/src`, not store or zir)

| ID | Where | Class | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | `format.rs` `Formatter::block` renders a one-statement block twice | 2^depth | fixed: the statement is rendered once, at the inner indentation, and written on the block's line when its text has no line break (no indentation enters such a text, and the first attempt runs only when the block holds no comment, so the two renderings were the same text); output identical to the old at depths 1 to 10. `l01_13_formatter::scaling::nested_one_statement_blocks_scale` (old: 8 s at depth 20, ratio 454 between depths 2 and 16; new: 6 ms at depth 20) |
| F2 | `elab.rs`: a default argument is elaborated again at every call site | exponential (confirmed: the term of `main` doubled with each function of a chain whose defaults call the previous function twice, 2.9 million characters at 16) | fixed: a default other than a literal or a name is elaborated once, as a definition `f$x` beside its function taking the parameters it reads, and a call leaving it out binds the call of it; a literal or a name is still copied. A call through a function value still copies its closed default, whose own calls of declared functions now stay small. `l01_16_elaboration::scaling::defaults_calling_functions_with_defaults_scale` (old: fails the size check; new: 46 ms at 16, a term of 32 characters) |
| F3 | `elab::topo`: linear scan for the next ready definition | O(n³) | fixed: Kahn's order with the ready items in a heap, smallest index first, `O((n + e) log n)`; the same order and the same cycle as the scan on 2,000 random dependency lists. `l01_16_elaboration::scaling::the_dependency_order_scales` (old: 15 s at 32,000, ratio 65.6; new: passes) |
| F4 | `stage::Mixer::mix`: `free` and `effects` per node, bound names in a `Vec` | O(k³) | fixed: `mix_top` indexes the term once in preorder (`Index`): each position holds the binder position of the unknown name read there, or `RUN` for an unknown free name or a port read outside a lattice join, in a range-minimum tree (`Mins`), so the subterm at `p..p + size` is build-stage exactly when the least value there is at least `p`, one `O(log k)` query; a `Let` whose name becomes known at build clears its reads, and whether a mixed body still reads a name is counted where a subterm is kept as written. The scope is a stack with each name's positions in a map, and `eval` closes a term over the entries it needs by position, `O(log k)` each, instead of a scan of the scope. Mixing a chain of local definitions: 6.4, 32, 206 ms at 100, 200, 400 definitions before, 1.8, 3.3, 7.2 ms after. `l01_28_staging::scaling::mixing_a_chain_of_local_definitions_scales` (ratio 7.9, set by loading the program), `scaling::mixing_a_term_that_reads_every_definition_scales` (8.7); `mix::a_local_definition_is_computed_where_it_is_read` and `mix::a_write_of_a_constant_is_left_to_run` catch three mutants of the index (a known name never cleared, `>` for `>=`, a write not an effect) that the earlier tests passed |
| F5 | term canonicalizer: one binder renamed per step, whole term walked per step; facets bubble-sorted | O(B·t·d), O(F³) | fixed: `canonicalize` reaches the rules' normal form directly. `binders::canonical` renames every binder in one walk, with each written name's new names in a stack in a map, `O(t log t)`; the facets are filtered of hints, sorted and deduplicated once, `O(F log F)`. The rules stay as the definition and for the termination tests, and the facet rule's rank counts inversions by merge sort, `O(F log F)`, where it compared every pair. A chain of 2000 binders: 4.7 s before, 12 ms after; 2000 facets in reverse order: 10.2 s before, 1.6 ms after. `m0_07_canonicalization::scaling::canonicalizing_many_binders_scales` (ratio 13.2), `scaling::sorting_many_facets_scales` (11.4); `alpha::the_normal_form_is_the_rules_applied_one_step_at_a_time` compares one pass with the rules on random terms, a fixed point, a variable outside its binder's scope and a binder already named by a level, and `rules::the_facet_rank_counts_the_pairs_out_of_order_and_the_facets` checks the rank; they catch the four mutants the other tests passed |
| F6 | `check`: `Info` cloned per signature-search run; `within` scans all adoptions | O(F·U) | fixed: the checker logs each write to `Info`'s maps with the value it replaced; a search's run, and a tried repair, are undone by popping their writes and truncating the errors, and a combination that checks keeps only the writes of its run, its adoptions inside the body read from those writes. The kept combination's writes are made again, through the log, so an enclosing search still undoes them. A run now costs what it writes, not the size of the unit. n functions searched beside 20n constants, at n = 400: 12.3 s before, 7.9 s after; the ratio at sizes 50 and 400 was 11.7 before and 8.4 after, so the copies did not yet dominate there, and a size at which they do (about 1600 functions) takes minutes per run in a debug build. `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::searching_the_signatures_of_many_functions_scales` keeps the cost linear; mutants of the undo (errors kept, a removal not undone, the kept writes not made again, a repair's check loosened) each fail the checker's tests. Comparing the kept combinations' adoptions over one side's writes only survives, and is equivalent: all kept combinations have the same fewest adoptions that differ from before the search, so a write on the other side alone holds the value from before |
| F7 | `Tables::uses_in` visits nested scopes' uses once per enclosing scope | O(uses·depth) | fixed: `Tables` lists each binder's uses once; `order` reads only the uses of its scope's own binders and places each in its statement, and outside the statement's `test` tables, by binary search, so each use is read once, for the scope that binds what it names: O(uses log n). `Tables::outer` gives each function the binders it reads, itself or through the functions inside it, from the function around it, in one sweep of the uses in the order of the text over the chain of open functions; each use adds its binder to one function, so the lists hold O(uses). Both were checked against the old scans by assertions over the whole test suite, and a mutant that ignores the `test` tables trips them. The case of a function's own name inside it is kept but cannot yet occur, since recursion is rejected before checking (L6). `Tables::outer` was removed with the signature search (F18). `l01_16_elaboration::scaling::checking_and_ordering_nested_functions_scales` |
| F8 | `source::line_col` decodes from byte 0 per diagnostic, note and test | O(k·N) | fixed: `source::Lines` finds the line starts of a text once, `O(N)`, and places an offset by a binary search and a count of the scalars before it on its line; `run::compile` makes one for all its diagnostics and their steps, whose source line now starts at the line start found there instead of a search back through the text, and `testing::checks` one for all its tests. `line_col` alone reads only the bytes before its offset, for the single places of a rejected file and the formatter. 4000 lines each with a diagnostic: 2.9 s before, 0.46 s after. `l01_29_diagnostics::scaling::placing_a_diagnostic_on_every_line_scales` (ratio 8.1, against 32 before); `l01_01_source_loading::places::every_offset_is_placed_as_decoding_places_it` checks every offset of random text with each kind of line break against decoding from byte 0, and catches the mutants of the index |
| F9 | `testing`: cone and `unit.close` per test over the whole unit | O(T·U) | fixed: `elab::Cones` indexes a unit once, each kernel name's definition in a map and each definition's free names found when first needed, and gives a term's cone by a worklist over the definitions it reaches, in the unit's order. `testing::checks` makes one per unit; a row's query key (`query::cone`) and the term it runs (`Cones::close`) read only that cone, so a row no longer runs under the lets of definitions it cannot read, as its key already assumed. `Unit::close` closes over the cone too, and a `Program` keeps `main` closed at its first wave. A rejected unit's diagnostics are found by line in a map, and the marked lines in a set. 800 functions with a row each, the front end's result reused: 4.9 s before, 0.11 s after. `l01_21_test_runner::scaling::running_the_rows_of_many_tables_scales` (ratio 8.8, against 52 to 74 before); `l01_21_test_runner::cones` checks the cone against a backward scan of random units and the order of the lets, and catches the mutants of the worklist and the order; `marks::a_marked_line_is_placed_at_its_first_diagnostic_and_others_stay_failures` catches keeping the last diagnostic of a line |
| F10 | `format.rs` comment scans over all comments per call | O(S·C) | fixed: the comments not yet written are kept in a map by start offset; `take`, `take_same_line` and `has_comment` read the range they ask about and remove what they write, `O(log C)` per comment and per query. 500 and 4000 statements each with four comments before and one after: 85 ms and 2.3 s before (ratio 26.5–27.4), linear after; one comment before and one after each of 8000 statements: 3.6 s before, 0.92 s after. `l01_13_formatter::scaling::many_comments_scale` (fails on the old code), which also checks that such a text formats to itself |
| F11 | formatter copies each level's string into its parent | O(N·depth) | fixed: the render functions return a tree of text pieces (`Doc`), each level adding only its own text, with whether each subtree holds a line break; the tree is written into one string at the end, without recursion. Output unchanged (the formatter's tests compare texts exactly). `f(a) = a + a + … + a`, debug build: 0.51, 1.0, 2.05, 4.6 and 11.3 s at 16k to 256k terms before (×2.4 at the top), 0.54, 0.99, 2.0, 4.05 and 8.1 s after (×2.0 at each doubling). No test: memory copying is fast enough that the old code passes the ratio-24 check up to 256k terms, so a test would not fail on it |
| F12 | checker's `else` pattern scans all binders | O(elses·binders) | fixed: the resolver records the range of binders each `else` pattern creates (`Resolution::patterns`, by the `else`'s text), and the checker gives those binders `_` where it scanned every pattern binder starting before the default. n bindings each with an `else` pattern, debug build: 1.14, 3.29, 11.3 and 42.7 s at 2000 to 16000 before, 0.66, 1.32, 2.64 and 5.36 s after. `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::many_else_patterns_scale` (fails on the old code) |
| F13 | `stage::free_in` with a `Vec` of bound names | O(t·depth) | fixed with F4: the bound names are counted in a map, `O(t log depth)` |
| F14 | lexer `"""` decoding: `cuts.iter().any` per character | O(chars·lines) | fixed: the cuts, which do not overlap, are sorted once; each literal piece finds its first cut by binary search and passes the cuts in step with its characters, `O(chars + pieces·log lines)`. A `"""` string of n indented lines each holding a hole, debug build: 45, 170, 715 and 2786 ms at 1000 to 8000 lines before, 4.5, 8.2, 16.9 and 34 ms after. `l01_05_lexer_strings::scaling::a_long_indented_string_scales` (fails on the old code) |
| F15 | nested string holes rescanned per level | O(N·depth) | fixed: lexing a text keeps `string::Holes`, the end of each hole found by the position of its `${` and the positions of the text's line breaks. A hole is scanned for its `}` once, where it was scanned again by the scan of each hole around it and by the lexing of each; the check that a hole of a `"` string stays on its line is a binary search in the line breaks, where it searched each nested string's whole text at each level. A string with holes nested d deep, debug build: 64, 270, 1322 and 8763 ms at d = 500 to 4000 before, 3.4, 5.1, 11.3 and 25.9 ms after. `l01_05_lexer_strings::scaling::nested_holes_scale` (fails on the old code) |
| F16 | `resolve` scope chain walk; overload check by pairs | O(uses·depth), O(K²) | fixed: each name keeps a stack of its bindings in the open scopes, innermost last, and each scope the names it binds, which closing it pops: a name is found in O(1). The parameter types of a scope's overloads are a set. Blocks nested 800 and 6400 deep, the innermost reading a top-level name n times: 0.53 and 18.3 s (ratio 34) before, 0.28 and 2.2 s (7.9) after; 1000 and 8000 overloads: ratio 26.4 before, 9.1 after. Kept: a use of K overloads records all K binders, and `Tables::new` lists the use under each, O(K·U) for U uses; at L1 every such use is an error (choosing among overloads arrives at L2), so valid programs never pay it. To fix at L2: share the set (`Arc<[usize]>`) and list uses by set. `l01_14_name_resolution::scaling` |
| F17 | `Ty::union`/`meet` by deep equality; `derive_into`/`also` note dedup by scan; `ast::args` | O(T²), small | partly fixed, rest kept: `union` and `meet` drop repeated tuples and functions by hash sets, where each was searched for in the list built so far; `ast::args` finds a repeated argument name in a map; `derive_into` was fixed with F19. An `if`/`else if` chain of n branches each giving a different tuple type, debug build: 0.16, 0.50, 2.07 and 11.7 s at n = 200 to 1600 before (×4 to ×5.7 per doubling), 0.16, 0.50, 1.71 and 6.4 s after (×3.6). Kept: what remains is the size of the types themselves. Each `if` of the chain has the union of the tuple types of the branches below it, so the types the checker builds, stores per expression and walks (`pieces`, `closed`, `first_order`, `diff`) total Θ(n²); the same chain with one tuple type is linear (0.67 s at 1600). Removing it needs types whose tuple sets are shared between a union and its parts, with those properties cached, a redesign of `Ty`. The scaling test cannot separate the two (the old chain's ratio between 200 and 1600 is 74, inside the limit of 192 for order n²), so no test was added. `also` still searches an error's notes for the one it adds: an error inside n nested calls of different inferred functions gets n notes, and checking took 0.35, 0.66, 1.33 and 2.68 s at n = 500 to 4000, linear at those sizes |
| F18 | `check`: each run of a signature search checked the functions declared in its body again, so the runs of nested functions' searches multiplied until RUNS stopped them; past depth 6 the rerun with notes then found no error and indexed past the errors (a panic) | 2^depth | fixed: inside the outermost search a function's signature is found once per context, which is the context of the search around it and the types of the binders it reads from the function around it (`Tables::outer`); contexts are numbered, so a key holds only those types. A search inside another leaves its writes as one log entry, `Undo::Nested`, that the maps do not hold; finding it again pushes that entry in O(1), and the outermost search makes all the writes once, when it ends. Each key is written only by the search of the innermost function around it, so the order they are made in does not matter (asserted over the test suite). An enclosing search compares nested searches' adoptions by number: each search's adoptions that differ from before it are interned with those of the searches inside it. The rerun with notes starts from the work count the quiet run started from, and falls back to the quiet run's error. The cap on searches is a budget of work, not of runs: the outermost search may check `RUNS` (20000) times its function's size in bytes, each run counted at its function's size less the functions declared in it, whose own searches are counted as they run. The searches inside one function then cost at most `RUNS` times its size however they nest. Two other caps were tried and rejected: one count of runs for the whole unit (never reset, a defect of the old checker: a large program's later functions were left untyped once its earlier ones had spent it), and one count per search, which stops no search of a function nested in others, since the context multiplies the searches through each level (7^depth for parameters with no declared type). The memo and the context numbers are hash maps by key (a kernel type hashes by its digest), not lists scanned for the key, whose cost grew with the square of the contexts of one function. Mutants of the tests: with no memo hits, and with every instance of a search given a new context number, neither test finishes in 40 minutes; with a hit's writes made again, the test of functions beside 20 others fails at ratio 34–36. The searches still multiplied through nested functions until the budget stopped them: for functions nested n deep, each with a parameter of no declared type, all of which the innermost adds, the work spent was 68k, 253k and 1.09M bytes at n = 8, 10 and 12, and reached the budget at n = 16 (11.26M), 24 (17.5M) and 32 (23.7M); 72, 120 and 168 s at n = 16, 24 and 32 in a debug build. This is not a missing visited set: logging every lookup at n = 4, 6 and 8 gives 103, 336 and 953 misses and as many distinct keys, so no search ran twice. The contexts themselves multiply: each search runs its body with `_` and then with type variables (and with carriers if those fail), each run reaches the nested function in a context that is really new, and the searches at the deepest level number 9, 34, 58, 91, 142, 229 and 388 for depths 1 to 7, almost all patterns of `_` and type variables. Fixed for now by a cap, `CONTEXTS` (64): inside the outermost search a function is searched in at most 64 contexts, and in any further one gives `_` for its undeclared parameters and its result, without checking its body. The time is then 0.07, 0.19, 0.44 and 1.05 s at n = 8, 16, 32 and 64. The cap changes results once it binds: at n = 8 `main` is `_` where it was int, since the context typing `x7` as int comes after 64 others; up to n = 6 they are unchanged. The cap, like `RUNS` and `COMBOS`, is a threshold to be replaced by an algorithm with no parameter (the author's direction, 2026-10-30). `l01_16_elaboration::scaling::searches_through_nested_functions_scale` (without the cap it did not finish in 25 minutes). **Replaced** (docs/thresholds.md, replacement 1): the search, its memo, its contexts and its caps are gone; an undeclared parameter is a class of a union-find forest of carriers, and each body is checked once. Functions nested 8, 32, 128 and 256 deep take 8, 23, 94 and 188 ms, and the innermost signature is `(T) -> T` at every depth, where the cap gave `_` from 8 deep; a function of 100, 400 and 1,600 parameters all added takes 42, 171 and 716 ms. `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::a_function_of_many_untyped_parameters_scales`, `a_chain_of_functions_with_untyped_parameters_scales`. Functions nested 200, 400 and 800 deep: with the memo and its writes made again at each finding, 3.65, 10.9 and 31.4 s (10n² writes; ratio 18.0 over 8×); with the context numbered by the instance of the search around, k+1 searches of the function at depth k; now 2.5, 5.6 and 11.1 s, ratio 8.3. The checker's output (adoptions, calls, fits, signatures, errors) is identical to the old one's on 1,900 random programs of nested functions. `l01_16_elaboration::scaling::checking_and_ordering_nested_functions_scales` |
| F19 | `check`: a derivation followed every read of a value again, so a value read twice at each of k bindings was derived 2^k times until the cut `DEPTH` (16), and each step was checked against every step already shown, O(n²) | 2^k, then n² | fixed (docs/thresholds.md, replacement 2): a set of the expressions derived and one of the steps shown, and an explicit stack; 0.15, 0.32, 0.63 and 1.20 s at 400 to 3,200 bindings. `l01_29_diagnostics::scaling::deriving_through_values_read_twice_scales` (with the visited set disabled it did not finish in 300 s) |
| F20 | `cost::frontier` compares every pair of feasible plans | O(P²·devices) | fixed: the plans are sorted by the lexicographic order of their cost vectors, where every dominator precedes what it dominates, and each is compared only with the frontier found before it: O(P log P + P·F·devices) for F plans on the frontier. Still quadratic when every plan is on the frontier; no known algorithm finds a Pareto frontier of more than three quantities in O(P log P). Test `m0_06_cost_scalarization::the_frontier_takes_time_linear_in_the_plans_when_it_stays_small` (0.19 s; the old filter does not finish in 500 s) |
| F21 | `check` `Ty::without_loose`, at each binary operator, takes the set difference over all the atoms of its operand | O(n²) for a chain of n failure atoms (`#e0 + … + #e(n-1)`: elaboration 0.42, 1.35 and 5.18 s at n = 500 to 2000, debug build) | fixed at L1.27. The cost was the complement in `a − b = a & !b`: `Atoms::not` builds the complement of its operand eagerly, and the loose atoms are every atom so far. `without_loose` now computes `(set − atoms) ∪ (set ∩ family)`, which equals `set − loose`: `atoms` lists no atom names and the family is small, so each step costs O(f log n) for f family atoms. Test `l01_27_absences::scaling::a_chain_of_many_failure_atoms_scales`, n = 1000 and 8000, each chain checked twice: 10.4 s (debug). On the old `without_loose` it does not finish in 10 times that (killed at 105 s) |
| F22 | `elab` `ty` folds a union or intersection of n types one at a time, and `check` `Ty::union_all` copies the union so far, with a hash set of its tuple shapes, at each member | O(n²) for `x is (#a0, int) \| … \| (#a(n-1), int)`: each tuple type is joined to the decision diagram of those before it, and each checker union copies them | fixed: `ty` joins in pairs (`Type::union_all`, `Type::meet_all`), and `Ty::union_all` builds the union once with one hash set. Debug build, n = 250 to 2000: elaboration 50, 180, 720 and 2,860 ms before, 4, 7, 15 and 34 ms after. Test `l01_22_type_representation::scaling::a_union_of_many_tuple_types_scales`, with the one-at-a-time union as its negative control |
| F23 | `elab` and `check`: each named argument looked for among all the parameters (three call paths in `elab`, one in `check`), and each default's reads among all the parameters | O(args·params) | fixed: each call path makes a map from parameter name to index once (the first index where a name repeats, as `position` gave), and the defaults' reads go through a map from kernel name to index. Tests `l01_16_elaboration::scaling::a_call_naming_many_arguments_scales` and `a_call_through_a_value_naming_many_arguments_scales`, n = 8000, elaboration timed alone; each of the four searches put back alone fails (32 to 44 times as long for 8 times the size; the default-reads search does not finish in 50 min) |
| F24 | `ast` `Display`: a lambda's or function's parameters, and an atom pattern's parts, were each made into a `String` and joined, so a lambda nested in a parameter's default was copied once per enclosing level | O(n·depth) | fixed: the parts are written straight into the formatter. Test `l01_10_lambdas_types_else_tuples::scaling`, depth 1,000 to 8,000. The test passes with the old code: at the depths the recursive builder reaches with a 4 GB stack the copying is too small to measure, and depth 32,000 overflows. Open: the builder and `Display` recurse once per level (about 32 KB of debug-build stack per level); with an explicit stack the test can reach depths where it would fail |
| F25 | `stage.rs` `mix`: each name a definition reads looked for in the list of the roots | O(defs·roots) | fixed: the roots are put in a set once. Test `l01_28_staging::scaling::the_values_passed_by_many_definitions_are_read_in_time_that_scales`, n = 2000; with the old code put back it fails at 29 to 32 times as long for 8 times the size |
| F26 | `stage.rs` `ends`: each name looked up by searching the list of the names bound around it, innermost last | O(n·depth) | fixed: the bindings are kept in a map from each name to its stack of bindings, with the order of binding beside it so that a scope is left in `O(1)` per name. Test `l01_28_staging::scaling::the_form_rule_on_many_nested_lets_calling_a_prelude_word_scales`, n = 1000; with the old code put back it fails at about 60 times |
| F27 | `testing`: the bytes each output port writes looked for among all the writes, once per port | O(ports·writes) | fixed: the first write to each port is put in a map once. No scaling test: the number of ports is the number a unit declares, and a wave writes each port once |
| F28 | `check`: the type variables of a signature collected by searching those found before (`Ty::vars_in`), and each new name, and each class, looked for in a list when the signature was named and finished | O(v²) | fixed: `Vars` keeps a set beside the order of first appearance, and the names taken and the classes of the level are sets. Test `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::a_signature_of_many_type_variables_scales`, n = 1000, which takes 4.2 s; with the old code it fails at 29 times as long for 8 times the size |
| F29 | `check` `reasons` and the notes of a failed call: each reason looked for among those shown before it | O(R²) | fixed: a set of the notes shown. Test `l01_29_diagnostics::scaling::the_reasons_of_a_class_narrowed_many_times_scale`, n = 1000, which takes 19 s; the old code passes it too, in 18 s, since comparing two notes costs little beside checking the merge that made each, so no control fails at sizes a test can run |
| F30 | `run` `step`: each step of a diagnostic shows the whole line its note is on, and finds its column by counting the scalars before it on that line | O(steps·line) | not a fault: the time is that of the output, which holds the line once per step. A unit with `n` notes on one line of length `O(n)` gives a diagnostic of `O(n²)` characters; the test of F29 puts each note on its own line |
| F31 | `check::ty` `meet` and `accepts`: each tuple shape of one type compared with each of the other | O(A·B) | not a fault: whether one union of tuple shapes lies in another is decided shape by shape by subtyping, not equality, so in the worst case every pair is compared, and `meet` can return a shape for every pair. Grouping the shapes by length would skip only pairs that cannot match |
| F32 | kernel emptiness: results kept for the whole thread, so a unit checked after an equal one looks up types equal to kept keys but built apart from them, and each lookup compares the two in full | O(n²) for a unit of size n checked after an equal one | fixed at L1.27: `check_with` first drops the kept results (`Type::forget_emptiness`), so results are shared within one unit only. Keying the results by identity instead broke the reuse within a unit that the L1.23 tuple-difference test needs (56–113 times per 8 times the size). Measured on the chain of F21, second check of the same text, old against fixed, debug build: 0.55, 2.0 and 7.8 s against 0.26, 0.43 and 0.85 s at n = 2000, 4000 and 8000. Test as F21: on the old memo the test passes in 74 s against 10.4 s, because the fastest of five runs at the larger size is the first, before an equal unit is kept, so the probe above is the control |
| F44 | scaling tests of memoized passes: `l01_10` "the text of nested patterns and lambdas", `l01_21` "running n functions' rows", `l01_23` "morphism differences" took the least of two or more runs in one process, so after the first run they measured memo hits; behind the `l01_23` case, `types/bdd.rs` `Part::forced` on an expression evaluates the whole expression twice per distinct atom | O(A·S) for `A` atoms in an expression of size `S`: quadratic in the count of differences | harness fixed (F44-memo): `tests/src/scaling.rs` runs each measured run, and the warm-up, on a new thread (`alone`, by `on_a_thread`, with the stack the caller has left, so the thread's bytes count), so the per-thread memos of zed-kernel (diagram node tables, emptiness results) start empty; tests whose closures held `Cell` or `RefCell` now hold atomics or a `Mutex`; `l01_21` builds the front end's result for each size before the timing and runs the rows on a copy of it. Fresh-run ratios: `l01_10` 7.7 times, `l01_21` under 24 (passes); the 1437 and 642 of the audit no longer reproduce after T2-parse. `l01_23` `the_parts_scale` fails on fresh runs: union of 16 against 128 morphism differences, 12.7 M against 610 M instructions, 48.0 times (limit 24); the old least-of-runs harness passes it at 0.49 M against 3.47 M, 7.0 times (negative control, run once, not committed). Probe by phase (fresh thread each): `is_empty` on the morphism union 11.0 M, 597.8 M, 36.6 G at 16, 128, 1024 differences; on the tuple union 4.8 M, 288.6 M, 18.2 G; union, complement, intersection and subtype linear. perf puts the cost in `Part::forced`. Fixed by the one-pass `forced` of step T6 (owner of `bdd.rs`, merged in 5b78cbd; ledger F44-forced): with this harness `the_parts_scale` passes, and `is_empty` on the morphism union is 3.67 M, 28.6 M, 229.7 M at 16, 128, 1024 (7.8 and 8.0 times). |
| F34 | `check`: each application of `+` (a binary operator or a step of `sum`) has a large constant cost, whatever the size of the unit | linear, with a large constant | superseded: the cost of each + node (about 1.05M instructions, T2-mid probe) was ledger item CHK-node-cost, fixed at F77 (about 140k instructions) |
| F33 | `store/product.rs` `Scope` projection: each projected formula kept looked for in the `Vec` of parts with a full structural compare | O(P²) per projection | fixed: the parts are put in a `HashSet<&Formula>` first. No test isolates it: S16's scaling test covers projection, but solving dominates its time |
| F35 | `parse` left recursion (item LR1): each growth of a left-recursive rule matches the rule again at the same token, and the Squirrel paper's bound assumes each rule match costs O(1), which a `*` or `%` loop in a rule's body breaks | as first built, every rule at the token went stale at each growth, so an alternative that failed after a long prefix walked it again: quadratic in the tokens (`(x, … x) + 1 + 1 …`, 8,000 against 1,000: 44.9 to 61.9 times, limit 24), and a ladder of k rungs quadratic in k (64 rungs against 8: 66.5 to 67.8 times). Still quadratic in the tokens: a loop written in the left-recursive rule itself, before a token that fails; a lookahead over a left-recursive rule at every token; and a cycle of two or more rules under a long list, at each growth at the list's own token | fixed for chains and ladders: the refinement at `squirrel_parser.tex:699`, taken further for rungs. An entry is marked only when its match read the seed of a rule further out, another rule read its own seed, or it read a marked entry, at its own token or a later one, and only a marked entry consults the version; a version per token keeps marked entries at other tokens good. A rule's reads of its own seed do not mark it, because that seed is finished when it returns; `Parser::low` and `Entry::depth` tell the two apart. As first built, marks stopped at a token's outermost rule, so a rule at an earlier token stayed unmarked and kept a match that a growth had made too short: on some grammars with `head`, it rejected an input or gave a wrong tree (2 cases in `leftrec.txt`). A marked entry now marks every rule on the path. `l01_08_parser::scaling`, three runs, ratio at 8 times the size, limit 24: 32,000 operators 8.6 to 9.6, 16,000 lines of chains 8.9 to 11.0, a long failed alternative 7.2 to 9.0, a lookahead before the recursion 8.9 to 9.9, a finished rung 6.4 to 8.8, a list at a later token 7.9 to 8.2, 64 rungs with the operators on the top rung 12.0 to 12.6. Each passes only with its mechanism, run one test at a time: every entry consulting the version fails the failed alternative (57.0 to 60.7), the lookahead (55.8 to 60.7), the rung (58.5 to 60.9) and the ladder (66.3 to 73.9); a rule's own reads marking it fails the rung (58.1 to 59.9) and the ladder (66.5 to 71.4); one version for all tokens fails the later token (57.2 to 57.7). On 205 million random grammar and input pairs, with `head`, `nest`, `HEAD`, lookahead and layers, acceptance and trees equal those of every entry consulting the version, with 0 differences; the same search found 12 differences in 113 million pairs against the first version. Open, a loop in the rule itself: `A = 'x'* 'q' \| A 'x' \| 'x'` is Θ(n²) (2,000 tokens 52.6 to 55.6 times 250, limit 192), since a `*` is no rule and has no memo entry, and a loop's nodes go into its parent's list, so a memo entry for it would still copy them. Moved to a rule of its own it is linear, so LR2 must write such a prefix as a rule. The same holds for a head of a cycle of two or more rules, which stays marked, when a rung above re-reads it. Open, a cycle under a list: a cycle of two or more rules marks every rule that reads it, so a list that reads one after its first item is walked again at each growth at the list's own token: `Items = INT (',' Outer)*` read by `Sum` at each growth is Θ(n²) (2,000 items and operators 60.3 to 62.7 times 250, limit 192; 6.4 to 10.5 times with marks stopping at a token's outermost rule, which is the wrong result above). A rung that is directly left-recursive does not reach this, since its own reads do not mark it. Open: `S = (&E 'x')*`, `E = E 'x' \| 'x'` is Θ(n²) (2,000 tokens 68.5 to 89.1 times 250, limit 192; the paper states the case), since each lookahead grows its own match over the rest of the input and no memo entry covers another start. The grammar has no left-recursive rule yet, so nothing reaches it; `&('{' Item)` (syntax.peg:138) and `!(Name ':') Expr` (:123) need a scaling test after LR2 |
| F36 | `parse` over the left-recursive grammar of sec:grammar (item LR2): the AST builder walks each chain; lookaheads `&('{' Item)` and `!(Name ':') Expr` read rules that grow | none found: chains, a call's arguments, a block's statements, whole programs and nested blocks are linear, each measured at n and 8n in `l01_08_parser/grammar_scaling.rs`, with one constant-factor cost: a rung tries its growth once more at each operand, so on the 67 `.zed` files of the tree a parse takes 1.2 to 1.7 times as long in the dev profile (86 to 102 ms before, 102 to 143 ms after, under load), a long chain about 1.4 to 2 times as long, and gives 2.6 times fewer nodes | linear. Negative controls run: a version bump of the whole table at each growth (58 to 75 times for 8x, or over 58 s) and no reuse of memo entries (over 58 s on nested blocks and on a long call). Recursion on nesting is unchanged (200 parentheses pass and 300 overflow a 2 MiB stack, before and after) and left to T2-parse, row F37 |
