| F39 | reference 14 (`zed/corpus/ref/src/p14.rs` and `p14/delaunay.rs`, single linkage to k clusters) | O(L + n log n · M(b)) bit operations, L the input bytes, b the bits of the largest coordinate and M(b) the cost of multiplying b-bit integers: O(n log n) operations on integers of at most 4b + 8 bits (the in-circle determinant), and storage of O(n) words and O(nb + n log n) bits. A Delaunay triangulation of the distinct points, by the divide and conquer of Guibas and Stolfi with exact integer orientation and in-circle tests, holds every pair two clusters can meet by at its distance, since the closed disk on such a pair holds no other point; a chain joins equal points. The pairs are sorted by squared distance, and the merges at each distance are a search by least label over the clusters its pairs join, by a union–find by size. The mesh reuses deleted edges' slots, so it holds at most 6n edges while two halves merge and 3n - 6 after. Every input is accepted; the output and every error message are those of the O(n²) form | closed by P14-MST after cross-review xp14. `m0_04_corpus_references` keeps the O(n²) form as the test oracle (`p14_oracle.rs`) and finds 0 differences in output and result on 523,137 inputs of up to 5 points of a 3 by 3 grid in every order with every k from -1 to n + 1, 6,000 random inputs of up to 40 points (many equal, some above 64 bits) and 15 malformed ones; `clustering_start_order_uses_least_label` and `clustering_errors_by_the_count_of_points_and_clusters` fix the tie rule and the error table. `scaling::clustering_of_*`, each checked against the oracle at 1,000 points, in instructions at 8,000 against 1,000 (limit 24): near a line 8.26, grid 8.89, rotated grid 9.29, many equal points 8.60 (six distinct points, each about n/6 times; P14-dup-scale), one line 8.08, one circle 7.92, two parabolas facing apart 8.31, two parabolas by turns 10.48, random 10.51. A call costs O(a + v log v) for `a` arguments and `v` bounded variables: the arguments each variable stands at are grouped in one pass. A function type nested d deep passed as itself (TYPE-forms repair 3): `fits_in` once walked each signature's parameters at each depth to find which bounded variables a parameter holds, and rewrote the whole expected signature at each depth to rename its variables, O(d^2). | F69 | literal adoption (LIT-adopt): `check::adopted`, the one place an expected type decides a literal's carrier, at a parameter and in a tuple at any depth; `Ck::equal`, which pairs the two sides of `==` component by component; the checker's per-expression type record (`Ck::seen`) and the data check of a written tuple (`walk::tuple_step`, `Ck::data`) | O(n) for an argument or a comparison of n nodes: `adopted` and `equal` walk the written tuple with their own stacks and borrow each level's component types; a tuple written out keeps no type of its own in `seen` (its type is rebuilt from its components' only when a derivation shows it, `Ck::tuple_seen`); a tuple written as a component of a tuple is not checked again as data, since it checked its own components. Three quadratic costs on a tuple nested d deep were found and fixed, all O(d^2): `data` cloned the type and walked each nested tuple once per enclosing level (from main), `seen` cloned each level's type (from main), and the first form of `equal` cloned each level's types | fixed. `l01_26_type_resolution::literals::adopting_the_literals_of_a_wide_tuple_scales`, a comparison, a call and a `sum` of n literals, 64 and 512: bytes 7.9x (13.3 MB to 105.4 MB, load 30). `adopting_the_literals_of_a_deep_tuple_scales`, a tuple nested n deep compared and passed, 128 and 1,024: bytes 7.9x (8.9 MB to 70.3 MB, load 30); limit 24x. Both build and resolve their inputs before timing and time `check::check` alone. Negative controls, run once and not committed: `data` and `tuple_step` as on main fail the deep test at 32 and 256 with 141.7 times the bytes (29.1 MB to 4,118 MB; limit 24); `seen` keeping each tuple's type fails it at 128 and 1,024 with 26.2 times the bytes (13.2 MB to 347.4 MB); `equal` copying each level's types, 26.2 times. On main, checking `y = ((…(0.0, 0.0)…), 0.0);` took 0.18 s at depth 64 and 13.8 s at 512; now 15 ms and 128 ms. Load 30 to 69. Repair (LIT-adopt repair#1). (a) Several tuple shapes of one length (`(1, 2)` at `(f64, f64) \| (String, f64)`): `adopted` keeps, at each open tuple, the expected shapes of that length borrowed from the expected type, and decides a literal's carrier from that component across them (`carriers_in`); no union type is built, so the cost is O(n) times the shapes of that length at each component. `adopting_at_a_union_of_many_tuple_shapes_scales`, 64 and 512 shapes: bytes 7.9x (5.0 MB to 39.8 MB, load 37). (b) The reduced product's and the preimage catalog's reading of a literal that follows its carrier (`store/product.rs` `plain_test`, `plain_comparison`, the `let` path of `forward_in`; `store/preimage.rs` `Node::Follow`): the value compared is found once per test, as in the plain comparison; `plain_comparison` reads the test as the plain one only where the value is a name at an integer, and a compound value is left as written, which the catalog reads as the plain test. `l01_39_reduced_product::follow::a_chain_of_following_comparisons_costs_what_the_plain_chain_costs`, a chain of 48 following tests against the same chain with `n: int`: 731 ms and 699 ms, bytes 1.0x (limit 3x); `following_tests_scale`, n and 8n: a conjunction of following tests of a name, 32 and 256, bytes 10.9x (6.6 ms to 73 ms); a chain of bindings of following tests, 64 and 512, 7.5x (2.8 ms to 21.8 ms); a conjunction of following tests of a difference, 16 and 128, 9.5x (31 ms to 231 ms); load 39 to 47; limit 24x. Negative control, run once and not committed: `plain_comparison` forwarding the compared value again for each test it is read in cost 2^d on the chain: d = 8, 10, 12 took 0.46 s, 1.87 s, 6.19 s against 13, 20, 27 ms plain, with 30.6, 82.4 and 230.6 times the bytes; at d = 48 the test was stopped at 300 s. The plain chain is itself superlinear (F70). Repair (LIT-adopt repair#2): `==` pairs tuple components across every tuple shape of the length a side holds, as `adopted` does (`sides` keeps, per component, its types in each shape, borrowed), and a literal operand decides by `carriers_in`; cost O(n) times the shapes of that length at each component. `adopting_at_a_union_of_many_tuple_shapes_scales`, now with a `p == (1, 2)` at the n shapes too, 64 and 512 shapes: bytes 7.9x (6.1 MB to 48.2 MB), time 8.5x, load 11; width 7.9x (13.0 MB to 103.2 MB), depth 7.9x (8.4 MB to 66.1 MB); limit 24x. Negative control, run once and not committed: `sides` removing duplicate component types by a linear search (O(s^2) in the shapes) failed the shapes test at 64 and 512 with 48.6 times the bytes (23.1 MB to 1,121 MB), load 17. Repair (LIT-deep-instr): the deep test times the check of the whole program, about 357,000 instructions a level, most outside `==`, so a `sides` that walks each level's whole type for variables, O(d^2) and allocating nothing, passed it at 128 and 1,024 with 9.1 times the instructions. `pairing_the_sides_of_a_deep_comparison_scales` builds the operands of `p == ((…(0, 0)…, 0), 0)` before the timing (`check::Operands`, hidden, for tests) and times `Ck::equal`'s pairing alone (`Ck::pair`), 2,048 and 16,384 deep: 8.0 times the instructions (217M to 1,734M), bytes within the limit, load 40 to 62. The pairing spends about 106,000 instructions a level, most in `class_of_ty` of each literal's carrier, so smaller depths do not show an O(d^2) walk. | F83 | `stage::free`, the effects of a part (`stage::color`, `passed`) and `stage::mix` on a term whose parts are shared (STAGE-dag, after TERM-id): each read the term as a tree, so a part held in one `Shared` node reached from k places was read k times, and `mix` gave each place its own copy, so the mixed term was a tree too. On d nested `if`s whose two arms are one node (2^d places, 2d + 2 distinct parts) that is 2^d | was 2^d on that family (4 times per 2 levels); now O(P log P) for `free` and the effects, P the distinct parts, and for `mix` O(Q log Q), Q the pairs of a distinct part and a map of the names it reads to their bindings that a place reaches it under | fixed; printing a term with shared parts is open as the ledger item STAGE-print (end of this cell): `Frees::summary` reads each node once on an explicit stack, keyed by `Shared::id` as `Reader::ids` is (a part held inline is read with the node that holds it), and keeps for it the set of names it reads (the hash-consed tries of `Sets`) and whether it reads a port. `Mixer::mix` keys what a node mixes to by its id and `Maps::restrict(top, names it reads)`, a hash-consed Patricia map from name to binding number restricted to those names, so two places with the same bindings of those names get one mixed node; the build test and `Let`'s drop read only that summary, and each (part, bindings) pair is recorded in the report once. Complexity tradeoff (stage.rs, `Maps`): a restriction costs the pairs of map and set nodes no earlier restriction read, with no bound in the term size proved, as for a union of `Sets`. Every binding not known at build (a `let` bound to a run-stage term, a parameter) has one number, `UNKNOWN`, since mixing leaves such a name as it is and evaluates nothing it reads, so two of them cannot mix a part to different terms; A binding known at build is numbered by what it is (`Mixer::number`): a literal by its value (a `BTreeMap<Value, Binding>`, `Value`'s order telling the number kinds apart), any other term by its `Shared::id` and the restricted map of the names it reads where it is bound, so two bindings with one number evaluate alike. Repair 2 (verifier's finding): before this each known binding got a new number from a counter, so on `main = (c) -> let x = 0; n_d`, `n_j = if c { let x = x + a; n_(j-1) } else { let x = x + b; n_(j-1) }` with one body node below both `let`s, `mix` took 9.2, 37, 153, 850 ms at d = 8, 10, 12, 14 and made 519, 2057, 8203, 32781 nodes, all literals (verifier's probe, load 40 to 63). Now, debug build, load 18 to 28: a = b = 0 gives 2d + 2 nodes and 32, 270, 2328 ms at d = 1000, 8000, 64000; a = 1, b = 2 gives d(d + 7)/2 nodes, one per value of `x` at each level, the size of the output needed, and 37, 149, 593, 2425, 10255 ms at d = 50 to 800 (4 times per 2 times d; perf: 29% `Evaluator::run` of the per-literal `eval`, a constant per output node). Tests `mixing_nested_lets_of_a_built_value_over_one_body_scales` (a = b = 0 at d = 1000 and 8000, power 1; a = 1, b = 2 at d = 50 and 400, power 2, the output being quadratic) and `..._gives_what_the_tree_gives` (d = 0 to 8 for (a, b) = (0, 0), (1, 2), (1, 1), (2, -2): printed term and report equal to the tree form's, node counts exact). Negative control for repair 2, run once and not committed, the counter restored in place (load 17 to 44): the output test failed at d = 2 (9 nodes, 6 expected) and the scaling test was killed at 60 s. Repair 3 (verifier's finding, was STAGE-fn): a function bound on each path to a term reading its earlier binding, `let f = (y) -> f(y) + 1` and `let f = (y) -> f(y) + 2` on the two arms of each of d nested `if`s over one body, gave 2^d different function bindings, so the mixed term had 2^d nodes (1298, 5144, 20510, 81956 nodes and 38, 89, 365, 2000 ms at d = 8, 10, 12, 14, load 55 to 74). Now a function binding gets a coarse number by its kind (lambda or not, arity, whether it ends), and the memo keys a part by its id and that coarse map to a trie of the questions mixing it asked of its bindings (`Probe`: a call `f(key)` or "is this the same binding"), each edge an answer (`Answer`); a part is evaluated with each function binding replaced by a stub lambda that reads a lookup port (`Stub`, `Port`, `Run`), so the questions it asks are logged, and a later place walks the trie with its own bindings' answers (`Mixer::recall`) and shares the leaf when every answer agrees (`Mixer::remember`). The questions a part asks are replayed into the part that holds it, so an outer part's trie holds its inner parts' questions too. On the family above this gives (3d^2 + 17d - 6)/2 nodes, exact at d = 2, 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 48, 96 (20 to 14637 nodes; 161 at d = 8), and 5, 10, 19, 42, 167, 671 ms at d = 8, 12, 16, 24, 48, 96 (debug, load 24). That quadratic is what the output needs (repair 4, after verify 4): `c` is run-stage, so every path stays in the mixed term; the `if` at depth k (k = 0 to d - 1) is reached under k + 1 functions `y + s`, s = k to 2k the sum of the steps above it, and the leftmost literal below it is 1 + s + d - k, so its k + 1 mixed forms differ as terms and no sharing can merge them. The output so holds exactly d(d + 1)/2 distinct `if`s (verifier 4 and repair 4 counted them by structure, hash-consed bottom-up, at d = 1 to 32: equal to d(d + 1)/2 and to the count by id), and (3d^2 + 17d - 6)/2 = 3 d(d + 1)/2 + 7d - 3. The node-count scaling test therefore allows power 2. Complexity tradeoff (stage.rs, `Mixer::eval`): a part is mixed once per distinct sequence of answers its questions get, and each answer is a value computed at build, so a family whose answers differ on every path still has as many mixed parts as answers; a part whose question or answer is not a value cheap to compare (a string not yet written out, a float zero, whose `==` and order disagree, a family or atom) is evaluated as written and numbered exactly, losing the sharing but not the result (`compared`, `Lookup::refuses`). Kernel change: the `Lookup` trait has a new default method `refuses(key)`, asked before the key is compared with keys read so far, since `Text`'s order writes a rope out in full. Tests: `mixing_lets_of_composed_functions_over_one_body_scales` (check_order at d = 12 and 8d, power 2, node count asserted equal to (3d^2 + 17d - 6)/2; the #[ignore]d test is removed), `..._gives_what_the_tree_gives` (unchanged), `a_call_the_stubs_cannot_answer_is_mixed_as_written` (`f(-0.0)` with `f = (y) -> 1.0 / y` gives -inf) and `a_call_at_a_long_join_is_mixed_as_written` (a string doubled 62 times passed to a stubbed `f`). Negative controls, each run once and not committed: stubs disabled, 818, 3136, 12366, 49244 nodes and 14, 49, 209, 906 ms at d = 8, 10, 12, 14 (load 21), the scaling test failed; `refuses` taking every key, the long-join test aborted ("memory allocation of 4611686018427387904 bytes failed"); float zero allowed as a key, the float-zero test failed (dag.rs:504). Repair 1 (verifier's finding): before this, each unknown binding got a new number, so on `main = (x, c) -> n_d`, `n_j = if c { let x = x + 1; n_(j-1) } else { let x = x + 2; n_(j-1) }` with one body node below both `let`s, `mix` took 6.3, 61, 1028 ms at d = 8, 12, 16 and made 1284, 20488, 327692 distinct nodes, all printing the same text; now 6d + 1 nodes (97 at d = 16). Tests `mixing_nested_lets_of_a_parameter_over_one_body_scales` (d = 1000 and 8000, `check_order` power 1) and `mixing_nested_lets_of_a_parameter_over_one_body_gives_what_the_tree_gives` (d = 0 to 8: printed term and report equal to the tree form's, 6d + 1 nodes). Negative control for the repair, run once and not committed, `stage.rs` of 3a1335eb (load 45 to 50): the scaling test did not end in 90 s, and the output test failed at d = 2 (18 nodes, 13 expected). Tests in `l01_28_staging::dag`: `mixing_nested_ifs_whose_arms_are_one_part_scales` (d = 1000 and 8000, `check_order` power 1, the mixed term 2d + 2 distinct nodes) and `reading_nested_ifs_whose_arms_are_one_part_scales` (`free`, `color`, `passed`, the same sizes); `mixing_nested_ifs_whose_arms_are_one_part_gives_what_the_tree_gives` (d = 0 to 10, the shared and the tree form: equal free names, colors, values passed, printed mixed term and report) and `the_effects_of_a_part_shared_by_both_arms_are_read` (a port read under 2^12 places makes the definition run-stage). Probe, debug build, load 32: d = 1000, 8000, 64000 gave `free` 1.2, 11, 60 ms, `color` 1.0, 11, 71 ms and `mix` 16, 174, 1280 ms (10.9 and 7.3 times per 8 times d); at d = 20, `mix` 0.42 ms. Negative control, run once and not committed, the old `stage.rs` at 5e409d79 on the same family, load 32 to 37: d = 10, 14, 16, 18, 20 gave `free` 0.20, 3.2, 7.5, 32, 116 ms, `color` 0.32, 7.3, 19, 77, 284 ms and `mix` 42 ms, 0.37, 1.39, 5.56, 23.8 s; the new `dag` scaling tests did not end in 60 s, and the output test failed at d = 1 (6 distinct nodes in the mixed term, 4 expected). Repair 5 (verify 4's finding): `Mixer::enter` recorded a build-stage term left to run in `Report::left` by `t.to_string()`, which prints the term as a tree, so on d nested build-stage `if`s whose two arms are one node over `w(w)` (left to run: its form does not show that it ends) the report took 2^d time and text: 2.3, 36, 143, 589, 2238 ms and 30.7 KB, 492 KB, 1.97 MB, 7.86 MB, 31.5 MB at d = 10, 14, 16, 18, 20 (repair 4, release build, load 9; verifier 4: 3.1 to 2855 ms, 40 KB to 41 MB). Now `Report::left` is `Vec<(Shared, String)>`: each entry holds the term, its parts still shared, and its reason, not its text (orchestrator's decision; the DESIGN.md L1.28 note asks that the report give each term left to run with its reason, and nothing reads the text: `run::stage` has no caller outside tests). An entry costs a `Shared` clone when the term is held in a node, and otherwise one `Shared::new` over a clone of the term held inline, the clone `as_written` makes for the mixed term already. `Display` and the derived `Debug` of a report still print each term as a tree; tests format a report only in the message of a failed assertion, and the determinism test `l01_28_staging::ids` formats the reports of small units. On the same family, test profile (optimized), load 9 to 14: `mix` with its report 1.26, 10.3, 108.5 ms at d = 1000, 8000, 64000. Test `reporting_a_term_left_to_run_whose_arms_are_one_part_scales` (was #[ignore]d): `check_order` power 1 at d = 1000 and 8000, the entry equal by structure to the term `td` built (each `Shared` compared first by id, so O(1)) and its reason; and at d = 1 the entry's text, `if (1 >= 1) { w(w) } else { w(w) }`. Negative control for repair 5, run once and not committed, `black_box(t.to_string())` added back at the call site (load 9 to 21): `mix` 0.99, 25.1, 107.8, 468, 1788 ms at d = 10, 14, 16, 18, 20, and the scaling test at d = 1000 did not end in 60 s. Verify 6: tests `l01_28_staging::differential` (12 cases) mix one part reached under bindings that differ in what the build knows (two literals; a literal and a parameter; a test node under a name rebound between its places; functions that answer alike or not; one function node over a function that differs; a part that rebinds a function it reads; shared parts inside a shared part; a function over a literal that differs; a call at a float zero, run as written; a part under a lambda or a match arm that binds a name it reads; parts left to run) as built and as its tree form, and check that the printed mixed terms, the reports and the residual program's values agree; each part reads the parameter `c`, so it is mixed and its memo is reached, not evaluated whole with the term around it. Negative controls, each run once and not committed (load 10 to 21): every answer to "is this the same binding" made one value, only the float-zero case failed, no earlier test did; the memo key without the bindings of the names a part reads, 5 of these cases and 2 `dag` tests failed; the answers of calls not recorded, 5 of these and 2 `dag` tests failed. The six `dag` scaling tests against `stage.rs` and `eval.rs` of 61f2250d: five did not end in 60 s, and the composed one made 28665 nodes at d = 12 (315 expected). The staged output `l01_28_staging::ids` prints is at most the size of its source on each of its 9 units (largest 2108 bytes, 11 ms). Open (STAGE-print): printing a term with shared parts in user-writable syntax within O(n log n) text and time, for `Report::left` and for `Term`'s `Display` and `Debug` in general. Why it is open: Zed has no form that names a part at its first place for later places to use; the forms that can name one are `x = e; body` and a lambda. A `let` placed above every place of a shared part is right only when each name the part reads resolves to one binder at all its places; on the composed family the part below each `if` reads `f`, bound differently on each arm, so it must be a lambda over such names, called at each place with them as arguments (lambda lifting), and the arguments written are the sum over places of the names lifted, which lambda lifting can make quadratic (a part reading k let-bound names at m places of one context and one place of another, (m + 1) k names on about 2k + m nodes, unless the m places share one `let` inside that context). An O(n log n) printing would need both: a `let` per part and context where the binders agree, and a lambda where they differ, chosen without blowing up either; no such algorithm is in the codebase. |
| WORDS-minmax | the prelude words `min` and `max` and the later-layer type table (CORPUS-words): `resolve::WORDS` and `resolve::LATER_TYPES` lookups, `check::word_arity`, and `Prim::Min`/`Prim::Max` in the kernel | O(1) per use: the word tables are constant slices of a few dozen entries, searched once per name that no scope binds; a call checks as any call of a three-arrow overload of two parameters, and the kernel compares two values once (a big integer or a rat in time linear in its digits, as every comparison) | fixed by design; no scaling test, since each use is O(1) beside the call it sits in |
| ENDS | `stage::ends`, the form rule that a term ends (build stage, `store/preimage.rs`, `store/product.rs`, `mutate`): read as a tree with the names bound around each place, so a chain of k `if`s whose two arms hold one shared part cost 2^k | was O(2^k) on k levels of shared arms | fixed: each distinct part (one `Shared` node, known by its id, row K44) is read once by an explicit stack, after its own parts, and gets what it needs of the names bound around it: none (it never ends) or the set of names it calls that must be free or bound by `let` to a function that ends; a binder reads that set, so one part shared under two bindings of a name still gets two answers. The sets are hash-consed Patricia tries (Okasaki and Gill) with a memo of the unions of pairs, so equal sets are one number and a union reads only the paths where its sets differ from a pair joined before; O(P · 32) for P distinct parts on the families below. No bound in the term's size is proved for the number of pairs of trie nodes the unions read (the `Complexity tradeoff:` comment on `Sets`). Checked: `l01_28_staging::ends`, four scaling tests at k = 1000 and 8000, terms built before timing: shared arms (probe 10.9M and 86.9M instructions, 7.97 times), arms calling two different `let` functions each level (59.1M and 526.6M, 8.9 times; 6.2G at k = 2000 and 109G at k = 8000 with unshared ordered sets, quadratic), k `let` functions called at the end, and two chains of calls joined at every level with interleaved name numbers (77.8M and 817.6M, 10.5 times; 0.93G and 56.6G without the memo of unions, quadratic), load 15 to 60. The new reading agrees with the tree walk on 200,000 seeded random terms with shared parts (160,359 end, 0 differ; two planted bugs gave 3,449 and 673 differences). Negative controls, run once and not committed: without the visited check (each shared part read again) shared arms take 16.5M, 66M, 264M, 1.05G and 4.2G instructions at k = 12, 14, 16, 18 and 20 (4 times per 2 levels), and the shared-arms and two-function tests are killed at 60 s; without the memo of unions the joined-chains test is killed at 60 s and the other three pass |
| F74 | `check::handled` (ISSUE1-else-atoms): the atoms each undeclared parameter's uses handle (`p else d`, `p else #a -> d`, `p is #a`), from `Resolution::handles`, through the names bound to it (`s = line`, `Resolution::aliases`); and the origin notes of a binding and a narrowing (`Origin::ty`) | O(B + A α(B) + H log H) for B binders, A aliases and H such uses: a union-find over the binders (by size, path halving, then one compression pass) joins the aliases, then one pass over the uses joins each atom set into its class's persistent set; an origin note keeps its type and formats it only when a message shows it, O(1) per binding; `param_ty` and `handled_tys` add one set per parameter at the end of a function or lambda | checked: `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::a_parameter_tested_against_many_atoms_scales`, a body testing one parameter against n distinct atoms, n = 400 and 3200, checked from source: 8.0 times the bytes (20.96M and 168.1M), load 39 to 45. Negative control, run once and not committed: the parameter's set rebuilt as the union of all its uses at each use failed at 400 (72.9 times the bytes, limit 24). Also `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::a_parameter_bound_to_a_chain_of_names_scales`, a parameter bound through a chain of n names written last first, each tested against its own atom, n = 400 and 3200: 8.0 times the bytes (26.8M and 214.8M) and 8.0 times the instructions (227.0M and 1819.9M), load about 50. Negative controls, run once and not committed: the origin notes formatting their type eagerly (the code before this change) failed at 45.9 times the bytes (83.0M and 3806.9M); a union that relabels every binder of the smaller class passed at 9.5 times the instructions, as expected: each binder is relabeled at most log B times, since its class at least doubles at each relabel, so that union is O(B log B), not quadratic, and that run was no negative control (an earlier text of this row called it O(A B)) |
| F75 | `check::outside_of` and `Ck::first_taking` (ISSUE1-else-atoms, repair 2): the loose atoms that reach each value from elsewhere than a handled parameter (`Ck::outside`, kept per expression span beside `unnamed` and `made`), used by `handled_sig` to give a function two arrows when the atoms its body handles also reach its result; and the choice of the first arrow that takes a call's arguments | one `KType` union per part of each expression, once in the walk's post-order, and each set is cut to the loose atoms of that expression's own type, so the work is at most that of the unions that already form the types of the parts (O(E) unions for E expressions; the sets grow to n atoms in the test below, and its 8.0 times the bytes shows that a union does not copy them each time); `handled_sig` reads the body's set once; `first_taking` tests each arrow's parameters once, O(A P) for A arrows (at most 2 here) and P parameters; repair 3: `outside_arrow` selects, for a call of a two-arrow function, the arrow its arguments' outside sets would take (`Ck::taking`, shared with `first_taking`), O(a log a + A P) for a arguments, the named ones looked up in a map | checked: `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::a_chain_of_atoms_from_elsewhere_than_the_parameter_scales`, a chain of n names each passing on the one before it and adding its own atom, n = 200 and 1600, checked from source: 8.0 times the bytes (61.2M and 489.2M), measured with the limit lowered for one run and restored. Negative controls, run once and not committed: formatting the set as text at each lookup failed at 33.9 times the bytes (115.6M and 3922.3M, limit 24); a linear scan of the map, an `is_empty` test on the loose set and a per-union `subtype` test each passed, since the checker's own per-node cost hides them at these sizes. Also `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::a_chain_of_functions_passing_a_handled_parameter_on_scales` (repair 3), n functions each handling `x` with `else` and passing it to the one before, n = 200 and 1600: 8.0 times the bytes (106.4M and 851.8M), load 38, measured with the limit lowered for one run and restored. Negative control, run once and not committed: every outside set cloned and formatted at each call (a re-walk of the earlier bodies per call) failed at 35.4 times the bytes (207.9M and 7353.3M, limit 24); a scan of the map with no allocation passed. Repair 4: a method call `x.r()`, `x?.r()` and a call through a local name of a function or a lambda (`f = r; f(x)`) take the same arrow selection, the callee resolved by `Ck::called` (one map lookup, O(log n)) and the receiver placed in the first parameter (empty for `?.`) and passed on as a part; `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::a_chain_of_functions_passing_a_handled_parameter_on_as_a_receiver_scales`, the same chain written `x.h0()`, n = 200 and 1600: 8.0 times the bytes (100.4M and 803.3M), and `many_calls_passing_a_handled_parameter_on_scale`, one function binding n calls `r(x)` or `x.r()`: 7.9 times the instructions (311.6M and 2463.6M for `r(x)`), each measured with the limit lowered for one run and restored, load 40 to 48. Negative controls, run once and not committed: a clone of `Ck::seen` per call in `outside_arrow` failed both chain tests at 31.3 and 30.5 times the bytes (limit 24); a linear scan of the uses map in `called` and a clone of the outside map per call passed every test, since the checker spends about 1.2M instructions per call `a = r(x);` (also with a one-arrow `r`), which hides them at these sizes. Repair 5: `outside_arrow` is replaced by a record per call (`Ck::tried`, by span, taken when the call's expression is seen): a call inside a function with a widened parameter whose argument holds a loose atom is applied once more on the arguments cut to their atoms from outside (`Ck::try_outside`, an undo-log trial: the undo is linear in the trial's own writes, and the trial checks the signature on argument types already found, with no walk of the argument expressions), so each such call costs at most two applications; a call with no such argument records its own result's atoms, O(1); a need replayed at a call records nothing. This covers every callee form the checker can call, `x.r`, `ap(r, x)` and a computed callee among them. `many_calls_passing_a_handled_parameter_on_scale`, n calls in one function, n = 200 and 1600: 7.6 times the bytes for `x.r` (52.5M and 399.2M), 8.0 for `ap(r, x)` (118.5M and 944.1M), 8.0 for `(if true { r } else { r })(x)` (91.7M and 730.0M); `a_chain_of_method_calls_without_parentheses_scales`, `x.r.r` … n deep, n = 1000 and 8000: 8.0 times the bytes (224.1M and 1789.7M); each measured with the limit lowered for one run and restored, load 22 to 25. Negative controls, run once and not committed: the trial repeated once per 64 entries of the outside map (work of a re-check of the earlier calls at each call) failed `many_calls_passing_a_handled_parameter_on_scale` at 31.3 times the bytes (90.9M and 2842.8M, limit 24) and ran the chain test past its 60 s limit; a clone of the outside map per trial, and of the class table, passed, since the checker spends about 1.4M instructions per call of the chain (debug build), which hides them at these sizes |
| F76 | `Ck::refused`, `Ck::pass_at`, `Ck::pass_needs` (ISSUE1-else-atoms, repair 6): per-atom pass-by (rule 6 of sec:type_resolution), the atoms of an argument that every arrow omits pass by and the rest stay in the argument, recorded per argument span in `Info::passes`; a need replayed at a call (a lambda through a higher-order parameter) records the atoms it refuses (`Ck::refused`, `Ck::need_passed`) and the outer call passes them by at the argument whose parameter holds the need's type variables | `refused` is one set difference per arrow, O(A) for A arrows; `pass_at` reads and writes one map entry of at most one pair per argument, O(log n + a) for a arguments; `need_passed` holds only the entries of the call being applied (truncated after each), and `pass_needs` drains them once, O(e P) for e entries and P parameters | checked: `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::many_calls_passing_some_atoms_of_their_argument_by_scale`, one function binding n calls `h(v)` (and n calls `ap((positional y) -> trim(y), v)`), n = 1,000 and 8,000 (ISSUE1-passby-scale; was 200 and 1,600), asserting that each run records n pass-bys: `h(v)` 8.0 times the instructions (535.0M and 4,270.1M) and 8.0 the bytes (118.6M and 947.4M), the `ap` call 8.0 and 8.0 (1,274.7M and 10,194.2M instructions, 232.3M and 1,856.9M bytes), 15.8 s for both at load 51 to 56; `a_chain_of_functions_each_testing_an_atom_of_its_own_scales`, n = 100 and 800: 7.9 times the bytes (35.0M and 276.3M), measured with the limit lowered for one run and restored, load 26 to 34. Negative control, run once and not committed: a clone of `Info::passes` at each `pass_at` fails the `h(v)` call at n = 1,000 and 8,000, 38.4 times the instructions (1,144.8M and 44,018.0M) and 32.4 times the bytes (210.2M and 6,810.8M), 48 s, load 52 to 65; at 200 and 1,600 it gave 18.6 and 15.3 times and passed, since each call checks in about 500,000 instructions, which hid the copy at the old sizes. The `ap` call with the copy runs past 60 s at 1,000, so that call is not shown to catch it. The `pass_at` bound above is wrong: it copies the call's own entries for each argument, O(a^2) for one call of a arguments (F94; open item PASS-at-args) |
| BODY-display | `ast::Builder::body_block` (`ast/body.rs`, row "Display in body position") | O(1) per brace: the check reads the `Block` node's alternative and, for a block, its one child and that child's rule; a rejection lists every node's parent once, in one walk of the tree by an explicit stack (`OnceCell`), O(n), and stops the build | none: constant work per brace, and the walk runs at most once per unit |
| KINDS | the kind-wide types `#_`, `(..)`, `{..}`, `#a(..)` and `#a()` (L1.40d): the lexer, the parser's message for a `..` in a list (`parse::dots_in_list`), the AST builder, resolve, `check::Denote`, `elab::kernel_form`, the checker's `pieces`, `sub_in`, `meet`, `diff`, `contains` and `meets` on a type with a kind-wide part, and the kernel printer (`types/print.rs`) | O(1) per token for the lexer and the message (two neighbors of the furthest token); O(n) per type of n nodes in the builder, resolve and `Denote`; in the checker, one kernel operation per tuple shape against the kind-wide part in `meet`, `diff` and `meets`, so linear in the shapes, and `sub_in` decides the kind-wide part by the kernel once, as it does a tuple piece; the printer writes `(..)`, `{..}`, `#_` and `#a(..)` in place of the words it wrote before, at no extra cost | checked: `l01_22_type_representation::kinds::a_union_of_kind_wide_and_exact_members_scales` (a union of n kind-wide differences and exact tuples and atoms, built, printed and read back by `check::read::parse_type`, n = 256 → 2,048: 8.1 times the bytes, within 24) and `narrowing_tuple_shapes_by_a_kind_wide_type_scales` (`meet` and `diff` of n tuple shapes with `(..) \ (0, 0)`, 256 → 2,048). Negative controls, run once and not committed: a quadratic loop of re-printing in the first test gave 36.4 times the bytes; `meet` reading every shape once per shape gave 62.2 times the bytes. Not changed: the pre-existing O(T1·T2) pairing of the two sides' tuple shapes in `Ty::meet` |
| BINDER-type | `resolve::Resolver::bind` and `names_type` (row "Binder naming a type") | O(1) per binder: the name is compared with the seven L1 types, a constant table; each rejection is one error with two notes, and the errors are sorted once, O(n log n) | `l01_29_diagnostics::binder_type::rejecting_many_binders_that_name_types_scales` (3n binders, n = 2000 against 16000) |
| CMDLINE | the `CommandLine` port (step L1.19a): `run::words` splits the command line at its first `--` and decodes each word as UTF-8, `run::arguments` builds the family keyed by position from 1, the record writes and reads one `arg` entry per argument, and a `main` row's `String` is split into arguments by `testing::given_args` | O(n log n) for n arguments of total length L: one pass to find `--` and one to decode, O(L); `Family::of` sorts the keys once; the record and the test-row split are O(L) | fixed by design: `l01_19a_command_line::scaling::a_run_with_many_arguments_scales` (`zedc run` echoing 2,000 and 16,000 arguments: 7.5 times the bytes allocated, within the limit of 24 in instructions and bytes). Negative control, run once and not committed: a quadratic `words` (each word compared with every earlier one) took 35.4 times the instructions, past the limit of 24 |
| F77 | `check` (CHK-tuple, CHK-lambda, CHK-node-cost, CHK-carrier): nested tuples, nested lambdas, and the constant of each `+`, negation and call | was n^3 for nested tuples (106M instructions per node at n = 400), n^2 for nested lambdas, and about 1.1M instructions per `+`, 390k per negation, 340k per call | fixed: each signature computes the variables it holds at any depth once (`ty::Facts`, a `OnceLock` that `Clone` resets and `Eq` and `Hash` ignore; no memo keyed on pointers), so `Ty::replace` leaves a function type unchanged without walking it when no variable it binds is inside, and `generalize` starts naming at the first free name instead of searching the names taken from T each time; `Type::split` reads every part of a kernel type at once in `numeric_carrier` and `mask_of`, replacing one meet per carrier; `without_loose` skips the set difference when the set has no atoms. Now about 140k instructions per `+`, 65k per negation, 80k per call, 39k per nested tuple and 62k per nested lambda, linear. Tests `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::{tuples_nested_8000_deep_check_in_linear_time, lambdas_nested_8000_deep_check_in_linear_time, a_variable_nested_in_tuples_checks_in_linear_time}` and the sum tests, n and 8n, limit 24; at b0a733ff they fail at 213, 54 and 202 times the bytes. Open: a held function at the bottom of n nested tuples is still n^2, because each error derivation's notes are O(n) characters; the binding text of `Ck::bind` prints each type eagerly, and `Ty::write` recursion overflows a 2 MiB stack below 8000 deep. Repair 1 (CHK-cost): the facts at first kept a class list and a hash set copied at each level, and `Ty::replace` rebuilt every inner signature with empty facts, so curried lambdas that use every parameter, `g(positional a0) = (a1) -> ... -> (an) -> (a0, ..., an);`, cost about n^2.8 (139.9G instructions at n = 1000 against 11.4G at b0a733ff). Now both sets of `VarFacts` are persistent `imbl::OrdSet`s merged small into large, a variable is inserted only when absent, `Sig::first_seen` walks the signature once and skips any function whose class set misses the wanted classes, `Ty::replace` returns a leaf it does not bind unchanged, and `solving` binds only the classes the signature holds: 0.75G at n = 250 and 12.4G at n = 1000 (old 0.75G and 11.4G), n^2 as before, because substitution was eager: at level k the new name replaced class a_k in each of the n - k inner signatures that hold it. Fixed in CHK-curried: `generalize` keeps each type variable as its class variable `$k`, stores the name on the class (`Class::name`), and replaces only the classes listed for its own level; `Ck::is_class` tells a named class from an open one, and `finish` writes the names once per signature shown (`Ck::shown`, `Sig::renamed`). Now 15.7M instructions at n = 125 and 130.6M at n = 1000 (2.8M and 22.9M bytes), 8.3 times for 8 times n, linear (it was 187M and 12418M). Test `curried_lambdas_using_every_parameter_check_in_linear_time`, n = 100 and 800, power 1, limit 24; the 947a5b48 checker fails it at 55.7 times the bytes (15.6M and 866M). Fixed in CHK-carrier (repair 4): `generalize` replaces a class solved to a type that holds no variable and is not the unknown (`is_solved`) only outside the functions the signature holds (`Sig::replace_outside`); inside them the class variable stays, and each place that reads those functions reads it through the union-find (`Ck::stale`): the comparisons (`Ck::sub`, `Ck::subtype`, `ty::fits_sig_with`, through the `solve` field of `Cx`, which `Cx::read` and the needs branch of `fits_in` use), the outer level of a signature a call reads (`Ck::open`), and the messages (`Ck::text`, `Ck::sig_text`, `Ck::show`), which settle the type they print, at the cost of printing it. `generalize` no longer names such a class as a type variable of the signature. `finish` settles each signature once (`Sig::settle`, memoized in the signature's `Facts`, not by address) and renames only one that still holds a class. `Sig::settle` drops solved variables from `bounds`, `own` and `held`. A chain of calls on one value, `g(0)(1)…(n)`, was also n^2 (51.8 times the instructions for 8 times n at 5e449f11): `Ty::union` put the functions of each side in a hash set, and the derived `Hash` of a signature walked it whole, at each call the depth of the rest of the chain; and `hold` walked each result function whole for its variables. Now `Sig::digest` hashes a signature once from its parts, each function it holds by its own digest (memoized in `Facts`; `Hash` for `Sig` writes it), and `hold` reads `Sig::facts` and rebuilds only the functions a need joins. Per node at n = 100 and 800: carriers 274103 and 274564 instructions (1.00 times), 49621 and 49236 bytes; call chain 413888 and 413130 instructions, 70057 and 69516 bytes. Before (5e449f11): carriers 792018 and 4900838 instructions, 99443 and 468431 bytes (49.5 and 37.7 times n); call chain 1.51M and 9.80M instructions, 117.8K and 486.6K bytes. Tests `curried_lambdas_whose_parameters_take_a_carrier_check_in_linear_time` (no longer ignored) and `a_chain_of_calls_on_one_value_checks_in_linear_time`, n = 100 and 800, power 1, limit 24; `l01_26_type_resolution::curried_classes` checks that 19 programs that call, pass, compare and print such lambdas give the signatures, types, call signatures, errors and notes the eager build gave. Negative control, run once and not committed: `replace(&outside)` in place of `replace_outside(&outside)` in `generalize` gives carriers 798369 and 4944027 instructions (49.5 times), 101619 and 481807 bytes per node, and call chain 911129 and 5051088 instructions (44.4 times), 118568 and 498621 bytes. Other families, per node before and after: nested lambdas 64073 → 68131 instructions at n = 1000; curried every parameter 127090 → 156435 at n = 100 (the resolver and settle work); wide lambda 185154 → 177674 at n = 800; param calls 201.4K → 239.8K at n = 1000 (19.3 times n at 8n before and after, pre-existing). Still eager: a class bound to another class or to the unknown is replaced at every depth (no family that is n^2 by it was measured); `Ty::diff` compared a tuple holding such a function with the type of an `is` test without reading its stale classes, so the branch where the tuple is not of that type kept it (`r = if t is (((int) -> ((int) -> (int, int, int))), int) { 1 } else { t }` with `t = (g(0), 1)` gave `1 \| (((int) -> ((int) -> (int, int, int))), 1)`, the eager build `1`; only in programs already rejected, since a tuple takes no function); fixed by `Ty::diff_with`, which the `is` test calls with `Ck::stale` as `Ck::subtype` does. `Ty::meet` keeps the functions of the narrowed type whole and compares none, as the eager build does. `curried_classes` now has 25 programs, 6 of them `is` and `is not` tests on such tuples, and `an_is_test_of_curried_lambdas_whose_parameters_take_a_carrier_scales` (n = 100 and 800, power 1, limit 24) measures 8.1 times the instructions and 7.9 times the bytes; negative controls, run once and not committed: `diff` without the resolver gives the wrong type above, and a resolver that solves the whole narrowed type at each lookup gives 43.6 times the bytes. finishing a signature renames it whole, so a program whose n nested lambdas each have a signature recorded for display would cost n^2 there (not measured; the curried test, which finishes every signature the checker records, stays linear). `Ck::data` reports the unknown `_` once per row, not once per `_`, since a row shared in a type can be reached a number of times exponential in its depth and the extra errors repeated one text at one span. The n^2 error cascade above is ledger item CHK-held-cascade, fixed there (F89) |
| F78 | `query.rs` keys (L1.37a; were BLAKE3 hashes of their inputs, so two keys of equal hash were one row) | a key is the encoded bytes of its inputs (`Key(Box<[u8]>)`), compared in full on a hash-map hit: O(key length) per lookup. A cone's part names each definition by a number the database gives its text once (`Db::cone`, `Cones::number`, kept in a `OnceCell` per definition), so a key is O(definitions in the cone + roots' text), not their whole text | fixed (L1.37a). Tests `l01_30_queries` (9) in both builds; `l01_37a_short_addresses::scaling::numbering_the_definitions_of_a_unit_scales` (n 40 and 320, order 1) |
| F79 | `cost.rs` `frontier` and `pick`: plans kept and tied by content address alone | the order is `(address, regions, cut edges)`, compared lexicographically, so two plans of colliding address keep one order: O(P log P) sort as before, plus a structural comparison only on equal addresses | fixed (L1.37a). Tests `m0_06_cost_scalarization` (18) in both builds |
| F82 | an intersection of function types as one function's arrows (AND-fns): `Type::and_operands` takes the operands with each intersection in parentheses among them replaced by its own, with an explicit stack, so `resolve`, `parse_type` and `Denote` read each intersection once, with the one around it; `Type::overload` compares each operand's parameter list with the first and each variable an operand's clause bounds with its first bound, in a `HashMap` by name; `resolve` and `check::read::parse_type` reject lists that differ; `check::Denote::ty` joins the operands' arrows, bounds and handled atoms into one `Sig`; `check::ty::fits_in` looks each arrow of the slot up first in a `HashMap` of the argument's arrows, then (AND-fns-fit) asks an `ArrowIndex` of the argument's arrows, built once per call, for candidates: per parameter position a posting list of arrow indices for each carrier and each bare atom name the parameter type offers, plus the arrows whose parameter type is open (variables, dynamic, cofinite atoms); the shortest needed list, merged with the open list, filtered by the other needed keys, tried in order up to the first that fits | O(n) for an overload of n arrows read, resolved, denoted, and passed to a slot of equal arrows; O(n + m + candidates) when they differ, the candidates of each slot arrow being the arrows that offer every piece its parameters need; O(n·m) when every arrow offers one shared piece of both the slot arrow's parameter and its result; the together check adds, for each slot arrow and parameter, the arrows that may cover it there | the equal case fixed: `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::passing_an_overload_of_many_arrows_scales` (n = 200 and 1600; 62.3 times the bytes before the lookup, the negative control) and `l01_29_diagnostics::printed::reading_an_overload_of_many_arrows_back_scales`. Nesting fixed: `l01_29_diagnostics::printed::reading_an_overload_nested_in_parentheses_back_scales` (n deep to the left and to the right) and `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::a_slot_written_as_an_overload_nested_in_parentheses_scales` (n = 100 and 800); the negative control, the code before AND-fns repair 2, which joined each nested intersection again at each depth by copying the inner signature, allocated 31.2 and 31.7 times the bytes (24 allowed) at load 45. Arrows that differ fixed (AND-fns-fit): `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::passing_an_overload_to_a_slot_of_other_arrows_scales` (n = 100 and 800; 7.9 times the bytes and 7.9 times the work; the negative control, the old scan of every pair, 57.1 times the bytes at load 21) and the differential tests `check::ty::tests::{the_index_of_arrows_gives_the_verdict_of_every_set_of_arrows, the_index_of_arrows_gives_the_verdict_of_every_set_of_arrows_on_wider_shapes}` (named `..._of_every_pair` before AND-fns-rule6; they compare the index with the all-pairs search and, since AND-fns-rule6, with a search over every nonempty set of at most 10 arrows; the second, from AND-fns-fit verify, adds cofinite and empty-payload atoms, tuples, kind-wide parts, int and bool literals, every rat and f64, and rigid variables; treating cofinite atoms as closed fails it and passes the first). Shared parameter piece fixed, and arrows that cover a slot arrow together (AND-fns-rule6, rule 6 of sec:type_resolution): `ArrowIndex` also files each arrow under the rarest carrier or atom name its result needs, so a slot arrow's candidates must also lie within its result's keys; `check::ty::covered` accepts a slot arrow whose parameter j lies within the union of parameter j of the arrows that hold its other parameters and return within its result, found through the same posting lists; probe `passing_an_overload_whose_arrows_share_a_parameter_piece_scales` (n = 100 and 800; 7.9 times the bytes and 7.9 times the work at load 9 to 10; 57.6 times the bytes before); the negative control, the result index skipping arrow 0, fails both differential tests and 15 tests of l01_26_type_resolution. Not fixed: arrows that all offer one piece of both the slot's parameter and its result, O(n·m); complexity tradeoff at `every_arrow_fits`: the general question is a containment join, the complement of Hitting Set, with no n^(2-e) algorithm expected (Hitting Set conjecture, Abboud, Vassilevska Williams and Wang, SODA 2016). Refused although rule 6 accepts them, complexity tradeoff at `covered`: arrows that cover only across two or more parameters (DNF tautology, coNP-complete), arrows whose parameter holds a variable or the unknown, and overlapping arrows whose results only intersect within the slot's result (the all-subsets condition of Frisch, Castagna and Benzaken); tested by `l01_26_type_resolution::fits::arrows_that_cover_only_across_two_parameters_or_by_intersected_results_are_refused`. AND-fns-rule6 verify: two defects fixed. (a) Soundness: `covered` took an arrow whose parameter type held a variable inside a tuple, so `(x: (T,)) -> String for T in int | f64` fitted `(x: (bool,)) -> String` (the union bound `T` to `bool` and no bound was checked); now the type must be closed at every depth (`Ty::closed`), and function types, read as variables, take no part either; tested by `fits::an_arrow_with_a_variable_in_a_tuple_takes_no_part_in_a_cover` (the negative control, the old top-level check, fails it). (b) Cost: `covered` ran the index and compared every parameter once for each parameter left out, the square of the width; now one `ArrowIndex::covering` per slot arrow and one pass per arrow (`sole_miss`): `scaling::a_function_of_many_parameters_that_misses_one_scales` (w = 100 and 800; 7.8 times the bytes; the code before, 38.8 times at load 22) and `scaling::an_overload_of_many_arrows_that_cover_a_slot_together_scales` (6.5 times). The differential tests now also check on the wider shapes that the index keeps every arrow that fits or may cover, and that the verdict is the same with the arrows of the function reversed, rotated and shuffled and those of the slot reversed (negative controls, not committed: dropping the arrows whose results need no key, dropping one result key list, and allowing no missed parameter each fail both tests; trying only the parameter the first arrow misses fails the order check). Not fixed, tradeoff at `covered`: arrows that all offer one piece of the slot's parameter are each compared with each slot arrow, O(n·m) (63.3 times the bytes at n = 100 and 800 for `(x: #c | #ai) -> String` against `(x: #c | #ak | #a(k+1)) -> String | #zk`). Shared-piece probe 7.9 times at load 9. AND-fns-rule6b: of the three refused cases, parts of the second and third are now built, and each refusal has its own message, naming the arrow and the reason (`check::ty::refused_cover`, one pass over the arrows on the way to an error, through `why_unfit` in `Ty::refused` and the named-function message). Built: a function type, a rigid variable or the unknown at the cover parameter (`binds_nothing` replaces `Ty::closed` there; `sole_miss` is now `part_of`), and the intersection form `check::ty::met`: the arrows that take all of the slot arrow, or all that the arrows within its result leave of one parameter (`Ty::diff`), are intersected as kernel types, one comparison per candidate. Refused, each its own message: a cover split across two or more parameters (coNP-complete); an arrow that would bind its own variable at the cover parameter (the product of the bounds); and arrows outside the result that meet within it only on different sets (as hard as Orthogonal Vectors with one parameter of atoms; Williams, TCS 2005, under SETH). Soundness hole fixed: `bounds_hold` took a bounded variable bound to values of several carriers, so `add(x, y) = x + y` fitted `(x: int \| f64, y: int \| f64) -> int \| f64` and failed when run ("Add applied to `int`, `f64`"); such a binding now needs one place an argument gives, outside function types, and none the arrow takes inside one (`one_place`). Tests: `l01_26_type_resolution::fits` (one test per case with its message and near miss, the hole and its near miss `inc(x) = x + 1n`, case 4); `check::ty::tests::a_fit_of_small_types_is_a_cover_of_their_values`, a new differential test against the values of small types (4000 cases; every fit must be a cover); the reference of the two index tests now includes `met`. Scaling (n and 8n, 24 allowed): `scaling::many_arrows_whose_results_intersect_within_the_slots_scale` (8.2 times the bytes at 100 and 800), `many_arrows_whose_results_meet_only_on_different_sets_are_refused_in_linear_time` (n = 400 and 3200; 8.1 times at 100 after the fix; the negative control, `arrow_text` copying the whole signature for each arrow it names, 29.9 times at 400), `an_overload_with_a_function_arrow_covering_a_slot_scales` (6.5 times), `a_bounded_variable_at_many_parameters_is_refused_in_linear_time` (7.8 times). Negative controls, not committed: `met` off fails the intersection test and its scaling test; `met` accepting any nonempty set fails the value test and the first index test; `one_place` off fails the hole test and its scaling test (not the value test, whose small types bound no variable of the given side); function types barred from covers fail the function-type test and its scaling test. AND-fns-rule6b verify#1: `meet_within` intersected kernel results only, so results that hold a function or a rigid variable were refused with the Orthogonal Vectors message, though rule 6 gives the fit and it is linear (`((x: int) -> String \| #a) & ((x: int) -> String \| ((y: int) -> int))` at `(x: int) -> String`). It now intersects the kernel parts exactly, leaves out a function piece where another result holds no function and a rigid variable where the carriers the other results may hold miss its bound (one pass from each end), and requires every other piece to be within the slot's result (Complexity tradeoff at `meet_within`: `(U,) \| String` and `(bool,) \| String` are refused). The messages printed the slot's variables as `_`; `text_at` names them and the rigid variables made from them. Tests: `fits::results_that_hold_a_function_or_a_variable_are_intersected` and `fits::a_refused_cover_names_the_expected_types_variables` (both failed before the fix); `check::ty::tests::a_fit_at_a_bounded_variable_is_a_cover_of_its_values` (4000 cases; 400000 over four other seeds, not committed, gave no unsound fit); `scaling::many_results_that_hold_a_variable_or_a_function_meet_in_linear_time` (7.7 times the bytes and 7.8 the work at 100 and 800; the negative control, the carriers of the other results read again for each result, 43.4 times the bytes). AND-fns-rule6b verify#2: the rule `meet_within` applies to a rigid variable is that each carrier of its bound is missing from some other result (the carriers every result but one may hold), which DESIGN, the message and the doc comment had stated as "the carriers another may hold miss its bound"; the three now say what the checker does, and `fits::results_that_hold_a_function_or_a_variable_are_intersected` holds a fit only that reading gives (`T | #a`, `int | String`, `f64 | String` at `String`), which failed on a control that drops the variable only where one other result misses its whole bound. `check::ty::tests::a_fit_with_functions_in_the_results_is_a_cover_of_their_values` checks the function path against values: two function types and one function value in both, at parameters and in results, and an own unbounded `T` bound to the slot's functions (4000 cases: 473 fit by one arrow, 165 by intersection with a function in a result, none unsound); a control that drops every function piece fails it at case 2, and the older value tests pass that control. Both value tests now check the verdict in every order of the arrows, which a control reading only the carriers before each result (a sound, order-dependent mask) fails at case 3500. The scaling test catches a quadratic re-read that allocates (43.4 times the bytes) but not one that re-reads the masks without allocating (passed at 100 and 800: the k² mask operations are below the work of checking k arrows); the pass from each end is linear by reading. CHECK-scaling-share: that test now times the checker alone (it was 30% of what was timed), and the non-allocating re-read of the masks, K times for each result, still passes it below K = 128 at 100 results, below K = 32 at 400 and below K = 4 at 3,200 (3.2 s): checking costs about 160,000 instructions a result and a re-read about 3 a pair, so K = 1 would need about 12,800 results. `check::ty::Meet` (`#[doc(hidden)]`) holds results built before the timing, and `scaling::many_results_that_hold_a_variable_meet_in_linear_time_alone` times `meet_within` alone on 1,000 and 8,000 results `U \| String \| #bi` (0.09 s): the re-read fails it at K = 1 (37.3 times the work, 17.2 million to 641 million instructions) and so does the copy of the masks (29.3 times the bytes). Its results hold no function, so the function path is timed only through the whole check. |
| F86 | the packrat memo of the parser (`parse::engine::Parser::call`, step LR2; PARSE-memo-cost): was a `HashMap` keyed by rule, position and `head` with SipHash, looked up again at each end of a rule; now a `Vec` of entries, the entries of a position a list through `prev` from `last[pos]`, the entry's index carried in `Frame::Rule`; what was expected at the farthest failure was a `BTreeSet`, one insert per failure there, and is now a `Vec` with one push per failure, made a set once when read (`Parser::expected`) | O(1) per call in the input size: a position holds at most one entry per rule and value of `head` (2R for R rules, 342 for the grammar's 171), so the walk is bounded by the grammar; on the measured texts a position holds 4.6 to 13.6 entries on average and at most 67; O(N·R) entries for N tokens, as before; the list of expected items holds one item per failure at the farthest position, at most the parse's work, O(E log E) to make the set at the end | checked: parse alone (lexed before timing, `Unit`), instructions per input byte at n = 8,000, before and after: `l01_26a` sum 2,851 and 753, calls 18,185 and 5,344, `l01_26` chain 10,387 and 2,811, fan 11,414 and 3,319, `l01_16` nested functions 11,093 and 3,754, a large callee 11,249 and 3,098 (3.0 to 3.8 times fewer; per term the calls 288,600 to 84,800 and nested functions 570,700 to 193,100); bytes allocated per input byte about 1,000 before, 164 to 1,130 after. Profile before (perf, instructions): SipHash 25%, the hash map's lookups, inserts and growth 17%, the `BTreeSet` of expected items 8% to 10% and its string comparisons 4%. A hash map with FxHash into the same `Vec` of entries, with the list of expected items too, took 6% to 27% more instructions than the lists and 1.9 times the cycles (9.46 and 5.09 billion on the six texts), its table scattered over memory. Tests: `l01_08_parser::grammar_scaling::functions_nested_many_deep_are_linear`, n = 1,000 and 8,000: 193,436,051 to 1,546,374,002 instructions (8.0 times), 37.8 to 302.3 million bytes (8.0 times); `a_sum_of_many_calls_with_literals_is_linear`: 85,139,744 to 679,958,260 (8.0 times), 17.9 to 143.5 million bytes. Negative controls, not committed: one list for all positions instead of one per position does not finish either test in 60 s; a walk of the frame stack at each new entry, O(depth), fails the nested test at n = 250 (59.1 times, 686,637,159 to 40,613,842,543) and passes the sum of calls and `a_program_of_many_functions_is_linear`, which nest to a constant depth. Differential, not committed: every `.zed` file of the repository (82) cut at every seventh token and parsed from `Unit`, `Expr` and `Type`, 8,628 parses (195 trees, 8,433 errors with their expected sets), gives output identical to the hash map's, byte for byte. The whole pipeline on the `l01_26a` texts at n = 1,000 (parse, resolve, check, elaborate; ELAB-scaling-isolation measured it before): sum 553 to 486 million instructions, calls 651 to 445 million, elaboration now 4.5% and 3.6% of it |
| F87 | a set of carriers printed as a type (`check::ty::mask_text`, BOUNDS-other): `_` when the set may take other values, `!_` when it is empty, otherwise its carriers joined by `\|` | O(1): a mask has 6 bits and at most 5 carriers | fixed by design; no scaling test, since the cost does not depend on the input. `l01_29_diagnostics::printed::every_set_of_carriers_prints_as_a_type_that_reads_back` checks all 64 masks |
| F88 | the grammar reader (`parse::grammar::Reader`, item GRAMMAR-read-cost): `e % sep` was read as `e (sep e)*` with `e` cloned into both places, so each `%` around an expression doubled it: a chain `'x' % ',' % ',' …` and a nest `(('x' % ',') % ',') …` of n `%` built 2^n nodes; the reader also recurses once per group and lookahead open around the place it reads | was O(2^n) in the `%` around one operand; now O(n) in the length of the line. The recursion is linear in the nesting depth and is kept, with a `// Complexity tradeoff:` comment at `Reader::within`: a line nested thousands deep would overflow the thread stack, and every line is the spec author's, nested a few levels | fixed: `Expr::Sep` holds `[sep, e]` once; the engine matches it by `Frame::Sep` (the first `e`, then a `Frame::Loop` over the sequence `sep e`, the same frames and kids the written-out form gave), and the grammar checker and fuzzer read it as they read `e (sep e)*`. Tests: `l01_08_parser::grammar_read`, the trees of `%` lists equal to those of the written-out form (nodes, branches and errors, nested lists too); the chain at n = 2,000 and 16,000, 408,681 to 3,265,689 bytes (8.0 times), the nest at 32 and 256, 24,377 to 191,257 bytes (7.8 times), limit 24, load 51. Negative control, the old reader at n = 2 and 16: 1,551 to 10,621,431 bytes (6,848 times) for the chain, 2,383 to 10,628,087 (4,460 times) for the nest |
| F89 | `check` (CHK-held-cascade): a function held at the bottom of n nested tuples, written in one expression, put in a tuple by n bindings, or by n nested calls of a function that wraps its argument in a tuple | was n errors, each with a derivation O(n) notes long, so O(n^2) text, and O(n^2) time from three walks of the whole type at each level: `Ck::finish` named and solved the variables of each binding's type (`Ty::vars_in`, `Ty::replace`), `Ck::meet` and `bound` did so at each call, and `Ty::kernel` walked the argument of each call to the function at its bottom; `Ty::write` recursed once a level and overflowed a 2 MiB stack below 8,000 deep, as did the drop glue of the rows | fixed: a function held in the row of a tuple expression is reported at that tuple, once; a row records whether a tuple expression checked it (`RowData::checked`, `Row::of_data`) and the first function it holds that none did (`RowData::unchecked`, `Ty::unchecked_function`), which `Ck::data` reports in place of every held function, so a value that flows through more tuples, bindings or calls meets the same function and is not reported again. Each row keeps the variables it holds at any depth, inside functions and payloads too (`RowData::vars`, a `VarFacts` made in `Row::made` from the components' rows and signatures, merged small into large; `Ty::var_facts`), so `Ty::replace`, `Ty::renamed` and `Ty::subst` keep whole a row that holds none of the variables replaced (`Row::holds_any`), and `Ck::solved`, `Ck::shown`, `Ck::settled`, `bound` and `Ck::meet` read the variables at the cost of the outer level. `Ty::kernel` refuses a type whose rows hold a variable, the unknown or a function at the outer level (`Row::open`, `RowData::held`). The printer (`write_parts`, `Ty::parts`, `Sig::parts`, `Shape::parts`) and the drop of a row (`impl Drop for RowData`) keep their own stacks. Instructions and bytes at n = 500 and 4,000, by `Meter`: one expression 24.5M to 192.8M (7.87 times), 3.9M to 31.0M bytes; n bindings 43.7M to 348.4M (7.97 times), 6.0M to 47.9M bytes (it was 720.8M at n = 800 alone); n calls 81.2M to 644.2M (7.93 times), 15.4M to 122.5M bytes (it was 1103.4M at n = 400); one error with two notes at every size; load 5-min 59 to 71. Tests `l01_26_type_resolution::held_functions::a_function_in_many_nested_tuples_is_one_error_in_linear_time` (the three shapes, n = 500 and 4,000, power 1, limit 24), `a_type_8000_tuples_deep_prints_and_drops_on_a_2_mib_stack`, `a_type_a_million_tuples_deep_prints_and_drops_on_an_8_mib_stack`, and four correctness tests of the one error. Negative control, run once and not committed: the text of each binding's type made eagerly in `bind` (walk.rs) gives, for n bindings, 1739.0M to 112502.2M instructions (64.7 times) and 342.4M to 21402.3M bytes (62.5 times) at load 75, and the scaling test does not finish in 60 s. Open: (a) n separate tuple expressions each holding the function, `t_i = (t_(i-1), 1)[0]`, are n errors, each a real one, whose derivations through the earlier bindings are O(i) notes: O(n^2) notes (99,301 notes, 3.5 MB of text at n = 200), since notes are not shared across errors; (b) one error whose derivation passes through n nested tuples of data, `t = ((1, 1), 1)…; v = t + 1`, has 2n notes, each printing a subtuple, O(n^2) text (112 KB at n = 200); (c) a type that shares a row at each level, `a_i = (a_(i-1), a_(i-1))`, prints in 2^n characters, as no syntax names a shared part; each is open for the author to decide what a message shows (no test asserts these sizes). The whole check of a function 2,000 tuples deep still overflows a 2 MiB stack, in the kernel's emptiness search (`zed_kernel::types::empty::Search::rep`, item T2-types-rest), and `Ty::kernel`, `Ty::replace_in` and `Ty::subst` still recurse once a level of the rows they enter (item T2-ty) |
| F90 | the needs a generic function's body records and each call checks (`Ck::need`, `Ck::replay`, `Ck::meet`; CHK-need-uses): destructuring a parameter into `n` names records `n` needs of its class, each checked at a call on the type the call binds; `need` found a use the class had met already by a scan of its uses, O(uses) per need; each check at a call copied the call's binds (`bound` and `replay`'s `before`), which hold one entry per need's result, and rebuilt and hashed the bound tuple of `n` (`Ty::replace_in`, `Ty::vars_in`), so the body and each call cost O(n) per need, O(n²) in all | O(n) per body and per call, up to the hashing of the needs' types: a class keeps its uses' results in a hash map by kind and whether the use gives a value (`Class::known`, merged smaller into larger with `uses` in `Ck::merge`, the uses kept first keeping their results as the scan did), with a flag for a data use; `bound` adds the variables the binds leave unbound to the call's binds for the replacement and removes them after it, so it never copies them; `Ty::replace_in` asks whether a signature holds a bound variable from the smaller of the binds and the signature's variable sets (`check::ty::holds_bound`, as `Row::holds_any` does for a row; since F97, `Replacer::holds`), rather than scanning every bound variable at each signature; `replay` no longer copies them (`meet` reads them and does not change them); a row that holds no variable, function or payload at any depth (`Row::fixed`, set when the row is made) is kept whole by `replace_in` and skipped by `vars_in` | fixed. Test: `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::destructured_parameters_check_in_linear_time`: one parameter destructured into n names and called on a tuple of n, n = 200 and 1,600: 34,396,491 to 280,304,093 instructions (8.1 times), 5,596,590 to 44,324,206 bytes (7.9 times); n functions each destructuring a pair, n = 100 and 800: 75,708,817 to 607,333,893 (8.0 times), 12,513,340 to 100,059,652 bytes (8.0 times); load5 65 to 60 on 32 threads. Negative control, not committed: the same test before these changes, 24,449,954 to 1,255,511,066 bytes (51.4 times; load5 54); with only `bound` and `Row::fixed` changed, 34.0 times the bytes; with all but the hash map, 8.6 times the instructions. Test: `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::needs_that_name_a_variable_the_call_leaves_unbound_check_in_linear_time`: a generic function passed as an argument and applied to n parameters (`app(id, 0, ..., n-1)`), n = 400 and 3,200: 201,786,349 to 1,666,506,279 instructions (8.26 times), 7.99 times the bytes; load5 58; measured before the rebase on c277b594, and passing after it. Negative control, not committed: with the copy in `bound`, 30,786,714 to 1,031,694,386 bytes at n = 200 and 1,600 (33.5 times; load5 68); with the copy removed but the scan in `replace_in` (on main before this item), 19.2 times the instructions at n = 200 and 26.5 times at n = 400 (load5 70 to 74). Measured and fixed in F97: when a signature does hold a bound variable, `replace_in` built the binds that are not its own by a pass over all of them |
| F91 | a test on a value a use of an undeclared parameter gives (`check::Ck::narrowed`, repair CHK-index-else): `args[k] else ""`, `args[k] else #notFound -> ""` and the false branch of `args[k] is t` in the body of `r(fs, args, k)` kept the whole type variable of `args[k]`, so a call binding `args` to a `CommandLine` gave `fs` a `#notFound` and was rejected; the test now records a `NeedKind::Narrow` need on that class, replayed at each call as the other needs are | O(1) per test and type variable of a class made by a need (`Class::given`), and one need replay per call, as for an index or a payload; parameter classes keep the handled atoms of F74 and are not narrowed | fixed. Tests: `l01_26_type_resolution::given` (the helper run on a present and an absent file, the named, bare-atom and `is` forms, a function value applied to the narrowed value, `else #ioError -> ""` and `is #ioError` still rejected); `many_helpers_narrowing_what_a_use_gives_scale`, n = 200 and 1,600 helpers, each called once, 74,942,632 to 599,279,897 bytes (8.0 times), work under the limit of 24, load 61. Negative control, narrowing off: 6 of the 7 tests fail. The error text of the forms still rejected grows linearly: one helper read 200 and 1,600 times, 273,142 to 2,231,808 bytes before the fix (8.17 times), 269,942 to 2,206,208 for `fs[args[k]]` and 277,945 to 2,270,211 for `else #ioError -> ""` after it; 200 and 1,600 distinct helpers 282,584 to 2,334,794 (8.26 times); 22 lines per rejected call. The 64 MB at 1,600 reads reported by L1.36a was not reproduced in these shapes |
| F92 | the citation checker (`crates/zed-tools/src/cite.rs`, `cite SPEC_DIR`, CITE-check): every `sec:` label and plan step id in DESIGN.md and in the files git tracks here | O(n) in the bytes of the files: the labels of DESIGN.md's headings and the steps of its plan, with the steps the plan DAG's rows add after "DESIGN step", go into hash sets, and each citation is one lookup; the line number is the index of a single pass over the lines | test `m0_03_grammar_tools::cite::cite_scales_with_the_number_of_citations`, 2,000 against 16,000 lines of four citations each, power 1: 8.0 times the work in instructions (12.4M to 98.6M, load 84). Negative control, run once and not committed: a count of the lines before each line (quadratic) fails it at 62.3 times. The whole tree and DESIGN.md: 0.17 s |
| F93 | the variables a row holds at any depth (`check::ty::merge`, called by `Row::made` for the sets F89 added; ROW-made-cost, found by the CHK-held-cascade merge) | was O(n^2 log n) over n rows `t(k) = (t(k-1), (t(k-1), Vk))`: each row merged its components' sets by inserting each variable of the smaller into the larger, O(k log k) a row although the two sets share all but one variable ("merged small into large" bounds the copies of a variable only when the rows form a tree); now each merge costs within a constant factor of the cheaper of the insertions (O(m log N) for sets of m and N variables) and the difference walk (`imbl::OrdSet::diff`, which steps over the nodes the two sets share, so a set made from the other by adding a few variables costs about the depth of the tree): O(n log n) on this family | partly fixed. `merge` runs the insertions and the difference walk one step each in turn and stops when either ends; both always run, with no switch on size. Test `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::rows_made_from_overlapping_variable_sets_scale` (n = 250 and 2,000, power 1, limit 24): 6,431,792 to 64,332,561 instructions (10.0 times; n log n allows about 11), 505,032 to 4,887,496 bytes (9.7 times), load5 68 to 70. Negative control, not committed, rerun at merge: the insertions alone (the code before), 22,904,397 to 1,616,272,669 instructions (70.6 times), load1 90. Open, author question A8 (`wt/zed0-review/pass2/QUESTIONS.md`): rows built from two sets that share no node, `z(k) = (x(k), y(k))` with `x(k) = (x(k-1), Ak)` and `y(k) = (y(k-1), Bk)`, cost O(n^2 log n) (71.2 times the instructions, 2,487,120 to 98,017,696 bytes, 39.4 times, at 250 and 2,000), since each `z(k)` holds 2k variables no other row holds, and an exact set for every row is the transitive closure of the DAG of rows, for which no near-linear method is known. Test `rows_made_from_disjoint_variable_sets_scale`, `#[ignore]` until A8 is answered. Found while measuring: the program form of the first family, `p(s, v) = (s, (s, v))` nested n deep, takes 2^n time because `Ty::replace_in`, called from `Ck::finished`, walks a shared row once per path (0.34 s at n = 14, 20.9 s at n = 20); fixed in F97 (REPLACE-shared-rows) |
| F94 | the runner's test-table rows (TEST-rows-rule6): `elab::Elab::row_call` builds a row's call with the rule-6 pass-through guard the checker recorded at the row key's span (a `HashMap` from argument span to the atoms that pass, then one pass over the arguments; the guard is `Elab::guard`, shared with `pass_end`); `testing::checks` lists each row of a rejected unit as a failure, one pass over the top-level statements and their rows | O(a + p) per row for a arguments and p recorded pass entries; O(r + text) per rejected unit of r rows | checked: `l01_21_test_runner::scaling::rows_whose_calls_pass_atoms_by_scale` (running the rows of 100 and 800 functions: 8.02 times the work, 8.00 the bytes), `a_row_passing_the_atoms_of_many_arguments_scales` (elaborating one row of 400 and 3,200 arguments, the unit checked before the timing: 8.0 times the work, 8.0 to 9.3 the bytes), `the_rows_of_a_rejected_unit_fail_in_linear_time` (400 and 3,200 rows), at load 52 to 90. Negative controls, not committed: the line table rebuilt for each row of a rejected unit allocated 26.1 times the bytes at 400 and 3,200 (24 allowed) and fails; at 100 and 800 it gave 17.9 times and passed, hence the larger sizes. The span map of `row_call` rebuilt for each argument: 56 times the work and 51 the bytes at 400 and 3,200 arguments, and fails (at 100 and 800, 41 times the work and 29.9 the bytes). The first version of the test timed `test_in` on a `Db` that held the front end's result, which never elaborates, so it never ran `row_call`, and that control passed it (9.0 times at 100 and 800). Not caught: a linear search of the recorded entries for each argument in place of the map, 11.5 times at 400 and 3,200 (about 2 instructions a pair against 24,000 an argument for elaboration; it would show near 5,000 arguments, where checking the unit before the timing costs too much, see below). The same search is in `Elab::passes` (`at.iter().find` per argument), the call path's counterpart. Not fixed, outside this item: `check::Ck::pass_at` clones the call's whole list of entries into the undo log for each argument that passes atoms and searches it linearly, O(a²) time and bytes for one call of a such arguments, against F76's O(log n + a) per argument; the front end of a call of 400 and 3,200 atom arguments allocated 22.2 times the bytes (23.0M to 509.1M), and 36 times at 1,000 and 8,000 (dhat: 92 MB of 182 MB written at 1,600 arguments came from that clone); open item ISSUE1-passby-scale |
| F95 | `check` (ATOM-payload-fn): `Ty::held_function` and `Ty::unchecked_function` look inside atom payloads (`Ty::function_in`), and `Ck::data` gives the classes in payloads their need | new walk: each type's own functions and its rows' memo (`RowData::held`, `RowData::unchecked`, which `Row::made` now computes through payloads too), then each payload's items and fields by an explicit stack; a row is read and not entered, and a payload is owned by its type, not shared, so the walk enters each payload once, linear in the type outside its rows, and needs no visited set | Test `l01_26_type_resolution::held_functions::a_function_in_payloads_is_found_in_linear_time`, n = 500 and 4,000, power 1, limit 24, four families: a function or an integer at the bottom of n levels of an atom around a tuple (`#a((#a((… , 1)), 1))`), and after n integers in one payload; passes, 7.9 times the bytes for the function under atoms and tuples, wall time 8.0 times for the wide payload, load 5-min 67 to 82. Negative control, run once and not committed: a walk that enters every row in place of reading its memo does not finish the test in 60 s. Left out of the test, and open: atoms nested directly in atoms, `#a(#a(…))`, cost O(n^2) on origin/main already (data 16.5, 52.4, 89.5 and 398.0 ms at n = 100, 200, 400 and 800; not profiled, likely the copy of each owned payload at each level), and with a function at the bottom the one error's derivation has n + 4 notes, each printing a payload O(n) long, O(n^2) text as in F89 open point (b) (47.9, 194.1 and 851.6 ms at n = 100, 200 and 400) |
| F96 | the kernel printer's prefix operations and index chains (`zed-kernel/src/print.rs`, `doc`, PRIM-hidden-names): `-x`, `!x`, `t[0]`, `f[k]`, each operand parenthesized where the grammar needs it | O(n) in the term's nodes: `primary` is one `match`, and an index chain `t[0][1]…` is one `Cat` of its receiver and its keys, gathered by a loop | test `l01_15_kernel_terms::scaling::negations_and_indices_print_in_linear_time`, depth 2,000 against 16,000, one line and width 80, terms built before timing, power 1: 8.0 times the bytes for each of the four (828,134 to 6,626,822 for negations on one line; 569,965 to 4,561,449 for indices at width 80), load1 68. Negative control, run once and not committed: each index as a `Cat` of the `Cat` inside it (the first version) fails at width 80 with 57.3 times the instructions (100.0M to 5,729.0M), since `fits` then walks down the whole chain before it meets the receiver's text |
