| F97 | replacing variables in a type whose rows are shared (`Ty::replace_in`, `Ty::subst`, `Ty::settle`, `Sig::settle`, with `Ck::data` and `Ty::vars_outside`, which walk the same rows; REPLACE-shared-rows, found by ROW-made-cost, F93) | was O(2^n): `p(s, v) = (s, (s, v))` nested n deep, `f(a0, ..., an) = p(p(a0, a1), ...)`, gives a type of n rows with 2^n paths, and each walk rebuilt or entered a row once per path (old code, load 69 to 71: 45 ms, 183 ms, 1.12 s and 4.26 s at n = 10, 12, 14 and 16; with a call of `f`, 47 ms to 2.26 s). Each rebuilding walk now keeps what it made of each row, by the row's address (`Replacer`, `once`, `Rows`), and `Ck::data` and `vars_outside` keep the rows they entered, so a row is met once: 8 ms at n = 22. Also, a signature whose own variables are bound copied the binds less its own, O(binds) for each such signature; the walk now counts the hidden own variables (`Replacer::hidden`) and looks up each variable it meets | fixed for the walks; open for the program family, whose O(n^2) awaits the author's approval, question A20 (`wt/zed0-review/pass2/QUESTIONS.md`), and is not approved. Test `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::replacing_in_rows_shared_on_many_paths_scales` (n = 100 and 800 rows `t(k) = (t(k-1), (t(k-1), Vk))`, power 1; 12,728,880 to 120,969,833 instructions for the three walks together, 9.5 times; 100, since `replace_in` still recurses once per level and 2,000 levels overflow a 2 MiB stack, item T2-ty). Test `replacing_in_many_functions_that_each_hold_one_bound_variable_scales` (n = 250 and 2,000 functions `(Oi) -> (Oi, Hi)` with all 2n variables bound): 8.4 times (11,765,151 to 98,927,940), load1 59; the copy of the binds took 60.9 times (198,445,939 to 12,090,064,185), load1 67. Negative control, not committed: the old checker does not finish either test of shared rows in 60 s (load1 83 to 97). Complexity tradeoff (comment at `Replacer::memos`): a row shared by several signatures that each hide variables is made once for each. Open, awaiting the author's approval, question A20: the program family is O(n^2), not O(n) (`rows_shared_on_many_paths_scale_as_n_squared`, power 2, a scaling test of the cost as it is now, not a claim that O(n^2) is acceptable; n = 25 and 200: 12.5 times, and 11.7 times with the call; 16.5 times at 50 and 400, 22.6 times at 100 and 800), because each call `p(s, v)` replays its data need on `s`, a type of k rows at the kth call (`Ck::data`, 53% of the time at n = 200), and `bound` and `solved` read the k variables of the argument; a memo of the rows `Ck::data` has checked would need to live in the undo log, since a trial call is undone |

## ZIR, native code and runtime

| ID | Where | Class | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Z1 | `zir/lower.rs`: an effectful `let`-bound lambda is inlined at every call, recursively | c^k nodes | fixed: a function that uses a port and whose name occurs more than once is one `lambda` node whose region takes and returns the ports' states, and each call is an `apply` that threads them (DESIGN L1.31, L1.33); a name used once is still inlined; `l01_33_lowering::scaling` (a chain of k functions each calling the one before twice: 3k+1 nodes, 25 ms at k = 800 in release; the old code runs out of memory at k = 16, and inlining every call gives 65,536 nodes there) |
| Z2 | `Lower::add` canonicalizes and encodes the whole subtree of each new node | O(n³) | fixed: a node's key is hashed from its normal-form operation, inputs and outputs and the keys of its regions (`canon::node_key`, `region_key`), so each node is hashed twice, when it is added and when its region closes; `l01_32_canonical_zir::fuzz::the_key_agrees_with_the_address` (catches region keys left out), `l01_33_lowering::scaling::lowering_a_deep_nest_scales` (`if` nest of 800: 28 s → 32 ms in release, old ratio about 8 per doubling) |
| Z3 | `zir/canon.rs` restarts from the top after every rewrite | O(k²) | fixed: every rule acts on one edge or one pattern alone and the binder rule's hint is removed by the hints rule, so `canonicalize` normalizes each edge and each pattern in one pass; `step` stays for the termination tests; `l01_32_canonical_zir::fuzz::the_one_pass_normal_form_is_the_one_the_steps_reach` (300 random graphs). The cost within one edge was the swap loop, fixed by Z18 |
| Z4 | `memory.rs` planner: `consumes` and `Uses::of` recomputed down nested branches | O(k²·d²) | open. Since lowering carries each name into a region once (Z9b), k is the number of distinct names used in the branch, so a chain of `if`s reading one name plans in linear time: 1 ms, 6 ms and 59 ms at d = 120, 480 and 1,920, where it took 5.9 s at d = 480 before |
| Z5 | planner finds an edge's node by `rposition` | O(n²) per region | fixed: the region's node bases are sorted, so `src` finds the node by binary search (`partition_point`), O(log n). Covered by `l01_36_cranelift::scaling` (Z14); no size run there shows the old cost apart from the others |
| Z6 | `lower.rs` `lookup` scans the whole scope | O(N·S) | fixed: `Lower` keeps the positions of each name in a map beside the scope, updated as names are bound and unbound, so `lookup` takes one map read. A chain of n `let`s, each reading the outermost name, debug build: 96 ms, 0.24, 0.79 and 2.75 s at n = 2000 to 16000 before, 75, 121, 201 and 434 ms after. Test `l01_33_lowering::scaling::reading_a_name_bound_far_out_scales`, ratio 38 before, passes after. Since Z12 the scope is a persistent map, so it has no positions to keep |
| Z7 | captures not deduplicated; merged by `contains`/`position` | O(raw·unique) | fixed: `node` merges the regions' captures through a map from each source edge to its position, so each raw capture costs one map operation. A lambda that reads n names bound outside it, debug build: 83 ms, 0.16, 0.41 and 1.16 s at n = 1000 to 8000 before, 76 ms, 0.13, 0.30 and 0.55 s after. The old code's ratio between 1000 and 8000 is 14, inside the limit, so no test was added |
| Z8 | `tables.rs` constants and call targets by linear search | O(C²), O(applies·lambdas) | fixed: `Tables` keeps each constant's index in a map (`const_at`) and each lambda's index by the path of its body (`lambda_at`, kept by `Tables::new` while it resolves the calls). Tests `many_constants_scale` and `many_lambdas_scale` (Z14). Old code, before the Z8 and Z14 fixes, one run per size: n lambdas 0.53, 1.6, 5.0 and 17.8 s at n = 60 to 500 (3 to 3.5 times per doubling); fixed 1.45, 2.9, 6.0, 12.0 and 22.9 s at n = 250 to 4000 (2 times). n constants: the old code passes the scaling test in 11 s (limit 62 s), so the test sizes do not show the old cost |
| Z9 | `tables.rs` `visit` cloned each node with its regions; each node kept its path, a string as long as its depth, and code generation built and looked up the same strings | O(N·depth) | fixed: `Node::regions` is `Vec<Arc<Region>>`, a `NodeInfo` holds its region and index, a planned region and node have numbers, and each region's plan is found once. A path is worked out from the links up for a message only. `Tables::visit` and `Gen::walk` keep the regions in progress on a stack of their own: `a_rate_table_thousands_deep_compiles_on_a_small_stack`, `a_decoder_thousands_deep_compiles_on_a_small_stack` and `ifs_thousands_deep_compile_on_a_small_stack` compile 4,000 levels in 128 KiB (one test per term, and the native run of each against the machine a test of its own, since CRANELIFT-scale-size), and a recursive walk put back overflows that stack. Tests `l01_36_cranelift::scaling::{ifs_nested_through_the_first_arm_scale, a_rate_table_of_many_tiers_scales, a_decoder_of_many_cases_scales, a_curried_function_of_many_parameters_scales}`, the object file timed alone on graphs built beforehand. A copy of each node's subtree put back in `visit` makes the first test fail its 55 s limit, against 21 s; one object file at n = 4,000 takes over 58 s, against about 3 s. One run per size, d = 120, 480 and 1,920: nested `if`s 91, 366 and 1,531 ms; a chain of `if`s 136, 497 and 2,339 ms; a chain of `match`es 28, 143 and 384 ms. Finding the plan still compares path strings, O(d log R) per region; it does not show at these sizes, and it goes with Z4. The generated function's frame holds a slot for each word, so running a term d deep takes stack linear in d (832 KiB at d = 4,000) |
| Z9b | object file of a chain of `if`s or `match`es superlinear: lowering carried a name through every region above each read, as a new input of each | O(d²) nodes' inputs, code and time | fixed: `Lower::bring` keeps each edge once per region, in `Frame::brought`, and stops its walk at the nearest region that has the edge, so a read costs O(1) amortized. Test `l01_33_lowering::scaling::a_name_read_at_every_depth_scales`, n = 500, and `a_name_read_at_every_depth_enters_each_region_once`; with the map ignored, lowering takes 529 ms and 8.5 s at d = 500 and 2,000, 16 times as long, against 16 and 93 ms, 5.8 times. Object file at d = 120 and 240 was 428 KB and 1,468 KB; it is now 133 KB at d = 120 and 2,111 KB at d = 1,920 |
| Z10 | `zir` validator matches state-edge uses by search | O(S²) | fixed: each state edge's uses are counted in a map, and the edges kept in the order of first use so that the same edge is reported. Test `l01_33_lowering::scaling::checking_a_graph_of_many_pushes_scales`, n = 4000, the check timed alone on graphs made beforehand; with the old code put back it fails at 59 to 60 times as long for 8 times the size |
| Z11 | `lower.rs` `ports`: the ports of each `let`-bound lambda found by reading its whole body, the lambdas nested in it included, and each port looked for among those found | O(n·depth) | fixed: the uses of each lambda body, the ports it reads or pushes and the names free in it in the order of their first occurrence, are found once and kept by the body's address; a lambda inside adds the uses of its own body less the names bound around it. A name bound inside the body is no longer taken for a function of the same name outside it. Test `l01_33_lowering::scaling::lowering_lambdas_bound_by_nested_lets_scales`, n = 500; with the old code put back it fails at 41 to 74 times |
| Z12 | `lower.rs`: a function called in place kept a copy of the whole scope it was defined in, and its call rebuilt the index of that scope | O(n·S) | fixed: the scope is a persistent map (`imbl::HashMap`), so keeping it and swapping it in each take `O(1)`, and leaving a scope restores the map saved on entering it. Test `l01_33_lowering::scaling::lowering_many_functions_called_in_place_scales`, n = 2000, which takes 9 s; with the old scope put back it does not finish in 30 min |
| Z13 | `lower.rs`: the current state of each port was found by searching the ports a region had used | O(n·P) | fixed: each frame keeps its ports' states in a map. Test `l01_33_lowering::scaling::lowering_pushes_to_many_ports_scales`, n = 2000, which takes 3.7 s; with the old code put back it fails at 42 to 44 times as long for 8 times the size |
| Z14 | native code and the counting machine on many fields, ports, constants, lambdas and uses of one value: each word held in a register across every call after it, a field's or port's index found by search, the ports a kernel term reads collected by `contains`, and the counting machine's memory check walking the whole heap after each step | O(n²) | fixed: each word is kept in a stack slot and loaded just before each use, so the register allocator sees only short live ranges (`codegen.rs` `Loc`); field and port indices are maps; the ports read are a set beside their order (`Lets`); the memory check looks only at the cells touched since the last check and runs the full audit only when one is wrong. Tests `l01_36_cranelift::scaling`, five shapes. Controls on the old code: many named fields killed at 671 s and many ports at 1411 s (limits 10 times the fixed time); many lambdas as in Z8; one value used n times is linear on the old code (0.05 to 0.75 s at n = 250 to 4000, against 0.08 to 1.28 s fixed), so that test does not show an old cost |
| Z15 | Cranelift's e-graph optimizer and backtracking register allocator | n^1.6 and n^1.4 measured; the e-graph pass also has fixed caps | fixed: `opt_level` `none` and `regalloc_algorithm` `single_pass` (`zed-native/src/lib.rs` `isa`), each pass linear. Zed does its own optimization over ZIR (DESIGN.md L23), not Cranelift's |
| R1 | `hooks.rs` slow path deep-copies inputs into the kernel and back | O(n) per op | fixed (T14-R1): each string, tuple or atom cell keeps the kernel `Value` made from it (`Cell::value` in `zed-rt/src/heap.rs`), set by `fresh` when a kernel result becomes cells and by `value` on the first read, and cleared by `free_cell`; a cell's parts never change while it is live, so the only write is the reuse of a freed slot, and the clear covers it. The cache is per cell, not keyed by an address. The conversion back from the kernel already made one cell per part, as the counting machine does (`memory.rs` `tree`), and it now keeps the value it was given, so a kernel result handed back to the kernel is not read again. `Stats::converted` counts the words `value` reads. On main a record of 65 cells compared 20 times read 1,341 words (20 calls of 66, plus the 21 of the result); now 61. Tests `l01_36_cranelift::kernel_inputs` (9): a tuple of 64n strings handed to the kernel n times, 7.8 times from 50 to 400 in instructions (47.8 on main, limit 24); a record the program built read n times, 7.8 times. Controls, not committed: main's heap with the counter, both scaling tests fail (47.6 and 65.0 times) and four count tests fail; the cache set by `fresh` only, the heap-level test fails at 64.9 times and a count is 101, not 63; the cache not cleared at free, the reuse test reads a stale `("nut", 40)` and nine other `l01_36` tests give wrong values |
| R2 | building an atom copies its whole payload through the kernel | O(n²) per list | fixed: `hooks::node` makes the atom from its words, as `Prim::Atom` in the kernel would, and calls the kernel only for a shape the kernel refuses, so the error is the kernel's. The field order is kept per node when the name and field names are constants (`Run::shapes`), so a struct atom costs no sort and no map. Test `l01_36_cranelift::scaling::a_list_of_many_atoms_scales`, n = 500: 8.0 times from 500 to 4,000, with no kernel call; on the old code it fails at 30.0 times. `l01_36_cranelift::hooks` checks field order, binders, a bare atom, a shape run many times and four refusals against the kernel and the machine |
| R3 | `Heap::parts` on a VAL word deep-clones the constant and appends to `vals` | O(n²) time and memory | fixed: since T14-value a clone of a constant is O(1), so the time was already linear (`matching_a_deep_constant_scales`, n = 1,000 and 8,000, passes on main too). What remained was memory: each match pushed every part it read to the table again, 100 entries after 1 match and 991 after 10 at depth 100. Now an entry's parts are entered once, together, the first time a match reads them (`Entry::parts`), and a match reads a cell's or a constant's parts in place with no copy (`Holder`, `Shape`). A literal pattern compares part by part against the word (`Heap::equals`), a string's byte length first, and `is` reads a constant or kernel value in place. Tests `l01_36_cranelift::reuse` (12) and `scaling::{matching_a_deep_constant_scales, matching_one_constant_many_times_scales}`; control, the old heap: `matching_a_rate_table_again_adds_no_entries` fails at 100 and 991 entries, and five more `reuse` tests fail. Open: `matches` copies the path for each binder (`at.to_vec()`), Σ binder depths, d²/2 for a binder at every level of a pattern d deep; `hooks.rs` reads that `Vec<usize>` and `release` walks each path again, so the fix changes the binder list both share (T14-hooks) |
| R4 | `Rope::chars` counts characters on every call; the kernel's `len` counted `s.chars()` | O(n) per `len` | fixed: `zed_kernel::Text` keeps each node's length in bytes and in characters, counted once when text is made whole and added at a join, so `len` is O(1) natively and in the kernel and never reads the text; both use `Text::char_count`. `zed-rt`'s `Rope` is gone, and the native heap holds `Text` (`Obj::Str`). Tests `l01_36_cranelift::string_scaling` (instructions, 8 times the size; limit 24): `len` of one string of 16,384n two-byte characters called n times, n = 100, natively 7.84 (main 39.1) and in the kernel 7.90 (main 63.9); `++` of two such strings n times natively 7.83 (main: over 60 s) and in the kernel 7.94 (main 7.94, already linear). `l01_36_cranelift::strings` compares kernel and native counts on ASCII, accented, CJK, emoji, empty and a line of 500 appends. Negative control, `char_count` and `join` counting by a scan: all seven tests fail, 39.1 to 63.9 times or over 60 s |
| R5 | rope flattened again after every append | O(n²) | fixed: a join is never written out; its text is read block by block (row C29); `Heap::value` hands the kernel the cell's `Text` itself, so a string crossing to the kernel and back is not copied, and the kernel's `len` reads the kept count. Tests `l01_36_cranelift::string_scaling`, n lines of 32,768 characters with a read after each, n = 60: append then `len` natively 7.89 (main 7.89, already linear) and in the kernel 9.38 (main 63.3); append then `show` natively, each `show` a kernel call whose result the next line is appended to, 7.89 (main 42.6). Read whole at each use, O(len) each: hash, printing, `lower`, `upper`, `trim`, the casts (the last four through `Text::flat`, a temporary not kept); equality and order of two strings of equal byte length, and `Heap::equals` against a literal, stop at the first byte that differs. Since WRITE-prefix-scale nothing is cached in a node, so holding every step of a chain of n joins is O(n log n) bytes (row C29); `Stats::copied` and the test `a_log_read_after_every_line_keeps_only_its_latest_text` are gone with the cache; `text::tests::reading_every_prefix_held_keeps_no_copy_of_it` checks that printing, hashing and comparing every held prefix allocates only the walks' stacks, and the `l01_36_cranelift` tests `reuse`, `kinds`, `strings` and `deep_heap` check on a block doubled 50 times (about 145 PB in 51 nodes) that a literal of another length, a test by kind, `show` and `Heap::value` read and copy none of it. Since T14-rope-len a join adds the two byte counts with `checked_add` and refuses a sum past `usize::MAX` (`KernelError::StringTooLong`, O(1) per join), so a string doubled 64 times is an error, not a wrapped or panicking length; tests `text::tests` and `l01_36_cranelift::string_length` |
| R6 | heap never reuses a freed cell; `vals` only grows | O(allocations) memory | fixed for cells: a freed cell goes on a free list and the next allocation takes it, so the cells held are the peak live, not the allocations (`a_string_made_and_freed_again_and_again_takes_one_cell`; on the old heap, one slot per allocation). A cell word carries the cell's generation in the 24 bits above its 40-bit index, and each free advances it, so a word kept past its cell's free is reported even after the cell is taken again; a cell freed in its last generation is not reused. `inc` checks the generation and the count with `checked_add` and returns the error at once; `hooks::rt_inc`, which generated code does not check, records it as the run's error, so it is reported when no `dec` follows (was kept until the next `dec`, and lost when the run ended first; `hooks` unit tests `an_inc_that_fails_*`, both fail on the old code). `release_one` puts each kept path with its part in one pass (was a filter of all paths per part, O(arity · kept)). `value` keeps the value of each cell held more than once and shares it after, so a DAG of cells is read once per cell: `reading_a_value_whose_parts_share_cells_scales`, n = 1,000 and 8,000; on the old heap a value of 64 cells, each holding the one below twice, is not read in 60 s. Not fixed, by design: the table. A VAL word has no count, generated code and hooks copy it freely, so no point says an entry is free; a free list for it cannot be correct. Its growth is now bounded by what a run reads: an entry's parts once, and the empty tuple and each bare atom once (`Heap::bare`). A match enters nothing since T14-hooks (R12). `Heap::fresh` still makes one cell per path of a DAG value, which mirrors the counting machine of L1.34; sharing them changes both |
| R7 | `matched` deep-copies a binder the arm does not use | O(unused) | fixed: with the scrutinee owned, the plan owns every binder the arm uses (`memory.rs`, arm modes), so a borrowed binder is one the arm never reads; it gets the error word and no copy, and the counts still agree with the machine. Test `l01_36_cranelift::scaling::many_matches_with_an_unused_binder_scale`, a tuple of 64n strings bound unused n times, n = 50: 7.7 times from 50 to 400; on the old code it fails at 38.1 times. Since T14-heap-rest the world is built before the timing; with the copy restored it still fails, at 39.4 times |
| R8 | `Heap::dec` allocated a stack on every call, even for a word that is not a cell or a count that stays above 0 | O(1) with an allocation per call | fixed: returns at once for those two cases, frees a cell with no parts with no stack, and otherwise reuses the stack `Heap::todo` through `mem::take`. About 12 ns to about 4.7 ns per drop that leaves the cell live (50 million drops, three runs each). The old code is also linear, so a timing test cannot fail on it; `heap.rs` tests check the stack is unused or kept. Tests `l01_36_cranelift::drops`, three shapes at n = 4000. Controls: a count of live cells per drop fails the shared-string test, 90 to 92 times as long at 8 times the size; a queue in place of the stack fails the wide-tuple test, 60 to 62 times |
| R9 | `Heap::fresh`, `value`, `release`, `matches`, `hooks::term` and `rename` recursed on the depth of a value, a pattern or a chain of closures | stack O(depth): overflow | fixed: each keeps its work on a stack on the heap, O(depth) memory, time unchanged. Tests `deep_heap`, 1,000,000 deep on a 2 MiB stack, and scaling at 100,000 and 800,000: 7.4 to 11.9 times. Controls: the old code aborts on every deep test; a scan of the stack at each step fails all four at 4,000 and 32,000, 51 to 164 times. `term` still walks a shared cell once per path; `value` reads it once since T14-heap (R6), and the path copy per binder in `matches` is open under R3 |
| R10 | generated code calls itself on the machine stack, and a function's frame has a 16-byte slot per word | stack O(program recursion + function size): overflow | fixed: `zed_native::run_at` runs the generated code on a stack that `zed_rt::Stack` maps for the run, as large as the machine's memory and swap together, reserved with `MAP_NORESERVE` (pages take memory only when touched) with a `PROT_NONE` page below it, switched to once with `psm::on_stack` and unmapped when the run ends; a panic on it is caught and resumed on the caller's stack. Depth and frame size are limited only by memory, as the heap is: no depth limit, no size switch, no change to generated code and no cost per call. Tests `l01_36_cranelift::deep_calls`, on a thread with a 2 MiB stack: `linked_list` of 10,000 and 80,000 nested calls gives the kernel's value; scaling 10,000 to 80,000 calls, 6.12 times the instructions (limit 24); `deep_heap::a_function_of_20000_concatenations_runs_on_a_smaller_thread_stack`, a frame of more than 312 KiB on a thread of 256 KiB, gives the kernel's value; `deep_heap::a_wide_function_runs_in_time_linear_in_its_width`, compiling and running that function natively at 2,500 and 20,000 concatenations on a thread of 256 KiB, 8.1 times the instructions and 8.0 times the bytes (limit 24); `zed-rt` unit tests: 100,000 frames of 1 KiB on a 2 MiB thread, the size of the mapping, a panic carried back, and `a_limited_address_space_refuses_the_reservation`: a process whose address space is limited to 8 GiB cannot reserve the stack and gets the error "runtime: cannot reserve a stack of … bytes for a run" (the complexity tradeoff at `Stack::new`: such a process runs no native code; a complete solution is a stack that grows by segments, checked at each function entry). Controls: on main the 10,000-call program and the scaling test abort with a stack overflow; the unit test without `Stack` aborts too; an O(n²) spin added in `rt_callable` fails the scaling test at 35.0 times; run on the thread's own stack, the 20,000-concatenation test aborts with a stack overflow; an O(n²) spin in `run_in` fails the wide scaling test at 28.8 times; with no limit on the address space the reservation test fails (R10-stack-notes). Stack slots reused by liveness, which would shrink frames, are not done; they are not needed for correctness |
| PRE-stack | `store/preimage.rs`, the transfer's analyses (`mentions`, `reads_mod`, `constant`, `lin`, `formula`, `fwd`, `clip`, `back`, `pa`, `by_class`, `close`, `scan`), on `Walk` with `zed-kernel/src/frames.rs` (was `store/frames.rs`) | stack O(term depth): overflow; on a 2 MiB thread the transfer overflowed between depths 1,000 and 3,000, since `zed_kernel::on_stack` is a no-op (T2-eval) | fixed (PRE-stack-explicit; PRE-stack first merged a growable stack, `stacker::maybe_grow` at `deeper`, as a temporary form by the author's decision of 2026-11-04): every analysis is an `async fn` of `Walk` (`close`, `mentions`, `reads_mod`, `constant`, `fwd`, `clip`, `summon`, `back`, `scan`, `pa`, `by_class`, `lin`, `formula` and their helpers), and each call that may recurse returns a `frames::Deep`, which hands the callee's state machine to the loop in `frames::Stack::run`; the loop keeps the suspended callers in a `Vec` on the heap and runs the top one, so the thread's stack holds one frame's code at any depth, and depth is limited by memory. The calls run in the order plain recursion ran them, and the memo is filled and read as before (`Walk::cached`, `Walk::translated`). O(1) per call: one boxed frame and one `Rc` cell for the answer; a call whose answer is in the memo takes no frame (`Stack::ready`). `stacker` is gone from `preimage.rs` and from `zedc`'s dependencies; the `// Complexity tradeoff:` comment at `deeper` is gone with it. `l01_25_preimage_catalog::deep`: a sum, a chain of lets and nested ifs, 100,000 deep, on a 2 MiB stack, and each scales at depths 12,500 and 100,000: 8.37, 8.09 and 7.97 times the instructions and 8.00, 8.18 and 7.99 times the bytes, limit 24. Control, run once and not committed: an O(depth) scan of the suspended frames at each push gives 49.8 times the instructions at depths 1,500 and 12,000 (fails; 8.03 without it); at depths 12,500 and 100,000 it ran over 60 s. Constant cost against the `stacker` form, measured at load 44 to 56: the deep tests take 2.12, 6.00 and 6.47 s before and 3.9, 7.5 and 10.5 s after, with peak memory 284, 883 and 695 MB before and 381, 1,274 and 796 MB after; the randomized unit test `entries_made_below_a_stop_give_what_reading_the_term_again_gives` takes 11% more processor time (perf samples), from allocation and moves of frame states. PRE-frame-size: the added memory was mostly the frames themselves. `frames::frame` wrapped the callee in an `async` block that kept the captured call apart from the call being awaited, so each frame was twice its callee (`back_in`'s frame 2,272 bytes for a 1,120-byte call, `by_class`'s 2,720); a frame now takes the function that makes the call (`Stack::call(move || ...)`), and `back_in`'s frame is 1,232 bytes (test `zed-kernel` `frames::tests::a_frame_holds_its_call_once`, which fails on the old form, 8,312 bytes for a 4,144-byte call). At depth 100,000, measured one test at a time against main 641b040c at load 46 to 48: sum 2.38 s and 257 MB against 2.79 s and 380 MB, chain of lets 6.54 s and 907 MB against 8.82 s and 1,274 MB, ifs 7.38 s and 700 MB against 11.54 s and 798 MB (processor time 1.58, 4.00, 5.67 s against 1.61, 4.17, 5.67 s); the stacker form had 284, 883 and 695 MB. Boxing the large inline awaits (`Box::pin` at 18 sites, `back_prim`, `call_back`, `summon`, `pa`, `fwd_prim`, `pa_if`, `by_class_node` and others) was measured and not kept: 278, 842 and 717 MB and no less time, since a boxed call that stays suspended under the recursion keeps its size and adds an allocation |
| R11 | `zed-native::run`: when `codegen::define`, `finalize_definitions` or a lookup failed, `?` returned before `free_memory`, so the JIT module's code and data pages were never freed | O(program size) memory leaked per failed compile | fixed: a `Jit` guard frees them on every way out (`zed-native` unit test `a_failed_compile_frees_the_jit_memory`) |
| R12 | the table of uncounted values: an entry per match, and no free list for entries | O(matches) memory | measured, no free list added: a match adds no entry (T14-hooks gave an unused binder the error word), and a run adds entries only for its constants, their parts once, and each distinct empty tuple or bare atom once, so the table does not grow with the runs of a match or a read. `zed_native::Outcome` now gives `table` and `cells` at the end of a run. Tests `l01_36_cranelift::table`, (entries, cells) at n = 50 and 400: an unused binder (0, 7) at both, a function matched by `is` or `_` (0, 2), n reads and drops of `((), #nil, #some(()))` with a bare atom made per call (4, 4); time for each at 500 and 4,000 by `check_run`. Controls: the copy of an unused binder restored gives 50 and 400 entries; no reuse of bare values gives 202 and 1,602. The kernel path for a function scrutinee still calls `uncounted` per binder, but there the only binder is the function, which the kernel's tuple of binders refuses, so it adds nothing |
| R13 | `is T` on a string, tuple or atom in cells made the whole value first | O(size of the value) per test | fixed: `Heap::is_in` meets `T` with the cell's kind (all strings, the tuple universe, or every atom of its name) and answers from that when `T` holds all of the kind or none of it; otherwise it reads the value as before. Tests `l01_36_cranelift::kinds` (4): 64 tests of a tuple of 2,000 and 16,000 strings, 1.0 times the work (limit 3); `is str` and `is int` on a concatenated string copy no bytes. Control, the old `is_in`: 8.0 times, and 2 bytes copied |
| Z16 | `codegen::dispatch` chose a branch by a test per block | O(k) per dispatch, below `kernel_match`, which clones all k patterns | fixed: a Cranelift `Switch` (jump table); constant factor, no order change |
| Z17 | `zir::validate` (`zir/mod.rs`) formatted the path of every node, input and region as a string (`format!("{path}.{k}")`, `format!("{at}/{j}")`, and a name per state use in `src_name`), eagerly and not only into an error; the path is as long as the depth | O(n·depth) bytes allocated | fixed by T2-mid (b35b33f5): one path buffer, extended by `write!` and cut back by `truncate` as the walk enters and leaves a node or region, copied into a `String` only for an error, and state uses keyed by edge, not by a path string. Before: whole `lower` at depth 500 against 4000 allocated 40.3 and 32.6 times the bytes (`prices` and `lets_of_lambdas`), and `validate` alone about 4 times per doubling. After (ZIR-validate-bytes, inputs built before timing, depth 4000 against 32000, load 36): 65,602,358 against 524,807,734 bytes (8.0 times) and 98,906,926 against 893,515,806 (9.0 times); instructions 8.1 and 8.4 times, about 2 to 3 s per test. Tests `l01_33_lowering::scaling::a_name_read_at_every_depth_scales` and `lowering_lambdas_bound_by_nested_lets_scales` check bytes and instructions again (`scaling::check`). Negative control, not committed: one copy of the path per node put back in `validate` gives 35.7 and 30.6 times the bytes at depth 4000 against 32000 (load 39), and both tests fail on bytes. At depth 500 such a control passed (14.1 and 14.6 times), which is why the tests start at 4000 |
| Z18 | `zir/canon.rs` `normal_edge` applied the facets rule as written: one swap or one drop per step, each step scanning the facets from the front | O(F²) steps of O(F) each for `F` facets out of order; 0.0081, 0.063, 0.356 s at F = 100, 200, 400 | fixed by ZIR-canon-sort: drop the hints, sort the facets once (`sort_unstable`), `dedup`, then `Domain::normal`, `O(F log F)` comparisons; the same normal form, since a facet's `Ord` is total and ties exactly `==`. Test `l01_32_canonical_zir::scaling::canonicalizing_an_edge_of_many_facets_is_n_log_n` (F = 100 and 800, facets in reverse order, every third a hint); the old loop fails it at 508.7 times the instructions. `l01_32_canonical_zir::facet_order` checks that `Value`'s `Ord` agrees with `==` and is transitive on every kind of facet value |
| R14 | feature `counts` of `zed-rt` and `zed-native` (OSP-diag): each cell records the node that allocated it; `Heap::survivors` groups the live cells by type and node | O(1) more per allocation; O(c log g) per listing, for `c` cells and `g` groups | fixed by design; with the feature off `Heap::allocating` is empty and no field is added. Test `l01_37_oracle::counts::listing_the_live_cells_scales`: 20,000 and 160,000 live cells by 2,501 and 20,001 nodes, 10.2 times the work (limit 24) |
| Z19 | `zir/lower.rs` `Lower::add` took a node of equal 256-bit key as the same node, with no confirmation (L1.37a) | one hash lookup and O(log k) shape comparisons per add for k nodes of its key, each at most the size of the smaller node (`impl Ord for canon::Shape`, an explicit stack over the two nodes' regions, skipping shared regions); k is 1 in the full build but for a 256-bit collision, about n/256 in the `short-addresses` build | fixed (L1.37a): the nodes of one key are kept in a `BTreeMap` ordered by `canon::Shape` (the normal-form bytes of the node's shell, then its regions by contents), so a key never decides equality alone and colliding keys cost O(log k) comparisons, in both builds. Tests `l01_37a_short_addresses::collide` (601 sums stay 601 prim nodes under 8-bit keys; a map trusting the key keeps at most 256), `l01_37a_short_addresses::scaling::confirming_a_key_reads_each_node_once` and `many_equal_nodes_are_found_by_their_keys` (n 500 and 4000, order 1). Controls, not committed: trusting the key, 8-bit build: 601 sums became 232 nodes, 301 became 180, and the oracle left closures live (4 of 19 tests fail); confirming by a scan of every node of the region: the scaling tests killed at 60 s. Repair #1 replaced the chain of nodes of one key, which cost O(k) confirmations per add, each encoding both nodes again, by the ordered map. Control, the chain at 62bca216 in the 8-bit build: 5 of the 20 tests of `l01_37a_short_addresses::scaling` and `l01_33_lowering::scaling` fail on bytes allocated (24.1 to 34.0 times, limit 24); the ordered map passes 20 of 20 there. Full build, instructions before and after: `l01_33_lowering::scaling` 119.9G and 121.2G, `confirming_a_key_reads_each_node_once` 768M and 792M, `many_equal_nodes_are_found_by_their_keys` 602M and 426M. A linear scan of the ordered map, comparing stored bytes, passed every scaling test even at 16,000 and 128,000 sums (the rest of the lowering costs far more per node than n/256 byte comparisons), so no test of feasible size separates it from the map |
| Z20 | `zir/text.rs` and `zir/binary.rs` read a family literal through `Family::of`, which panics when a key repeats | a panic on damaged data, not a cost | fixed by ZIR-family-key: `text::family` maps the keys to keys (`-0.0` to `0.0`), sorts them (`sort_unstable_by`) and refuses the least repeated key, then builds the family by `Family::from_leaves`, O(n log n) comparisons for n entries. Test `l01_31_zir::scaling::reading_a_wide_family_and_refusing_a_repeated_key_is_n_log_n_in_its_entries`, n = 2,000 and 16,000, in instructions: text read 9.5, text refusal 9.0, binary read 9.9 times (limit 24). Negative control, not committed: a pairwise check of the keys fails it at 54.2 times (90.1 M to 4,880.9 M instructions) |
| Z21 | an executable of `zedc build` (step L1.36a, `zed-native/src/exe.rs`, `link.rs`): its start decodes the program from its data (ZIR's binary form) and plans it again to rebuild the tables the code reads; `zedc build` runs the system linker over the object file and the runtime library | O(n) to decode, the planner's cost to plan (Z4), O(n) tables; the link is the linker's | measured, linear: `l01_36a_executables::scaling::an_executable_reading_n_files_scales`, a program of n reads of files its n arguments name, n = 200 and 1,600: 52.2 M and 429.3 M instructions, 8.2 times (limit 24, load 64), the child process counted by the inherited counter. Negative control, run once and not committed: a quadratic loop over the data at the start gave 60.9 times and failed. Planning at start, not storing the tables, keeps the data to the graph alone; `-Wl,--gc-sections` cut a link from about 3 s to 1.5 s and an executable from 41 MB to 14 MB |
| Z22 | `zir/binary.rs` checks each name and binder it reads (ZIR-name-binary) by `text::not_a_name` / `not_a_binder` | O(length) per name, so O(n) in the data | fixed by ZIR-name-binary (new code, linear): `l01_31_zir::names::reading_names_in_binary_is_linear_in_their_bytes`, n = 40,000 and 320,000 (n/8 atoms with 8-byte names and fields, and one binder n bytes long), passes at limit 24; by time 4.28 and 36.38 ms, 8.5 times, load 65. Negative control, not committed: a check that rereads every prefix of a name fails it at 62.9 times the instructions (102.0 M to 6,415.7 M) at n = 2,000 and 16,000 |
| Z23 | `zed_native::link::runtime_of`, `build_symbol` and the check in `link::build` (NATIVE-lib-pick): the runtime library built with an executable is found by reading the executable's symbol table for `link::build`'s mangled name, whose hash differs between builds of zed-native with other features, and each `libzed_native*.a`'s archive symbol index for that name | O(S) for an executable of S symbols, O(s) for each library of s indexed symbols, read once each, with no table built | measured, linear: `l01_36a_executables::runtime::finding_the_library_scales`, an object file and two archives of n symbols each, n = 20,000 and 160,000: 58.0 M and 462.3 M instructions, 8.0 times, and 8.0 times the bytes (limit 24, load 68 to 86). Negative control, run once and not committed: a quadratic scan of the symbols already read gave 64.1 times and failed (it took 453 s, over the 60 s limit for controls) |
| Z24 | `zir/text.rs` writes and reads the binders the compiler makes (ZIR-text-binders): `pattern` writes a canonical binder `\0k` as `$$k`, and `P::generated_binder` reads `$k` and `$$k` and accepts them by `not_a_binder` | O(length) per binder, so O(n) in the text | fixed by ZIR-text-binders (new code, linear): `l01_31_zir::scaling::writing_and_reading_generated_binders_is_linear_in_their_number`, a tuple of n = 12,500 and 100,000 binders, `$k` and `$$k` in turn, written and read, passes at limit 24 (load5 about 60). Negative control, not committed: a reader that counts the `$` in the rest of the line at each binder fails it at 66.0 times the instructions (45.8 M to 3,019.8 M) at n = 1,250 and 10,000, load5 81. |
| Z25 | `zir/legalize.rs` `legalize` (LOWER-legalize): the expansions a back end declares, at every depth, and the set of nodes it swapped by their number in preorder; `zed-native/src/tables.rs` `Tables::new` numbers the nodes the same way, a `fam`, `fix` or `gen` node's regions counted by `legalize::nodes_in`; `zir/lower.rs` `raise_node` raises a swapped node as the operator written | O(n); a bit set of n bits; O(1) per node raised | fixed by design: one walk from a stack of regions on the heap, each node moved once, a derived node's certificate legalized before it is put in the node's place, so no node is read twice; no size switch, the set is a constant per back end. A region a graph shares, which lowering never makes, is copied and legalized once per use, as validation and planning read it (PLAN-shared-regions plans it once). Test `l01_36_cranelift::legalize::legalization_is_linear_in_the_graph` (20,000 and 160,000 derived nodes each holding `>` and `>=`: 7.97 times the instructions, 8.00 times the bytes, load 57). Repair 1, numbering the swapped nodes: the same walk numbers each kept node once and sets one bit per swap, so the pass stays linear; the test still passes at 20,000 and 160,000 (35.8 MB and 286.3 MB allocated, 8.0 times the bytes, limit 24, load 61-67). `Tables::new` counts an unplanned region's nodes once each by `nodes_in`, and `raise_node` reads one bit per node it raises. An executable of `zedc build` (Z21) holds the graph as lowered and its start legalizes it before planning, one more linear pass, since ZIR's binary form does not record the swapped nodes |
| Z26 | the content address of an exact number past `i64` (CONST-wide-addr): `zir/binary.rs` (under `zir::canon::address` and the node keys) and `canon/serial.rs` (under the kernel address) wrote its decimal digits, `Rat::numer_text` and `denom_text` | about n^1.5 in the bits n (num-bigint's conversion to decimal): 0.25 s at 2^20 bits, 1.24 s at 2^22, 15.6 s at 2^25, about 80 minutes extrapolated at 2^30 (load 54 to 68) | fixed: each writes the sign and the 64-bit limbs of `N` and `D`, the value `±N/D` in lowest terms, built by `Rat::built_parts` (a power of 2 is a shift, `built` in `num.rs`), O(n); `canon::VERSION` 3 to 4 (PRIM-hidden-names had made it 3). Lowering a constant of 2^30 bits: 0.75 s random, 0.56 s for `2^(2^30)` kept as a power. Tests `l01_32_canonical_zir::wide_constants::addressing_a_wide_constant_is_linear_in_its_bits`, 2^17 and 2^20 bits, power 1: 5.5 times the work in instructions (144K to 787K, ZIR address of a random number; 5.5 negative, 5.4 for `2^n` kept, 5.7 kernel address), 7.9 times the bytes (load 58 to 60); and `..._stays_linear_over_64_times_the_bits`, 2^14 and 2^20 bits, limit 192 times. Negative control, run once and not committed: the decimal digits gave 22.3 times the instructions for 8 times the bits (23.8M to 532M), under the 24 that `check_order` allows for order n, which is why the second test compares 64 times the bits; there the control gave 295.1 times the bytes and failed. Open: a power of an odd prime kept in a `Rat` is built by num-bigint's squaring, whose multiplication (Karatsuba, Toom-3) is above O(n log n), as it was under the decimal digits; interval ends and congruence classes past `i64` are still serialized by their decimal text (S33, S36) |

## Complexity tradeoffs

Sites that give up some inputs, or some precision, to avoid the cost of a complete algorithm,
and that had no row before (docs/reviews/tradeoffs.md, finding 6). Each site carries a
comment beginning `Complexity tradeoff:` that names this row. Columns: where, what is given
up and the answer those inputs get, why (the cost of the complete algorithm), and the input
that triggers it with the test. Rows marked "violation" switch behavior at a size or a
magnitude and break the no-thresholds rule; TD-ends, TD-values, CAP-power and CAP-bits
replaced them and updated their rows.

| ID | Where | Given up | Why | Trigger and test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | `store/presburger.rs` `hull_from` (fixed by TD-ends, row S33) | nothing: every bound is exact at any magnitude | ends are unbounded integers (`End::Fin(Num)`), one machine word while they fit in `i64` | a bound whose magnitude exceeds `i64`. `l01_38_presburger::omega::a_bound_past_i64_is_exact`, `omega::a_model_may_hold_values_past_128_bits` |
| C2 | `store/presburger.rs` `products` | a product of two variables is an opaque atom, bounded only by products of pairs of linear premises; a goal that needs other nonlinear reasoning is `Entailment::NotProved` | entailment over integer polynomials is undecidable (Hilbert's tenth problem); over the reals it is doubly exponential in the variables | a product atom in the premises or the goal. `l01_38_presburger::entailment::a_product_is_bounded_only_through_the_premises_on_its_factors` |
| C3 | `store/product.rs` `Lattice for` the type facet, `widen` | a type that grows within a base type (more literals, more tuple shapes) becomes the whole base type, so a loop's type keeps none of the members it gained | types have infinite ascending chains (`#a \| #b \| …`), so the fixpoint needs a widening | a widening at which `next` has a value in a base type part that `self` lacks. `l01_39_reduced_product::controls::a_type_widens_to_its_whole_base_type` |
| C4 | `store/product.rs` `Lattice for` the type facet, `narrow` | in a part that `self` does not hold whole, a smaller `next` is not taken (`1 \| 2` narrowed by `1` stays `1 \| 2`) | types have infinite descending chains (`int`, `int` without `0`, …); with this rule each part changes at most once | a part of `self` neither empty nor the whole base type or universe part. `controls::a_type_widens_to_its_whole_base_type` (its narrowing cases) |
| C5 | `store/product.rs` type facet listing (`reduce_domains`) | an interval with more members than twice its pieces is not listed into the type, so the type keeps all of `int` there | listing costs time and space in the members, exponential in the bits written | a finite interval set whose members exceed twice its pieces (relative to the written endpoints, not a constant). `l01_39_reduced_product::controls::a_listed_set_is_enumerated_and_a_range_is_not` |
| C6 | `store/product.rs` `Scope::enumerate` | a constraint is enumerated only when at most one of its names is open, its form shows that it ends (`stage::ends`) and the name's members are listed; any other narrows nothing there | enumerating k open names costs the product of their members, exponential in k; whether a constraint ends is the halting problem | two or more open names, a constraint that calls itself or loops, or a range not listed. `controls::enumeration_keeps_the_members_a_written_constraint_holds_at`, `controls::a_listed_set_is_enumerated_and_a_range_is_not` |
| C7 | `store/interval.rs` `End` (fixed by TD-ends, row S33) | nothing: no end is rounded or dropped; `End::from_i128`, `End::lower`, `End::upper` and `interval::rounded` are gone | ends are unbounded integers (`End::Fin(Num)`), one machine word while they fit in `i64` | an endpoint whose magnitude exceeds `i64`. `l01_25_preimage_catalog::entries::bounds_past_i64_keep_the_box`, `l01_38_presburger::omega::a_bound_past_i64_is_exact`, `l01_25_preimage_catalog::overflow` (the complement of a set at an end of `i64`, `store/revision.rs` `complement_in`, S22c) |
| C8 | `store/interval.rs` `IntervalSet::narrow`; `zed/prelude/lattice.zed` `intervalNarrow` | a finite end of `next` moves back out to the nearest end of `self` or landmark on its side, so a tighter bound a loop shows between landmarks is lost (`0..=99` narrowed by `5..=50` stays `0..=99`) | interval sets have infinite descending chains; with this rule a chain of narrowings has O(\|K\|) elements for K landmarks. S13 measures the widening phase | a finite end of `next` with an end of `self` or a landmark beyond it. `m0_05_widening_and_iteration::widening::a_narrowing_keeps_old_ends_and_takes_a_bound_for_an_infinite_one`, `widening::interval_narrowing_chains_stay_on_the_first_ends_and_the_landmarks` |
| C9 | `store/lattice.rs` `Congruence::narrow`; `zed/prelude/lattice.zed` `congruenceNarrow` | a class below the top keeps itself when `next` refines it to a larger modulus (`0 mod 2` then `0 mod 4` stays `0 mod 2`); only the top, the bottom and a constant are replaced | without this a descending chain of moduli need not end | a narrowing from a class of modulus other than 1 to one of a larger modulus. `m0_05_widening_and_iteration::widening::a_congruence_narrows_at_most_three_times` |
| C10 | `store/affine.rs` `Bounds::offset` (fixed by TD-ends, row S33) | nothing: a moved bound is exact at any magnitude | as C7 | a bound whose constant passes `i64`. `l01_24_refinement_lattices::bounds::an_offset_moves_every_member` |
| C11 | `store/affine.rs` `Bounds::leq` | bounds with different terms are never compared, so `leq` may answer false where the element is below (`int[..=n]` against `int[..=n + m]` with `m >= 0` known); an iteration may then take one more step or widen | comparing affine bounds with different terms is a Presburger entailment between linear facts on the symbols; this check is linear in the bounds | bounds of `other` whose terms no bound of `self` has. `l01_24_refinement_lattices::bounds::the_order_is_sound_and_holds_of_meets_and_joins` |
| C12 | `store/affine.rs` `Bounds::widen` | a symbolic bound that appears during a loop is dropped; only bounds the loop's entry had survive the widening | symbolic bounds form infinite ascending chains (`n`, `n + 1`, …) | a bound of `next` that `self` lacks. `bounds::widening_chains_are_finite`, `l01_24_refinement_lattices::loops::a_counting_loop_keeps_its_symbolic_bound` |
| C13 | `store/affine.rs` `Bounds::narrow` | a side that already has symbolic bounds keeps them, so a tighter bound a later step shows (`..=n - 2` after `..=n - 1`) is not taken | symbolic bounds form infinite descending chains; with this rule each side changes at most once | a side of `self` with at least one symbolic bound. `loops::a_counting_loop_keeps_its_symbolic_bound`, `loops::a_loop_counting_down_keeps_its_symbolic_bound` |
| C14 | `store/preimage.rs` `union` | a union of boxes that is not a box is answered by its box hull, which holds points neither side has (necessary, not exact) | a union kept exactly grows to 2^k boxes for k disjunctions and branches, and every later operation pays for each box | two boxes that differ in two or more inputs, neither within the other. `l01_25_preimage_catalog::scopes::a_union_of_boxes_is_exact_when_it_is_a_box` |
| C15 | `store/preimage.rs` `StopKind::Outside` at `fix`, `gen` and `fold` | the transfer stops at the loop: the term's answer is the whole box (necessary only), and the stop is reported | reading through a loop unfolds it once per iteration, which need not end; a fixpoint over the loop is the full analysis the transfer avoids | a `fix`, `gen` or `fold` term between the demand and an input. `l01_25_preimage_catalog::stops::a_term_outside_the_scalar_catalog_is_reported` (through `sum` of a tuple); `stops::a_fold_between_the_demand_and_an_input_stops_the_transfer`, `stops::a_gen_between_the_demand_and_an_input_stops_the_transfer` and `stops::a_fix_between_the_demand_and_an_input_stops_the_transfer` stop at each loop, on an unbounded and on a listed domain |
| C16 | `store/preimage.rs` `project` (HC4) | each operand is narrowed alone against the other's forward interval, so the relation between operands is lost and the box may hold points that fail the demand | the exact preimage of a nonlinear operator is not a box, and finding it costs the points of the box (undecidable for polynomial tests over unbounded integers) | an operator with no exact catalog entry, or two operands that both read inputs. `l01_25_preimage_catalog::entries::a_strict_disjunction_with_a_failing_side_is_necessary_only` |
| C17 | `store/preimage.rs` `mod_pre` | the periods between the first and the last are merged into one piece, which also holds the members with other remainders | listing every period costs pieces in the width of `within` divided by `c`, exponential in the bits written | `within` spanning more than two periods of `c`, or unbounded. `l01_25_preimage_catalog::random::a_remainder_inverts_to_its_first_and_last_periods_and_one_piece_between` |
| C18 | `store/preimage.rs` `enumerate` | a box with a range wider than its endpoints, with two or more inputs of several members, or with a term whose ending is not shown is not enumerated; it gets the catalog's and the linear relation's answer, which may be wider | a box's points are the product of its axes, exponential in the inputs and the bits of each range; a term not shown to end may not end | those three properties, each relative to what is written (docs/thresholds.md replacement 8). `l01_25_preimage_catalog::entries::a_listed_box_is_enumerated_and_a_range_is_not` |
| C19 | `store/preimage.rs` `POWER_FACTORS` (removed by CAP-power; see S30: any number of equal factors is read as a power. `preimage_ref.rs`, the frozen copy of the old transfer that tests compare with, keeps it) | a product of more than 64 equal factors is read as nested products (the HC4 entry for `*`), wider than the power's exact inverse | the stated reason, that the power would leave `i64`, does not hold: `Int` is unbounded and `x ** k` inverts by a k-th root at a cost in the bits of its bounds | more than 64 equal factors. `l01_25_preimage_catalog::scopes::equal_factors_written_twice_are_read_as_a_square` (below the constant only) |
| C20 | `stage.rs` `ends` | a term that ends but whose form does not show it (recursion, `fix`, `gen`, a call through a parameter) counts as possibly not ending: the build stage leaves it to run time, and enumeration and `@exact` skip it | whether a term ends is undecidable; a step count would switch behavior at a size (docs/thresholds.md replacement 6) | any of those forms in the term or in a function it calls. `l01_28_staging::mix::a_term_whose_form_does_not_show_it_ends_is_left_to_run`, `mix::a_term_whose_form_shows_it_ends_is_built_with_no_fuel` |
| C21 | `zed-kernel` `types.rs` `Type::values(cap)` (removed) | none: removed in T6 repair 2 (SYNTHESIS Q4) with its cap; the lazy `Type::each_value` lists a type `Type::finite` accepts one value at a time, so a caller pays per value it takes; since TD-values the types inside its boxes are made lazily too (K28) | | `l01_23_subtyping::listing` |
| C22 | `zed-kernel` `types.rs` `Type::finite` (was `Type::listed`, removed in T6 repair 2) | a type with finitely many values that `Type::finite` does not show finite (a recursive type, a payload, a struct, a function, or a negated tuple type left in the box it is met with, such as `(int, 0) - (int - 0, 0)`) is treated as not finite, so the Trick (`stage`) keeps it whole | deciding whether a Boolean combination of tuple types is finite would decide its emptiness; `finite` reads the type alone and compares no size | any of those forms. `l01_23_subtyping::scaling::listing_a_diagram_with_exponentially_many_paths_scales`, `l01_28_staging::trick::a_finite_domain_is_split_on_the_values_passed_and_an_infinite_one_is_kept` |
| C23 | `zed-kernel` `num.rs` `MAX_BITS` (removed by CAP-bits; see K30: no size limit, a power kept as powers of primes and not built) | an integer or rational past 2^20 bits gives `KernelError::Overflow`, though `Int` is unbounded | `x ** e` builds a result whose size is exponential in the bits of `e`, so one short expression could take all memory and time | a result, or for a power a predicted result, of more than `MAX_BITS` bits. `m0_09_kernel_evaluator::a_power_by_a_huge_exponent` |
| C24 | `store/utvpi.rs` `potential`, the search on a graph with cycles (UTVPI-GOR, UTVPI-NEAR, UTVPI-union) | nothing: every answer is exact; since UTVPI-union the graph is split into strongly connected components, taken in topological order with one offset each (O(V + E)), and each component with an edge inside is searched alone; the work there is at most five times the cheapest of five searches run one unit each in turn, the first to finish deciding, where the first two share their labels (`Shared`) and run again as a second such pair on the graph with every edge reversed, whose potential negated is one of the graph (since UTVPI-both-slow): Goldberg and Radzik's (O(V·E) worst case, two or three passes on most graphs), BF-Dijkstra (`negsssp::Rounds`, O((E + V log V)·r) for r the most negative edges on a shortest path) and Duong's deterministic near-linear search (`negsssp`, Duong 2026, arXiv 2609.05590, local branch only: O(E·log³V·log log V·log(V·W)) for the largest weight W, inferred from the paper's lemmas) | Duong's search alone has a large constant: on BAD-GOR augmented it takes 0.91G instructions at k = 256 and 11.5G at k = 2,048 (12.6 times), about 60 times the race's cost at k = 256, so the decided race of Goldberg and Radzik with Duong alone grew 67 times from k to 8k, past the limit of 24 (measured, UTVPI-NEAR); BF-Dijkstra was added as the third search; the other two decide the same, since every potential gives the same parity decision (`odd_zero_cycle`) | BAD-GOR of Cherkassky and Goldberg augmented with heavy random edges and permuted labels (Cassis et al. 2025, arXiv 2502.11999): Goldberg and Radzik alone take 129 passes at k = 256 and 1,026 at k = 2,048, 78.2M → 4.68G instructions, 59.8 times (negative control, run once, not kept); the three-way race 26.8M → 209M, 7.8 times (`l01_38_presburger::stages::bad_gor_scales`); BF-Dijkstra alone 8.8M → 74.4M. Duong's search alone on random feasible graphs, 512 → 4,096 nodes (`l01_38_presburger::near::duong_scales_alone`). The cyclic hub, 512 → 4,096 variables, 7.9 times (`the_cyclic_hub_scales`; Bellman–Ford–Moore 41.9). The pass in topological order taken when the graph has no cycle, O(V + E): BAD-GOR unaugmented joined with the fan below without its edges back to `a`, k = 256 → 2,048, 3.13M → 25.1M instructions, 8.0 times; alone on it, Goldberg and Radzik 52.8 times, BF-Dijkstra 64.9 times, Duong 10.8 times but 573M → 6,203M (`l01_38_presburger::stages::bad_gor_and_a_fan_with_no_cycle_scale`, UTVPI-dag-test). Negative control, run once and not committed: with the pass removed the race takes 141.3M → 9,142.5M, 64.7 times, and 43.9 times the bytes (load 40 to 54), while `the_acyclic_hub_scales` still passes; rerun it when UTVPI-union changes the race. Failing (measured, UTVPI-NEAR repair): BAD-GOR augmented joined with a fan (a path of weight -1 edges into a hub fanning out to k leaves, which makes BF-Dijkstra take k rounds of k work) makes both quick searches quadratic while they are still cheaper than Duong's, so the race takes 201.9M → 12,236M instructions from k = 256 to 2,048, 60.6 times, past the limit of 24 (`l01_38_presburger::stages::bad_gor_and_a_fan_scale`, ignored); alone, Goldberg and Radzik 80.8M → 4,699M (58.2 times), BF-Dijkstra 62.3M → 3,708M (59.5 times), Duong 1,176M → 14,070M (12.0 times). In units of work, Goldberg and Radzik 546k → 33.4M, BF-Dijkstra 344k → 19.3M, Duong 3.72M → 43.1M and 404M at k = 16,384; the race is at most three times the cheapest in units, so asymptotically Duong's bound holds, but the crossover lies past k = 2,048. Starting each merge's BF-Dijkstra from the copies of the tails of negative edges only cut Duong's cost on this family from 1,880M to 1,176M at k = 256; passing would need about 2.7 units per edge per scaling step against about 14 now and about 1 for the pass over the edges alone, so a cut in this constant cannot pass it; a near-linear search with a small constant is needed (open: item UTVPI-union). Main before UTVPI-NEAR (Goldberg and Radzik alone, not resumable) takes 53.3M → 2,993M on this family, 56.2 times: quadratic as well, and about 4 times cheaper at both sizes, since the race does at least one unit of each search per unit of the one that finishes (measured at load 76–82, UTVPI-NEAR repair 2). Duong's figures on BAD-GOR alone (0.91G, 11.5G) predate that change. **Ruling (orchestrator, 2026-11-03 12:21, after UTVPI-NEAR verify 2):** the gap on this family is accepted for the merge. The race costs about 4 times main: main 53.3M → 2,993M, the race 202.3M → 12,236M (both measured in repair 2), so main grows 56.2 times and the race 60.5 times, the same growth, and both fail the limit of 24. The extra cost is a constant bounded by the number of racers: the race does at most one unit of each of its three searches per unit of the one that finishes, and Goldberg and Radzik's search made resumable costs 80.8M against main's 53.3M at k = 256. In exchange BAD-GOR passes (main 59.7 times there, the race 7.8). The fix belongs to item UTVPI-union. **UTVPI-union (2026-11-05): fixed; every family below is 8.0 to 8.7 times from k = 256 to 2,048, in instructions (load 55 to 77).** (1) Split by strongly connected components (Tarjan, `components`, now shared with `odd_zero_cycle`), each component searched alone, then the components in topological order, each shifted by the least of `bound[v] − q[v]` over its nodes, where `bound[v]` is the least of 0 and `p[u] + w` over edges into `v` from earlier components; this replaces the Kahn pass and decides a graph with no cycle in O(V + E). On `bad_gor_and_a_fan_scale` (BAD-GOR and the fan share no variable) it alone gives 35.6M → 284M, 8.0 times. (2) Condensation does not fix the connected variant: with heavy edges both ways between the halves and a heavy ring on the fan (each fan node to the next two, weight n², so fill > 0), the halves are one component of the positive literals, but the half slow for Goldberg and Radzik and the half slow for BF-Dijkstra fall in opposite signs of the literal graph (a row `x[u] − x[v] + w ≥ 0` is the edge `2u → 2v` and the reversed edge `2v+1 → 2u+1`), so the split still separates the slow parts and that variant passes at 8.1 times. With the fan's edges reversed as well (`union_family(k, true, true)`), both slow parts share one component, and the old race on it takes 459k → 25.9M units, 56 times, won by Duong's search at k = 2,048. (3) `Shared`: Goldberg and Radzik's search, at the start of each pass, takes the labels BF-Dijkstra lowered; BF-Dijkstra, at the end of each round, takes those Goldberg and Radzik lowered. Proved: every label is the weight of a walk from the source; pass i of Goldberg and Radzik leaves each label at most the shortest path of i edges, and round i of BF-Dijkstra at most the shortest walk with i negative edges, both monotone under extra decreases, so the n-pass and V-round tests for a negative cycle and Goldberg and Radzik's cycle test stay sound; a label taken mid-round would be skipped if its node was settled, hence the round boundary. On the joined, reversed family the race takes 38.1M → 332.8M, 8.7 times (`the_joined_union_family_scales`); through `decide`, `the_joined_union_family_scales_through_decide` (the old code 618M → 18,351M, 29.7 times). Other families: the fan reversed, not joined, 39.7M → 329.9M (8.3); `bad_gor_and_a_fan_scale` 36.1M → 301.2M (8.3); BAD-GOR alone 25.1M → 209.3M (8.3); the acyclic family 5.29M → 42.3M (8.0). (4) The wide-row variant (a fresh unit variable `y` in `y + x[0] − x[a] ≥ 0`): `decide` drops `y` as bounded on one side only, and the halves reach the search apart, so it passed on the old code too (421M → 3,852M, 9.1 times, the same as unjoined); `the_wide_row_family_scales_through_decide` and `the_union_family_scales_through_decide` stay as tests. Candidates, scored 0 to 10: split and `Shared` together, all families 8.0 to 8.7 times, 10 (kept); `Shared` alone, all families pass but the acyclic family costs 20.1M → 166.4M, 3.8 times the split, 7; split alone, the joined reversed family 56 times, 2; a cut in Duong's constant, rejected in UTVPI-NEAR repair (about 2.7 units per edge per step needed, about 14 measured), 1; BCF, below, 1. **BCF rejected** (Bringmann, Cassis and Fischer, "Negative-Weight Single-Source Shortest Paths in Near-Linear Time: Now Faster!", FOCS 2023; arXiv 2304.05279, checked against its LaTeX source in UTVPI-union verify: a Las Vegas algorithm in O((E + V·log log V)·log²V·log(V·W)) time "with high probability (and in expectation)" (Theorem 1.1), O(E·log²V·log(V·W)·log log V) in its abstract): (a) its bound is expected time over random choices; DESIGN's rule on low complexity (DESIGN.md, "Every algorithm is of low complexity") admits randomness only as a last resort at points derived from content, and with a seed derived from the input the bound is no longer an expectation over that input, so a family can be slow for its fixed seed (inferred); (b) a deterministic near-linear search, Duong's, already gives the asymptotic bound in the race; (c) it is built on the same scaling and low-diameter decomposition as Duong's, so its constant is likely of the same order, which is what made Duong's search lose below k = 2,048 (inferred, not measured); (d) no measured family needs it. A family slow for both Goldberg and Radzik and BF-Dijkstra along the same shortest paths, constructed in UTVPI-union verify: `split_bad_gor`, BAD-GOR with each weight 1 edge of its path split into edges of weight -1 and 2. From k = 256 to 2,048 in time (load 65 to 73), Goldberg and Radzik alone grows 232 times, BF-Dijkstra alone 131 times, Duong alone 223 ms to 7.27 s, and the race 432 ms to 33.8 s (78 times, 1.9 and 4.6 times Duong alone); from k = 128 to 1,024 the race allocates 33.5 times the bytes, past the limit 24, while Duong alone passes. The guess that the labels BF-Dijkstra passes slow Goldberg and Radzik down was wrong (UTVPI-both-slow, in instructions from k = 128 to 1,024, load 58 to 68): Goldberg and Radzik alone 32.7M → 1,694M (51.8 times, 127 and 1,024 passes), BF-Dijkstra alone 46.6M → 2,826M (60.7 times), `Shared` 98.2M → 5,943M (60.5 times; labels passed one way only, 56.5 and 58.2 times; not passed, 57.3 times, but then `the_joined_union_family_scales` takes 287M → 16.5G), Duong alone 535M → 6,218M (11.6 times), the race 175M → 9,778M (55.9 times): both quick searches are quadratic on this family by themselves. **UTVPI-both-slow (2026-11-05): fixed.** The reverse of a graph has the potential `-q` for each potential `q` of the graph and the same cycles; on the reverse of `split_bad_gor` both quick searches are fast, so a second `Shared` on the reversed graph joins the race (`utvpi::race`, with `reversed` and `negated`), one step after each unit of Duong's search. `stages::split_bad_gor_scales` passes: 24.2M → 198M, 8.2 times (bytes 3.90M → 31.6M, 8.1 times); `the_joined_union_family_scales` 50.9M → 411M (8.1), `bad_gor_and_a_fan_scale` 40.7M → 324M (8.0), `bad_gor_scales` 26.5M → 206M (7.8). The differential test runs `Shared` on the reverse, negated, against Bellman–Ford on 6,000 random graphs; a mutant without the negation fails three unit tests. Negative control, run once and not committed: with the reverse pair disabled `split_bad_gor_scales` fails at 32.4 times the bytes (18.9M → 612M; load 39 to 50). Still failing (measured, open: item UTVPI-both-ways): `split_bad_gor_both_ways`, the family beside its reverse in one component, slow for both pairs: the race 385M → 30,322M instructions (78.7 times, 51.2 times the bytes; 298M → 21,980M, 73.6 times, without the reverse pair; load 39 to 54), `stages::split_bad_gor_both_ways_scales`, ignored. Inferred from a model, not measured: with the quick searches quadratic and Duong's linear on a family, and Duong's search first to finish from size k* on, the race grows about 8·k*/k times from k to 8k for k*/8 ≤ k ≤ k*, past 24 for k < k*/3, whatever quick searches race; only a smaller constant in Duong's search moves such families under the limit. UTVPI-dag-test's control rerun: with the split removed (the race over the whole graph) `bad_gor_and_a_fan_with_no_cycle_scale` and `the_acyclic_hub_scales` both pass (20.1M → 166.4M, 8.3 times), so the split was compared with `integer_empty_by(.., Search::BfDijkstra)` at k = 256: 5.29M against 44.6M (BF-Dijkstra 2,891M at 2,048, 64.8 times). Negative control, the code before UTVPI-union, run once and not committed: `bad_gor_and_a_fan_scale` fails at 48.8 times the bytes (202M → 12,236M instructions, measured before), `the_joined_union_family_scales` at 47.6 times the bytes (253M → 15,604M instructions); the three tests through `decide` pass on it except the joined one (load 44 to 69) |
| C-WIDEN | `check::widen`, the intervals of the widening warning | intervals come from literals, the operators, names bound by `=` and the joins of `if`, `else` and blocks, with no narrowing by a test and no relation between operands: a parameter, a call or a value a test bounds is any `int`, and its operations are not reported; `x - x` with `x` in `int[lo..=hi]` gets `int[lo - hi..=hi - lo]`, and is reported when that interval leaves the word though the value is 0 | narrowing by tests needs the facts on each path and relating operands needs a relational domain, whose cost per operation grows with the number of names in scope (exponential in the worst case for Presburger formulas), where an interval costs O(1); the store's forward transfer over the definition graph, which narrows by tests and relations, is the complete reading and replaces this walk at step L1.40b; until then a linear walk gives the warning for the common case of large literals | a parameter in an operation; `x - x` with `x` near the ends of the word. `l01_29_diagnostics::widen::a_parameter_is_not_bounded_and_does_not_warn`, `a_difference_of_a_value_with_itself_is_read_as_a_range` |
| C25 | `store/interval.rs` `bit_word` (step L1.39a) | each operand of a bit word but the `x` of `shl` and `shr` is read by its hull, and the result is at most two intervals, so a hole in an operand is lost and `bitAnd`, `bitOr` and `bitXor` are bounded by their operands' digits, not by the digits they share; `f64Bits` is `int[0..]`, since `2 ** 64` is past `i64` | the words are not monotone, so the exact image of a union of pieces is a search over the pieces' digits; the hull rule is O(1) per word, and `shl` and `shr`, monotone on each sign, map each piece of `x` by its corners in O(pieces) | an operand of more than one piece, or `bitAnd`, `bitOr`, `bitXor` on operands of more than one value. `l01_39a_bit_words::transfer` (soundness on 100,000 random sets of intervals); the known-bits domain of step L1.40a is the complete solution |
| C26 | `check/mod.rs` `Ck::known` (step L1.39a) | an operand's domain is decided from its text (literals, names bound to such text, `+ - * div mod **`, the bit words) and its listed values, not from the reduced product's cell, so an int that only a test narrows (`if n >= 0 { popCount(n) }`) or that comes from input is rejected as possibly negative, as `**`'s exponent is | the checker runs before the store's iteration; asking the store at each call would run a fixpoint per call, where reading the text is O(1) per node, each node read once, children before parents on an explicit stack, and a binder's value read only when a bit word or an exponent reads its name. A complete solution needs int-range types on parameters and results (step L10, ledger BIT-checker-ranges), so a signature states the domain, or the checker querying the reduced product's cell for the operand once the store's fixpoint has run | an operand whose sign or width only a test, a parameter or a tuple component shows. `l01_39a_bit_words::errors::an_operand_not_shown_in_its_domain_is_rejected`; cost: `l01_39a_bit_words::scaling::{the_checker_reads_each_operand_of_a_nest_once, the_checker_reads_each_binding_of_a_chain_once, a_constant_no_bit_word_reads_is_not_folded}`; depth: `l01_39a_bit_words::deep` |
| C27 | `cost.rs` `per_device`, the device footprint (step M0.6a) | a region's `retained` bytes stay counted until the end of its device's schedule, so the footprint is an upper bound on the live set, never above the sum of footprints step M0.6 used; a plan whose retained results die early can be judged infeasible when its true peak fits (a 9 GiB job keeping 8 GiB for the next job only, then a 10 GiB job: 18 GiB reported on 16 GiB, true peak 14 GiB) | `Region` does not carry each region's last reader, which freeing at the last reader needs; with it, one more pass subtracting at each last reader gives the exact peak in the same O(r). The pass is O(r + devices): plans of 20,000 and 160,000 regions take 1.39M and 11.05M instructions (7.9 times); recomputing the earlier retained bytes for each region, the negative control, takes 63.7 times at 1,000 and 8,000 | a region that retains bytes read only by an early successor, followed by large regions; `m0_06_cost_scalarization::peak::retained_bytes_count_until_the_schedule_ends`, scaling `the_peak_takes_time_linear_in_the_regions` |
| C28 | `check/mod.rs` `Ck::code_point_call` (step L1.39c; extends C26) | only a call of `codePoint` written in the text, as a bit word's operand or as the value of a `name =` binder, gets the interval `0 ..= 1114111` in `Ck::known`; a `codePoint` result that reaches a bit word through a function's result or a parameter is read as any int and rejected ("`bitAnd` takes `a` in int[0..], and this `a` is int") | as for C26: the checker runs before the store's iteration, and following each call into the body it returns would check that body once per call site, where reading the text is O(1) per node. A complete solution needs int-range types on results (step L10, ledger BIT-checker-ranges), so `codePoint`'s signature states `int[0..=1114111]` and a function's result type carries it, or the checker reading the reduced product's cell | a `codePoint` result returned by a user function: `f(s) = codePoint(s); x = bitAnd(f(s: "a"), 1);`. `l01_39c_code_points::checks::a_code_point_returned_by_a_function_is_read_as_any_int` |
| C29 | `zed-kernel` `text.rs` `Text::join`, `Text::chunks`, `Text::flat`, `LEAF` (WRITE-prefix-scale) | a string is a persistent AVL tree of shared blocks, balanced by height at each join, and nothing read is cached in a node. A join builds O(log n) new nodes, not one, so a `foldl` of n joins is O(n log n), not O(n); a reader of the whole text (hash, printing, `lower`, `upper`, `trim`, the casts, `flat`) walks the blocks again at each read, O(len + blocks), where the old code read a block written out once. Holding all n prefixes of a chain is O(n log n) bytes (was O(n²)), and `<` or `==` of equal byte lengths stops at the first byte that differs, O(log n + bytes read) (was each prefix's whole length). Measured, release, 200,000 joins (probe, not committed; old code in brackets): a `foldl` of 8-byte pieces 270 ms [16 ms], of 128-byte pieces 277 ms [7.5 ms]; on the 25.6 MB result one hash 12.7 ms [9.9 ms], one print to a `String` 33 ms [3.1 ms], one `flat` 21.6 ms [0, cached]; on the 1.6 MB result one hash 4.8 ms [0.4 ms]. The merge check measured the join again (release, load 62 to 69): a `foldl` of 200,000 one-byte joins 166 to 222 ms. DESIGN.md (line 376, `++` O(1); L6.16, "builds a node in O(1)") states O(1) per join, so this row departs from it by a factor of log n per join; plan row TEXT-append-O1 is the structure with amortized O(1) appends. One more effect of the blocks: a join of at most `LEAF` bytes is one block, so staging keys a call at it by its answers (`zedc::stage::compared`), and a call at a longer join by the function's term; the program's value is the same either way | Why: a block shared by a prefix and its extension needs writes past the end of a block the prefix's readers hold (`unsafe`, Miri); a balanced tree of shared blocks gets the same bounds in safe Rust, at a factor of log n per join and a walk per whole read. `LEAF` (128 bytes) is a representation constant: a join of a short block onto a short block copies both into one, so text built a character at a time is held in blocks, not one node per character; it never changes what a reader sees. Measured with merging off (release, load 62 to 69, 200,000 one-byte joins): 200,000 leaves of height 18 against 1,563 of height 11, `==` 34 to 40 ms against 0.2 ms, a hash 22 to 24 ms against 0.3 to 3.6 ms, printing 18 to 23 ms against 0.3 to 1.3 ms; 8-byte pieces `==` 11 to 39 ms against 2 to 5 ms; so the constant stays (comment at `LEAF`) | `zed_kernel` `text::tests`: `every_prefix_held_and_compared_costs_order_n_log_n` (n prefixes held, each compared with a text differing in byte 0; n = 2,000 against 16,000, limit 24): 10.1 times the instructions, 10.3 times the bytes; `a_chain_either_way_is_built_in_order_n_log_n` (left and right chains, n = 5,000 against 40,000): 9.8 times the instructions, 10.0 times the bytes; `a_chain_a_million_long_either_way_is_balanced_read_and_dropped` (height at most 29); `joins_in_any_order_keep_the_tree_balanced_and_the_text_whole`; `equal_texts_hash_alike_however_joined`; `reading_every_prefix_costs_the_text_read` (power 2, the output is n² bytes) stays. Negative control, the old code (not committed), n = 200 against 1,600: held and compared 63.4 times the bytes, compared alone 33.4 times the instructions; both fail, and at n = 2,000 the run passed 60 s |
| OMEGA-cycle-growth | `store/presburger.rs` `Omega::cycles`, the equalities that cycles of rows imply, found once, before the first step of `Omega::run` that is not an equality | the rows `x - y + k >= 0` with `k <= 0`, and `x + k >= 0` and `-x + k >= 0` through a node for zero, are edges of weight `-k`; the rows of each strongly connected component become equalities, or the system is empty when one has an edge of weight above zero (Tarjan, `utvpi::components`, O(m log m + n)). Not found: a cycle whose weights have both signs and sum to zero, and a cycle that only forms after a definition or an elimination; such a system goes on to the stages after as before and may be refused `Refusal::Growth` | a zero-sum cycle with mixed signs needs a potential of the graph, a shortest-path search with negative weights (C24); searching again after every step costs O(m) each, O(m·n) over a run, and keeping the components current as rows are added is incremental strongly connected components | Before the fix the 4-cycle `x0 >= x1 >= x2 >= x3 >= x0` in `[-1, 1]^4` with `1 <= x0 + x1 + x2 + x3 <= 3` entered the loop with 14 rows and took no step (every elimination had fill 10, four live variables), and the box gave `Growth`; now `Ok(false)` under every naming (`l01_38_presburger::names::the_orbit_of_a_row_under_a_rotation_or_a_reflection_is_closed`, `l01_38_presburger::omega::a_cycle_of_rows_makes_its_variables_equal`). Scaling: `l01_38_presburger::scaling::a_cycle_of_rows_scales`, a cycle of 512 and 4,096 variables, 9.4 times the instructions and 8.0 times the bytes (2026-11-05, load 57 to 59); control, not committed: the components found again for each equality, 38.2 times the bytes, fails |

## Kept

| Where | Why |
|---|---|
| Fourier–Motzkin pairs | an elimination runs only while it adds no terms to the rows (DESIGN L1.38), so the pairs of one elimination are at most the rows it removes times the rows it adds back, and the rows never grow; a system needing a growing elimination is refused (Growth) |
| undeclared parameters (check), `apply` | each body checked once; a class is merged or loses a carrier at most (values + 6 × classes) times, each step O(log N) by union by rank, so O(N log N); an application over unions keeps one state per set of arrows still alive, at most 2^(A+1) for an operator of A arrows, a constant of the prelude, so it is linear in the parts of its arguments. The search this replaced (RUNS, CONTEXTS, COMBOS, F6, F18) was exponential and capped. A literal joins its class by one find, O(log N): a plain one keeps only its own carrier, and where a type does not hold that carrier the menu names at most three forms (LIT-plain, 2026-11-05); `fewest` reads each arrow of a call once against each argument, O(A × the size of the arguments) |
