| emptiness and subtyping of tuple and morphism parts (`types/bdd.rs`, `types/empty.rs`) | deciding emptiness of a Boolean combination of tuple or morphism types is coNP-hard, so `is_empty` is sound and not complete (`Type::verdict` names each refusal: Fork, a recursive component two tuple types differ in, the subset construction; Residues, the NP-hard covering problem for residues, one prime split at a time; TupleSplit; MorphismSplit, one split into morphism types per branch; NoWitness; each has a Complexity tradeoff comment at its site in `types/empty.rs` and a case in `l01_23_subtyping::refusals::each_refusal_names_its_cause`): it never calls an inhabited type empty, but a part that only a split of the box in two or more components at once proves empty counts as inhabited, and a subtyping check that needs it fails with an error naming the types (the Complexity tradeoff comment in `types/empty.rs`; `l01_23_subtyping::scaling::a_type_not_proved_empty_promises_no_key`, four pigeons in three holes). No count stops the check (docs/thresholds.md 9; memory one path and one split at a time). A diagram has no more nodes than its operands together, so none is exponential in what was written; its paths can be, and so can its display (a parity of `C` tuple types has `2^(C-1)` clauses) |
| `zedc::axioms::of` and the M0.11 harness check of the order axioms (AXIOMS-order) | `of` filters the constant table `AXIOMS` (137 entries since FLOAT-minmax), whose size the language fixes and no program changes, so a lookup is O(1) in the program. The harness checks `Implies` on the pairs and `Composes` on the triples of a fixed instance set (17 integers, 9 floats), as `Transitive` already did; it is a test of the table, not of a program, so it has no scaling test. |
| M0.11 harness check of the bit-word axioms, `check_word` and `check_shift` (BIT-axioms) | `check_word` builds the table of `bitAnd`, `bitOr` or `bitXor` on the `w`-digit values, 4^w evaluations, and checks a law with one lookup per tuple: 8^w triples for associativity, 4^w pairs, 2^w singles, so the work is linear in the tuples checked. `check_shift` evaluates two terms per pair (`x`, `k`). `m0_11_prelude_axioms_and_limits::scaling::the_harness_is_linear_in_the_tuples_it_checks`: associativity of `bitXor` at 8^6 and 8^7 triples, power 1. |
| `min` and `max` on floats: `eval::ieee_min_max` and the code generator's inline `fmin`/`fmax` (FLOAT-minmax) | one comparison and at most one sign test per call, O(1); native code runs them inline with no kernel call, so a program's work in them is linear in the calls. `l01_36_cranelift::minmax::a_fold_of_min_scales_linearly`: a fold of `min` over 1,000 and 8,000 floats compiled and run natively, 64,650,566 and 529,608,554 bytes (8.2x, limit 24 for linear work), load 52 to 53. Negative control, run once: a fold made quadratic was refuted at 32.4x. |
| `zed_tests::scaling` `Meter::first_that_moves`, `one_test_at_a_time`, `zed_alloc_count::os` (PERF-portable) | `first_that_moves` reads a fixed list of at most two counters, each over 100,000 passes of a constant loop; `one_test_at_a_time` reads the test binary's arguments once, linear in their number; each `os` call is one system call. None depends on a program's size, so they have no scaling test; their unit tests are in `scaling.rs`. |
| `zed_alloc_count` `count`, the walk up the chain of threads at each allocation (WRITE-prefix-scale; SCALE-threads made the chain) | on Linux each allocation adds its bytes to the count of its thread and of every thread in the chain that started it, O(d) per allocation for a chain d threads long. Only test binaries use this allocator, and the compiler and runtime start no threads (every `spawn` in `crates/` is in a test), so d is set by a test's own code: the harness's thread for the test, the thread of each run (`scaling::alone`) and the threads a test starts, about three, and no program's size changes it. Keeping one count per thread and summing a thread's descendants at each read would make a read O(threads) and need the live threads listed; the walk is the cheaper side. Tests `zed_alloc_count::tests::a_thread_counts_in_the_count_of_the_thread_that_started_it`. |
| `zed_kernel::words` `utf8`, `from_utf8`, `codePoint`, `scalar` (step L1.39c) | nothing is given up: `utf8` of a string of n bytes is a family keyed `1 ..= n`, built through `Family::from_map` from keys in increasing order, O(n log n) by the persistent map where an array would be O(n), since L1 has no arrays (layer L3); `from_utf8` reads the values once in key order and validates once, O(n); `codePoint` and `scalar` are O(1) in the scalars they read. `l01_39c_code_points::scaling::{utf8_is_linear_in_the_bytes, from_utf8_is_linear_in_the_bytes}` at 20,480 and 163,840 bytes, power 1. |
